## Goal

Deliver the end states of `cruxvault/INSTRUCTIONS/peeling_the_onion/peeling the onion.md` under the binding decisions D1 to D16 in `long-horizon decisions.md` and the PI rulings of 2026-11-10 listed below, in the EIC-first order that the PI set. (1) The new EIC dataset exists: every EIC experiment, accession-checked (D8), reprocessed from FASTQ with the standard ENCODE-DCC pipeline of its assay, with native views, designed counterfactual arms from FASTQ and from alignments, pseudoreplicate halves, and per-replicate and per-library views where they exist. It is the dataset for the whole miniCANDI ladder. (2) The onion eval set and benchmark exist on it: the 7 headline metrics, the replicate-ladder layers (D12) on the rungs that EIC replicates populate, Max's development evals, the training-free baselines, the copy-leak guardrail, and the exact EIC scoring route (D9, D9b, D16). (3) miniCANDI meets the done rule D14: rungs 1 to 6 and their per-rung ablations ran with at least 3 seeds, the selection rule D13 settled each comparison on the EIC validation tracks, and the final design beats the strongest training-free baseline on all three goals, on validation and then once on the blind tracks. The extended 4-axis dataset keeps only its catalog and census chunks in this plan; its build sits behind gate G5, after the done rule. Scaling miniCANDI to CANDI is out of scope. The final report is one /prezit deck and one 12-page /storify report (headline results, findings, methods, discussion). All work lands on the umbrella branch `implementation/t152-peeling-the-onion` (draft PR #61) as crux child tasks of t152, with no branch per child. The PI merges.

## PI rulings

Dated 2026-11-10. Binding. They supersede the first plan's decisions list. Chunk V0 writes them into the notepad.

1. Rescope to EIC-first. The new EIC dataset is THE dataset for the miniCANDI ladder. The extended dataset keeps only its cheap catalog and census chunks now; its build is a gate after the done rule. Onion layers use the replicate rungs that the EIC experiments' own replicates and libraries give, with pair counts and interval widths.
2. Selection split is EIC's own split. Train on training tracks, score ablations on validation tracks, touch blind tracks once at the end. Headline metric 4 is scored on never-trained covariate pairs of the validation tracks.
3. Uniform base: measure first. The alignment-only FASTQ cost is measured on the EIC reprocessing and reported (CPU-h per experiment, wall time at the reached concurrency). It is not a blocker.
4. Scorable set. An assay enters headline macro averages and gate thresholds only when validation holds enough tracks and cell types and its replicate rungs give a usable interval. The other assays stay in training and go in a separate table.
5. EpiATLAS controlled access: no. Catalog it; use public data only.
6. Metric 4 floor: half against half. Predict the target's pseudoreplicate half from its other half; the model and the depth-only baseline are scored on the same half.
7. Seed spread: max minus min across seeds, the larger of the two arms compared.
8. Withheld never-trained covariate combinations: the agent decides and logs it. Pure depth-subset pairs do not count toward correction validity.
9. Cluster for FASTQ processing and pipeline images: no PI preference. The environment change (Caper venv, chip-seq-pipeline2, atac-seq-pipeline, dnase-seq-pipeline v3.0.0 as a new pull) is approved on the chosen cluster. The Nibi scratch quota discrepancy is checked by wave 0 and the cluster is picked on evidence.
10. Flashzoi and genomic-language-model venv: approved, as a separate venv. Reuse Max's setup if present.
11. Nibi `/scratch/mforooz/t112_cf`: the PI runs the Globus transfer Nibi to Fir; the foreman then deletes the scratch copy after verifying sizes.
12. Superseded by ruling 13. (It asked that the space of headline metric 1 be a parameter with both spaces computable.)
13. Headline metric 1 has two spaces, confirmed. Counts (log1p at the target's depth, pseudoreplicate noise floor) for the selection rule D13. The challenge's signal space (D9b for miniCANDI, published entries as they are) for the comparison with external EIC methods.
14. Architecture autonomy. The agent may try architecture ablations beyond the listed ones without asking (e.g. the control as a maskable 36th channel and an output-only smooth lambda head as two arms of the control ablation; the rung 5 axis arms; the rung 6 latent arms). Such arms stay outside D14 condition 1 unless listed, are settled by D13 like any arm, and are logged in the notepad with their evidence. The EIC model keeps a control output regardless (D9b).
15. Data movement autonomy. The agent moves data between Nibi and Fir as it sees fit. Constraints: cluster-to-cluster moves go through Globus, over an already-authenticated route only (never authenticate for the PI; if no such route exists, the chunk stops and says so); datasets never transit the laptop; sizes and checksums are verified at the destination; the foreman deletes Nibi `/scratch/mforooz/t112_cf` only after that verification (purge noted for 2026-11-20).

### Amendment 3 (2026-11-12 PI notes)

Chunk sections of this amendment are the `####` headings of this section. Amended once in place after the cold review `.orchestrate/review_A3.md` (2026-11-12): every blocking finding is fixed; the declined minors are listed at the end of section E.

**Rulings applied.** (27) Both RNA-seq arms are ablation only, reported in a side table; they never join a design (`joins_design = no`), so D8 holds. (h) The resolution arms get four score tables: 25 bp (arms 1, 5, 25), 50 bp (arms 1, 5, 25, 50), 125 bp (arms 1, 5, 25, 125) and 250 bp (all arms). **Inputs read, and what was verified in code.** `cruxvault/results/pi_notes_2026-11-12/{MAX_SPRINTS,ARCHITECTURE_SURVEY,RNA_CENSUS}.md`, `cruxvault/results/t209/ARM_SMOKE.md`. Verified: `RES = 25` at `tools/minicandi/contract.py:48`, `tools/onion_data/align_views.py:76`, `tools/minicandi/dna_embed.py:48`, `tools/onion_eval/strata.py:47`, `tools/onion_data/import_testbed.py:78`; `average_input` at `tools/minicandi/stage2_arms.py:56`; no `revcomp`, `multinomial`, cell-embedding or per-cell-vector key in `tools/minicandi/*.py` (grep); `loss_weights` weighs target kinds only, never assays (`tools/minicandi/losses.py:13`); `baselines.py` holds the marginal NB, the position average and depth-only scaling, and no retrieval baseline; `layers.py:295` thins every rung of an assay to one common depth.

**A fact that the resolution work depends on (verified, `tools/t112/bin25.py:58-79`).** The stored counts use an overlap rule: a read adds 1 to every bin from its start bin to its end bin. Such counts do not add across bin sizes (25 one-bp values of a read sum to its overlap length, not to 1). The total-variance identity across scales needs an additive measure. So the recount uses one position per read, call it the point rule: the read's 5' end, shifted by half the fragment length (`frag_len` of `measurements.tsv`) toward its 3' end for ChIP-seq, and unshifted for DNase-seq (its 5' end is the cut site) and ATAC-seq (the pipeline's tagAlign already carries the Tn5 shift, `TN5_AWK`, `align_views.py:96`). Any bin size is then an exact sum of the 1 bp array. The resolution group is self-contained under the point rule; its 25 bp arm is its own base and is not the ladder's base.

**A. The total-variance identity (PI notes 1 and 6). Pinned here so that TV1 and TV2 run in parallel.**
- Chain. Y is the count of one assay in one bin. Conditioning sets, each a superset of the one before: Z0 = (assay, position); Z1 = Z0 + cell type; Z2 = Z1 + donor; Z3 = Z2 + experiment (lab, antibody lot, protocol); Z4 = Z3 + biological replicate; Z5 = Z4 + library (technical replicate); Z6 = Z5 + sequencing run; then the read sampling given Z6 and the covariates C (depth, read length, run type, pipeline settings). Identity, by the law of total variance applied 7 times: `Var(Y) = Var(E[Y|Z0]) + sum_{k=1..6} E[Var(E[Y|Zk] | Z(k-1))] + E[Var(Y|Z6)]`. The sampling term is Poisson: given Z6 and C the reads are independent draws, so `E[Var(Y|Z6)] = E[Y]` on counts. Any excess over Poisson belongs to the library layer or to a layer outside it, never to the sampling term. The covariates C are held fixed in the chain; the variance that a change of C causes, `Var over C of E[Y|Z6, C]`, is a separate covariate layer, and headline 4 measures it.
- Pair estimator. Two measurements that share Z(k-1), are conditionally independent and identically distributed given it (exchangeable), and have equal covariates satisfy `0.5 E[(Y - Y')^2] = E[Var(Y | Z(k-1))]`, i.e. the sum of all terms from k outward. Call this the pair value of the rung. Rung to set: pseudoreplicate shares Z6, sequencing Z5, technical Z4, isogenic Z3, anisogenic and other_experiment Z1 (they are crossed, see below), other_cell_type Z0. A layer is the difference of two consecutive pair values.
- Assumptions that TV1 must state per metric and per layer, and TV2 must test: (1) conditional independence and identical distribution of the pair; (2) equal covariates, above all equal depth, on the scale used; (3) the order of conditioning: donor and experiment are crossed in ENCODE, so the nested order is a choice and `mixed_model.py` is the order-free check; (4) scale: the identity is exact for counts, and on log1p it holds only at one fixed depth, with a sampling term that depends on the rate; (5) the same cells and positions in both rungs of a difference; (6) the factor 0.5 and the mean term (variance of the difference against mean square difference).
- The model in the same identity. With S = the model's inputs and C', the identity is `Var(Y) = Var(mu_hat) + E[(E[Y|S,C'] - mu_hat)^2] + E[Var(Y|S,C')]` plus a cross term that is zero only for a calibrated mean. The predicted NB variance `mu + mu^2/n` estimates the last term; its part `mu` is the Poisson sampling term and its part `mu^2/n` is the sum of the layers that S does not determine. Bounds: the lower bound is the sampling term (the pseudoreplicate pair value); the upper bound is the pair value `E[Var(Y|Zk)]` at the deepest Zk that is a function of S, because then S carries at least the information of Zk. Calibration is therefore read as the ratio of the mean predicted variance to the pair value of each rung, one ratio per rung; headline 7 is that ratio for one rung.
- Metric to term (TV1 writes this table out; TV2 tests it). Headline 1 (`specific_variance_recovered_above_noise`, log1p at the target's depth): the share, explained by `mu_hat`, of the specific variance `sum_{k=1..6} E[Var(E[Y|Zk]|Z(k-1))]`, i.e. the truth's variance around the across-cell mean minus the sampling term (the Spearman-Brown floor N removes the sampling term only, so the donor to sequencing layers stay in the denominator). Headline 2 (`r_spec`): the correlation form of the same specific part, with no noise correction. Headline 3 (count CRPS skill against the position average, counts): a proper score of the whole predictive distribution; the position average predicts `E[Y|Z0]`, so the skill reads the variance beyond Z0 that the model explains, and the `oracle_scaled` and `scale_error` split isolates the depth part of the covariate layer. Headline 4 (correction gap closed): the share of the covariate layer beyond depth that the model removes; the floor is the sampling term and depth-only scaling is the baseline. Headline 5 (depth law): a first-moment check, `E[Y|Z6, depth]` proportional to depth; it estimates no variance term. Headline 6 (PIT deviation): the whole law of `Y | S, C'`; under a calibrated PIT the predicted variance equals the second plus third term of the model identity within each stratum. Headline 7: as above. D13 terms: imputation = headline 1; correction = headline 4; uncertainty = headline 6 against the marginal NB, whose variance is the total `Var(Y)` with no conditioning.

**B. Arms, and what was cut.** New arms run as a group "stage 3" on the design, in the same waves as stage 2 (S2a, S2b), 3 seeds, 80,000 windows (`tools/minicandi/configs/budgets.json::windows_seen_matched_params`).

| arm | change | joins a design | source of the idea |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rna_track` | 2 input-only channels: log1p of the mean plus-strand and minus-strand unique-read signal per 25 bp bin (ENCODE bigWig, GRCh38; an unstranded experiment gives half its signal to each channel); a learned null where the cell has none | no (ruling 27) | PI note 4a; Max: RNA-seq input +0.008 to +0.019 r_spec (second-hand) |
| `rna_vector` | the cell's log1p TPM over 2,000 genes, MLP, then FiLM (feature-wise scale and shift) on the covariate embedding; a learned null vector for the 11 cells without RNA-seq, trained by 10% dropout of the vector | no (ruling 27) | PI note 4a, corgi; Max: a TF-expression FiLM vector carried no information (second-hand), so the expectation is a null |
| `cell_embedding` | a learned vector per training cell through the same FiLM; id 0 = unknown, trained by 10% dropout | no: it cannot serve a cell type with no training track | PI note 4c, scooby |
| `loss_shape_x5` | per-bin NB loss plus 5 x a multinomial shape loss per (window, track) | yes | AlphaGenome, Borzoi, BPNet |
| `loss_assay_equal` | each assay's loss is divided by its own running mean, so every assay has equal weight | yes | Max: the equal-weight loss beat the likelihood head by +0.024 (second-hand) |
| `loss_assay_learned` | a learned log weight per assay with the regularizer `-log w` | yes | corgi |
| `loss_multiscale` | adds the NB loss of the predicted sums at 4 and 16 bins (100 bp, 400 bp), size pooled as in section C | yes | AlphaGenome (1 bp and 128 bp heads) |
| `aug_revcomp` | each training window is reversed with probability 0.5, DNA reverse-complemented | yes | common practice |

- The 5 further arms chosen from the survey are the last 5 rows. `average_input` (PI note 4b) needs no new chunk: S2a already trains it with 3 seeds on the design, and E7a builds its table. Addendum to the foreman's brief of chunk S2b: state the `average_input` verdict beside the D10 expectation; and beside the archived blended-CANDI number of `AGENTS.md` section 7 where comparable; no blend is rebuilt (foreman decision g).
- Cut, with the reason. FiLM in every block: covered (rungs 2, 3, 4 use `covariate_injection = film` as the default, e.g. `rungs/conv_ae.py:68`; `inject_film` exists on rungs 5 and 6; `inject_adaln_zero` in stage 2). U-Net skips: rung 4 with `skip_counts`, `skip_dna`. Control as input: stage 2 control arms. Squash transform: `tf_soft_clip` and 7 other transforms. Multi-resolution output heads: replaced by `loss_multiscale` here and by the resolution arms of section C. ChromBPNet-style bias model: two training stages per assay, and the ChIP analog is the control arms and the output-only lambda head (chunk A4); no accessibility analog is planned. Ensemble distillation: Max measured the ensemble gain; out of scope. scooby's hypernetwork decoder: `cell_embedding` is the cheap form. Small-shift augmentation: training windows already start at random bins. RNA-seq recount from FASTQ: a new pipeline; the ENCODE bigWigs serve an input-only channel.
- RNA-seq cells (`RNA_CENSUS.md`): 40 of 51 cells have an experiment; 32 are the same biosample, donor or line, and 8 are another donor. All 40 are used, with a flag `rna_match` (same|other_donor), and the report also gives the 32. 11 cells have none (7 of the 12 cells with blind tracks).

**C. Resolution (PI note 5, rulings 28 and 29).**
- Store. Not genome-wide: a 1 bp genome is 3.1e9 positions, 3.1 GB per view as uint8, and 365 base views (267 training + 45 validation experiments + 53 controls, lines "Early start" and interface B above) would be 1,131 GB. The region store holds the 256 eval regions and 512 seeded training regions (disjoint from the eval regions, mask fraction at least 0.9), each 409,600 bp: 768 x 409,600 = 314,572,800 positions = 0.31 GB per view, 365 views = 115 GB raw. Halves of the 45 validation experiments, eval regions only: 256 x 409,600 = 0.105 GB x 2 x 45 = 9.4 GB. Per-replicate views of the validation experiments, eval regions only: about 45 x 2 x 0.105 = 9.4 GB. Total about 134 GB raw on Nibi `/scratch`, less after gzip (not measured). uint8, or uint16 for a view with a value above 255.
- Analysis, "how much variance is lost because 25 bp is too coarse". Layers that the store populates: the pseudoreplicate rung, the isogenic rung (RCa also builds the `br<i>` views of the validation experiments on the eval regions, 9.4 GB more), and the cell-type layer. The technical and sequencing rungs are not in the store and are printed as not populated. On the 1 bp arrays, two nested block chains, 1, 5, 25, 125 and 1, 5, 25, 50. For a block size b with block index Z: `Var(Y_1bp) = E[Var(Y|Z)] + Var(E[Y|Z])`; the first term is the variance below b. Reported per assay and per onion layer that the subset populates: (1) the raw share below 25 bp; (2) the replicate-reproducible share at each step of the chain, from the cross-covariance of the two pseudoreplicate halves (the sampling term cancels in a cross-covariance); (3) both on counts (exact) and on log1p of the block sums (stated as not additive).
- Model arms. Matched tokens (1,024 tokens: contexts 1.0, 5.1, 25.6, 51.2 and 128 kb) is the main design: which is the trade a fixed-budget model faces. The token count is equal; the FLOPs are not, because the DNA tower convolves `L * res_bp` bases (`components.py:212-226`), 128 kb at 125 bp and 1 kb at 1 bp. Chunk RM records the measured FLOPs per window of each arm in `run.json` (`train.py`'s counter), and RMb prints them. One matched-context arm, `res5_ctx` (5,120 tokens = 25.6 kb, 5 x the FLOPs), separates resolution from context. A matched-context 1 bp arm (25,600 tokens) is cut: 25 x 6.5 = 162.5 CPU-h per run. Trunks: rung 2 and rung 4 at their base configs. Two decoupled arms answer "input and output" of PI note 5 apart, both with 1,024 tokens of 25 bp (equal context and tokens): `in5_out25` (each token reads its five 5 bp sub-bin counts per track; the output is at 25 bp) and `in25_out5` (25 bp input; the head emits five 5 bp sub-bins per token and the loss is at 5 bp). Both read the region store. 8 arms x 2 rungs x 3 seeds = 48 runs. Frozen DNA embeddings do not apply (`NotApplicable`). Repetition confound, stated by RMb: 80,000 windows on 512 regions of 409.6 kb (210 Mb) are about 49 passes at 125 bp (128 kb windows) and about 0.4 passes at 1 bp.
- Fair comparison. An arm is scored at every table size that its output bin divides, by exact sums of predicted means, never by interpolation. A sum of k predicted NB bins gets the moment-matched size under conditional independence, `n_pool = (sum mu_i)^2 / sum(mu_i^2 / n_i)`; the comonotone bound `(sum mu_i)^2 / (sum mu_i / sqrt(n_i))^2` is reported beside it, and the PIT deviation of the pooled prediction says which holds. 50 bp does not divide 125 bp, and a 125 bp arm cannot be scored at 25 bp. So there are four tables (foreman decision h): 25 bp (arms 1, 5, 25), 50 bp (arms 1, 5, 25, 50), 125 bp (arms 1, 5, 25, 125), 250 bp (all); `in5_out25` enters by its 25 bp output and `in25_out5` by its 5 bp output. Each run predicts once: the cache `<run dir>/res_pred/<target>.npz` holds, at the coarsest of (the arm's output bin, 25 bp), the additive statistics `mu_sum`, `v_ind = sum(mu_i^2/n_i)`, `s_com = sum(mu_i/sqrt(n_i))`; every table is a block sum of the cache, and no table predicts again. Region span per table (409,600 bp is no multiple of 125 or 250): 25 bp uses 16,384 bins (Haar depth J = 14, as today); 50 bp uses 8,192 bins (J = 13); 125 bp and 250 bp use the first 256,000 bp of each region, 2,048 bins (J = 11) and 1,024 bins (J = 10), and drop the trailing 153,600 bp. The variance analysis uses the first 409,500 bp for the chain that ends at 125 bp and the whole region for the chain that ends at 50 bp. The tables carry no designed pairs, so the correction term of D13 is undefined: the report gives the imputation and uncertainty terms with the seed spread of ruling 7 and uses the 2 x spread bar in words only. Nothing is settled into a design (ruling 28).
- Hard-coded 25 found (grep, 2026-11-12). Model side, generalized by chunk RM through a config key `res_bp` (default 25): `contract.py:10,48`; `components.py:62` (local windows 40 and 400 bins), `:187-192` (DNA tower `AvgPool(25)`); `dataset.py:86,459,484` (`dna_shift_bp // 25`, `start*25`). Counting side: `t112/bin25.py:49,89` (already a parameter `res`), `align_views.py:76,853`, `import_testbed.py:78`; untouched, the new tool `recount.py` stands beside them. Eval side, untouched because scoring pools to a common size: `evalset.py:194,231`, `strata.py:47,49`, `signal_from_counts.py:105-106,232-235`, `resolution.py:77`, `external_signal.py:87`, `predict.py:20-21,48`, `dna_embed.py:48,61`, and `eic_score.py:89` (the challenge grid).

No planned chunk is cut in full. **E. Schedule and budget.**
- Now, before `eic_v1` (track C, parallel to everything running): wave C1 = TV1, TV2, MS, BRt, XRd, XM, XD, RC0, RCt, RVt (10 chunks, disjoint files); C2 = LS, XI, XRf; C3 = S3K, RM, and TVf if TV2 names a real defect; C4 = AS3.
- Read-set purge rule (copy or stop). RC0 reports the purge date of the oldest read set that RCa needs. If that date is earlier than 2026-12-15, the foreman starts, at once and before wave 7, one job that tars the base tagAligns, the per-replicate tagAligns and the two halves of the validation experiments to Nibi `/nearline/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion/readsets/` (one tar per 50 experiments, sizes and file counts verified, the nearline file limit of 1,025 respected). If nearline refuses or the tar fails, RCa does not start and the foreman asks the PI. No file is touched to reset its age.
- After the freeze: RCa and BRa (wave 7, after E7a), RVa (wave 8), YS, PW and RMa (wave 12), RMb (wave 13), XIm (wave 20b, conditional), S3a (wave 21), S3b (wave 22).
- Critical path of Amendment 2b (about 18 to 19 days after the freeze): unchanged in length. Stage 3 runs beside stage 2 (24 runs beside 93); on an axial trunk the shared queue may add up to 1 day (estimate). FDa then also reads stage 3: addendum to the foreman's brief of chunk FDa: pass `results/<T:S3>/winners_s3.tsv` beside `winners.tsv`; the assembly rule is unchanged; new key groups `loss shape`, `assay weighting`, `multi-scale loss`, `augmentation`. On a mix or evolved design, chunk XIm (wave 20b, conditional) adds at most half a day before S3a; to make it rare, addendum to the foreman's brief of chunk MXh: a hybrid module calls `extra_inputs.extra_kwargs(cfg)` and the two hooks as the six rungs do after XI. XI, LS and RM merge only with their default-digest tests green (below), so a merge during the ladder changes no ladder run; each run's `ckpt.pt` already records its `git_sha`. A real defect in a D13 term (TVf) must merge before L1a (wave 8); a layers defect before E7a (wave 6) or the layers are rerun. Addendum to the foreman's briefs of RD and RSy: Methods takes `onion total variance.md`; the RNA-seq side table, the resolution tables and `MAX_NUMBERS.md` enter as given by S3b, RMb and MS.
- Added compute, small trunk, all on Nibi CPU unless noted. Unit costs from the table "Unit costs per run" below: training 6.5 CPU-h (rung 2), 6.7 (rung 4), scoring 6.6 at the planning factor and 0.83 at the 64-region pilot cost. TV2 5; RNA fetch and binning 20 (estimate); BRa 7; YS 2 x (6.5 + 6.6) = 26; PW 2; AS3 30 (Fir; 16 short runs x 1.2 + resolution smokes); stage 3: 24 x (6.5 + 6.6) = 314 + 9 second scorings x 6.6 = 59, sum 374; recount 130 (estimate, RC0 measures it) + 20 for the per-replicate views; variance analysis 50 (estimate); resolution arms: training 3 x [(5 x 6.5 + 32.5) + (5 x 6.7 + 33.5)] = 396, plus the decoupled arms 3 x 2 x (6.5 + 6.7) = 79, sum 475; one cached prediction per run on 64 regions, scaled by tokens per region, 6 x (20.8 + 4.2 + 4.2 + 0.83 + 0.42 + 0.17) = 184, plus 12 decoupled runs x 0.83 = 10; the further tables read the cache and cost at most the pilot metric cost each, 48 runs x 3 tables x 0.83 = 120 (upper estimate); scoring sum 314; resolution sum 789. Total: 5 + 20 + 7 + 26 + 2 + 30 + 374 + 150 + 50 + 789 = about 1,450 CPU-h and 0 GPU-slice-h, i.e. 5% of the 29,200 CPU-h of Amendment 2b. With an axial design, stage 3 is 24 x (31.7 + 32) + 9 x 32 = 1,817, so the total is about 2,900 CPU-h. With a rung 1 design, stage 3 is 24 x 1.04 + 33 x 5.3 = 200 GPU-slice-h in place of its 374 CPU-h. Storage added: 134 GB (region store) + at most 40 GB (RNA-seq files; XRf reads the sizes first) + the 25 bp pooled files and prediction caches (sparse, under 10 GB, estimate) on Nibi `/scratch`.
- Review minors declined. (1) An arm that cites Enformer: its two ideas are already present (attention over pooled positions in rungs 3 and 5; a Poisson loss in `loss_poisson_mean`). (2) Technical and sequencing views in the region store: the per-library read sets belong to priority 4 (`eic_v2`), after wave 7; the two rungs are printed as not populated. (3) Finding on line (g), that the D10 expectation stays untested: the foreman decided (g); the archived number is quoted where comparable.

#### TV1 The total-variance note   wave C1   task: new
Goal: Write the formal note that states the identity of section A, draws the graphical model, and maps every headline metric and every onion layer to a term and its assumptions.
Owns: `cruxvault/INSTRUCTIONS/peeling_the_onion/onion total variance.md`.
Interfaces: sections with these exact headings: `## identity`, `## graphical-model` (a Mermaid graph with the nodes position, assay, cell type, donor, experiment and lab, biological replicate, library, sequencing run, read sampling, covariates, observed count), `## rung-to-term-map` (one row per rung of `layers.RUNGS`), `## metric-to-term-map` (one row per headline metric 1 to 7, the error budget keys, and the D13 composite terms: the term estimated, the scale, the assumptions (1) to (6) of section A that it needs; the rows start from the paragraph "Metric to term" of section A and may refine it, never drop a metric), `## model-variance` (the model identity of section A, its two bounds and the per-rung calibration ratio; the sampling term is Poisson), `## scales` (the block identity of section C), `## assumptions`. Sources: `onion eval protocol.md`, `onion variation and objectives.md`, the docstrings of `layers.py`, `error_budget.py`, `metrics.py`. The note asserts no defect; chunk TV2 does.
Depends on: none
Test: `for h in identity graphical-model rung-to-term-map metric-to-term-map model-variance scales assumptions; do grep -q "^## $h" "cruxvault/INSTRUCTIONS/peeling_the_onion/onion total variance.md" || echo MISSING $h; done` prints nothing, and `(cd cruxvault && $CRUX validate --check=tree,tasks)` exits 0.

#### TV2 The audit of the estimators against the identity   wave C1   task: new
Goal: Simulate the chain of section A with planted layer variances and report every place where `layers.py`, `resolution.py`, `mixed_model.py`, `error_budget.py`, `metrics.variance_ratio` or the eval protocol do not sum to the total or are biased.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/total_variance.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_total_variance.py`, `results/<T:TV2>/AUDIT.md`.
Interfaces: `simulate(layers: dict, depth: dict, n_cells, n_pos, seed) -> dict` (a gamma-Poisson draw per level; returns count tracks and a pair list in the `pairs.tsv` rung names); `telescoping_check(layers_tsv) -> rows` (the layer increments plus the innermost rung against the outermost rung value); `budget_check(scores_json) -> rows` (`V = explained + unexplained + noise`, `E = unexplained + noise + added`, shared + specific + level against the normalized error); `per_rung_variance_ratio(n, mu, pairs) -> dict` (the calibration ratio per rung). The existing tools are called, not edited. `AUDIT.md` holds one table: `place (file:line), claim tested, planted, estimated, bias, defect (yes|no), fix`. Cases that must be in it: unequal depth between the two sides and between rungs; pseudoreplicate halves at half depth; different cell sets in two rungs of one increment; the factor 0.5 and the mean term; log1p against counts; crossed donor and experiment; the Spearman-Brown floor with unequal halves; the clip in `error_budget` (`floor_flag`); the fixed depth slope of the mixed model; headline 7 against each rung. A row is a defect only when the bias exceeds the bootstrap interval in the simulation.
Depends on: none
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_eval_total_variance.py -q` prints 0 failed (the simulator's own layer sums equal the planted total within 2%; each check function flags a planted violation), and `grep -c '^|' cruxvault/results/<T:TV2>/AUDIT.md` is at least 12.

Owns: exactly the files that those rows name among `tools/onion_eval/{layers,resolution,mixed_model,error_budget,metrics,score}.py`, plus `tests/test_onion_eval_tv_fixes.py`. With no such row the chunk is not launched.
Interfaces: no key of `scores.json`, `layers.tsv`, `resolution.tsv` or `mixed_model.tsv` is renamed. A changed value is listed in the slip with before and after on the synthetic tables. If a D13 term changes, the slip says so in its first line and the foreman logs it before L1a.
Depends on: TV2
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_eval_tv_fixes.py tests/test_onion_eval_total_variance.py -q` prints 0 failed, and `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh TVf && bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh TVf -m pytest tests/ -q -k onion_eval` prints 0 failed.

#### BRt Retrieval baseline   wave C1   task: new
Goal: Add the training-free retrieval baseline: copy the target assay's track from the training cell whose available input tracks correlate best with the target cell's.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/baseline_retrieval.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_baseline_retrieval.py`.
Interfaces: `class Retrieval` with `fit(batches)` and `predict(batch) -> {"n", "mu"}` as the classes of `baselines.py`; the neighbor is chosen per target from the fit regions only (mean Pearson of log1p counts over the shared input assays; the target cell itself is excluded), its track is scaled to the target's depth, one NB size per assay is fitted as `baselines._fit_size`. CLI `baseline_retrieval.py run --tables <dir> --evalset <json> --out <dir>` writes `scores.json` through `score.py`'s external-prediction route (chunk SC), with the same keys. `score.py` is not edited.
Depends on: none
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_eval_baseline_retrieval.py -q` prints 0 failed (synthetic tables with two planted cell groups: the neighbor comes from the target's group; a target never retrieves itself).

#### XRd RNA-seq input tool   wave C1   task: new

#### RCt Recount tool at 1 bp with the point rule   wave C1   task: new
Goal: Write the tool that builds the region store of pinned interface E from tagAligns.

Point rule as in the fact paragraph above; the strand is column 6 of the tagAlign.

#### LS Loss arms   wave C2   task: new
Goal: Add the four loss arms of section B.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/losses.py`, `tests/test_minicandi_loss_arms.py`.
Interfaces: config keys `loss_shape_weight`, `loss_assay_weight`, `loss_multiscale` of pinned interface E, read in `total_loss`; with all three at their defaults the loss is bit-identical to today's (a test pins 5 steps). The shape term is the multinomial negative log-likelihood of `y` under `mu / sum(mu)` over the loss-mask bins of one (window, track), divided by the count. The equal weight divides each assay's mean loss by an exponential running mean (momentum 0.99, detached). The learned weight reads `out["assay_log_w"]` and adds `-sum(log w)`. The multiscale term pools `mu` and `n` by `pool_nb(mu, n, k)` (exported; the `n_pool` formula of section C) on windows where the loss mask covers whole blocks. If `MAX_NUMBERS.md` gives a different definition of the equal-weight loss, the chunk implements that one under the same key and says so in its slip.
Depends on: MS
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_minicandi_loss_arms.py tests/test_minicandi_losses.py -q` prints 0 failed.

#### XI Integration of the extra inputs in the dataset, the contract and the six rungs   wave C2   task: new
Goal: Make every rung build and train under `rna_track`, `cell_vector`, `cell_embedding`, `aug_revcomp` and `loss_assay_weight = learned`.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/contract.py`, `tools/minicandi/dataset.py`, `tools/minicandi/registry.py`, `tools/minicandi/rungs/{pointwise,conv_ae,conv_attn_ae,unet,axial,perceiver}.py`, `tests/test_minicandi_extra_integration.py`, `tests/fixtures/minicandi_default_digests.json`.
Interfaces: the keys of pinned interface E, emitted by `OnionDataset` only when their config key is set; default batches stay sha256-identical (the test of chunk A2 is rerun). `tools/golden.py` builds only `candi.model` and proves nothing about `tools/minicandi`. So the chunk first writes, on the tree before its edits, `tests/fixtures/minicandi_default_digests.json`: per rung 1 to 6 under its `base.json` on the `synth.py` tables, seed 0, the sha256 of the initial `state_dict`, of the forward output on one fixed batch, and the loss of the first 5 training steps. The integration test demands the same values after the edits (exact for the digests, 0 difference for the losses). A new module or parameter is constructed only when its config key is set, so the initialisation RNG stream of a default model does not change. Each rung adds `ExtraTrackStem` output to its per-position input features beside the DNA features, and applies `apply_cell_state` to the input and output covariate embeddings; a rung that cannot take a key raises `NotApplicable`, and the support matrix is printed by the test. `registry.build_model` adds the parameter `assay_log_w` (zeros) and returns it in the output under `loss_assay_weight = learned`. `aug_revcomp` applies in training mode only. The wiring check (`guardrail.py`, part 1) passes on every arm on the synthetic tables. No existing test is edited.
Depends on: XM, XD

#### RM Model code for another bin size   wave C3   task: new
Goal: Let a rung train and be scored at `res_bp` in 1, 5, 50, 125 from the region store, with the existing scorer unchanged.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/res_dataset.py`, `tools/minicandi/res_adapter.py`, `tools/minicandi/components.py`, `tools/onion_eval/res_score.py`, `tests/test_minicandi_resolution.py`; after XI has merged, at most 20 lines in `tools/minicandi/registry.py` and `tools/minicandi/contract.py` (the dispatch on `res_bp` and the doc line), and the input lift and the output head of `tools/minicandi/rungs/conv_ae.py` and `tools/minicandi/rungs/unet.py` (the keys `res_in_sub`, `res_out_sub`; other rungs raise `NotApplicable`).
Interfaces: `ResDataset(tables_dir, mode, config, seed)`: the standard batch with L tokens of `res_bp` (counts = `recount.pool` of the 1 bp store, `x_dna` of length `L * res_bp`), training windows from the train regions only. `components.py`: the DNA tower pool and the local windows read `res_bp` (default 25; default outputs bit-identical, a test pins it). `ResAdapter`: presents a model of `res_bp` < 25 to `score.py` at 25 bp (it reads the fine inputs of the same span, runs the model on consecutive token windows, sums `mu`, pools `n` by `losses.pool_nb`). Decoupled arms: `res_in_sub = 5` gives each 25 bp token its five 5 bp counts per track as input features; `res_out_sub = 5` makes the head emit five `(mu, n)` per token and track, with targets and loss at 5 bp. `res_score.py predict --run <dir> --tables <eic_res_v1> --evalset <json>` writes the cache of section C once per run (`<run dir>/res_pred/`); `res_score.py table --run <dir> --at 25|50|125|250 --out <json>` reads only the cache and the truth, pools both exactly with the span and Haar depth of section C (J = 14, 13, 11, 10), and writes `imputation_gap_closed`, `uncertainty_gap_closed`, `pit_deviation_pooled`, `count_crps` and its split from the `metrics.py` functions, with both size bounds; it refuses an arm whose output bin does not divide the size, and it fails if the cache is absent (it never predicts). At 25 bp its values equal `score.py`'s on the same run within 1e-6 (a test). Every run's `run.json` holds the measured FLOPs per window. Defaults (`res_bp = 25`, both sub keys 1) leave the default digests of chunk XI unchanged; the chunk reruns that test. `score.py` is not edited.
Depends on: XI, RCt, LS

### Amendment 4 (2026-11-12: evaluation-only second experiments; metric 7 readers)

Fourth planner call-back, umbrella at 5bfad89. It applies PI ruling 33 and finishes the two leftovers of rulings 30 to 32 (Log, entry "TVg accepted"). It changes no finished chunk, no PI ruling, no Log line and no existing chunk section. Where it touches an existing chunk it says "addendum to the foreman's brief of chunk X". Every chunk below is `task: new`. Chunk sections of this amendment are the `####` headings of this section. Amended once in place after the cold review `.orchestrate/review_A4.md` (2026-11-12): every blocking finding is fixed; the declined minors are listed at the end of the section "Compute, storage and the critical path". Names: `K`, `O`, `M`, `W`, `T1`, `T2`, `EV`, `R`, `PT`, `RC` as in "Names used in the amendment 2 sections". New names: `OXL=$O/eic/other_experiment_ledger.tsv` (the reviewed list), `BX=$O/eic/other_experiment_build_list.tsv`, `KX=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/KX`, `T1X=$O/tables/eic_v1x`, `EVX=$O/eval/eic_v1x/evalset.json`. "Second experiment" means the one ENCODE experiment chosen for an EIC track under ruling 33. "Add-on" means a file set that holds a frozen file set byte for byte, plus second-experiment content.

**What was verified in code and on disk (2026-11-12).**
- Build list. Columns: `tools/onion_data/build_list.py:79-81` (`row, priority, experiment, eic_track, role, assay, assay_class, unit, arm, fastq_files, fastq_gb, control_experiment, pipeline, keep_bam, expected_measurements`). `ROLES = (train, val, blind)` (`:71`). A ChIP row finds its control through the ledger column `encode_possible_controls` (`:169-192`, `:365-368`); each control is its own row. A base row requests signal products only for a validation or blind track (`:465-475`). `check` counts every experiment that is neither a ledger experiment nor a named control as outside (`:636`). So the second experiments cannot enter `build_list.tsv`.
- The control is read only by the signal pass. `slurm/onion_data/build_row.sh:14-16`: only a row whose products include signal waits for its control (exit 75). A row that requests `counts+pr1+pr2` needs no control.
- Row numbers are global. `build_row.sh:53` reads the active rows from the array task ids of every `onion_build`, `onion_signal` and `onion_stage` job, whatever the build list. A second build list must use row numbers that the EIC list (rows 1 to 1217) does not use.
- Role to table. `align_views.py:613` writes `in_eic = role` into `meta.json`; the stand-alone CLI accepts only `train, val, blind` (`:1690`).
- Evaluation-only marker of the testbed. `import_testbed.py:81` (`USE_BLIND`), `:144-151` (`rows_for_tables` refuses a blind row without `allow_blind`). `build_tables.py:96` and `:540-563` write `evaluation_only.tsv` (`measurement_id, use`). `check_dataset.py:199`, `:252-255` read it. `OnionDataset` does not read it: it filters on `in_eic` only (`tools/minicandi/dataset.py:171`, `:323-324`), and `group_tracks` keys tracks by (cell, assay) (`:418-425`). `tables_sha256` hashes six files and not `evaluation_only.tsv` (`:168-169`, `:211-217`).
- Ledger gate. `build_tables.py:10-11`, `:503`: a measurement whose experiment has no ledger role goes to `missing.tsv`. `check_dataset.py:243-251` counts it under `measurements outside ledger` and `accessions outside the published EIC list`. `eic_accessions.py check` (`:19-22`) compares the ledger with the published list only, so it stays at `mismatches: 0` as long as the ledger file is not touched.
- Pairs. `build_tables.py:334-424` writes `pseudoreplicate`, `isogenic` or `anisogenic`, `technical`, `sequencing`, `other_cell_type` and `designed`. The rung list names `other_experiment` (`:423`) and no line writes it.
- Readers. `layers.py:25`, `:81-83`, `:184`: `other_experiment` is the experiment layer over `isogenic`, and the doc states that the pair shares cell type and donor. `score.py:194`: `H7_UPPER_RUNG["imputation"] = "other_experiment"`; `:831-846` reads the pairs of the scored split from `pairs.tsv` of the eval set's tables; `:1039-1066`: the explained fraction on the experiment layer needs a prediction for both members of the pair. `score.py:505` refuses a validation score when the run's tables differ from the eval set's tables. `arm_report.py:101` holds metric 7 with the target `one`.
- Selection facts from `other_experiment_census.tsv` (121 tracks with a candidate; computed for this amendment). Split: 100 training, 20 validation, 1 blind. Class: 47 broad histone, 42 narrow histone, 30 DNase-seq, 2 ATAC-seq. Best candidate: other lab and same donor 19, other lab and other donor 29, same lab and same donor 21, same lab and other donor 52 (the donor column is empty for some EIC experiments, so OXs recomputes these). 72 tracks have exactly one candidate. No candidate is an EIC experiment. The 89 histone candidates span 26 distinct (cell, lab) groups.
- Pipeline code. `git diff --stat cfd0ab7 HEAD` on `align_views.py`, `fastq_uniform.py`, `fastq_dnase.py`, `build_list.py`, `build_row.sh`, `stage_fastq.sh` is empty. The kit KF recipe and the umbrella hold the same pipeline code.
- Cost (`cruxvault/results/t180/projected_cost.tsv`, `smoke_cost.tsv`, `KIT_INTEGRATION.md:104-132`). Priorities 1 and 2: 396 rows, 9,880 + 32,960 = 42,840 million reads, 1,565 + 4,282 = 5,847 CPU-h, 446 + 1,273 = 1,719 GB of FASTQ. Per row: 108 million reads, 14.8 CPU-h, 4.34 GB. Rates: ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq alignment 0.160 to 0.177 CPU-h per million reads, DNase-seq 0.04, views 0.027.
- Storage (Log, RC0 entry): Nibi `/scratch` 3,905 to 3,908 GiB used, soft quota 1,024 GiB, hard limit 20,480 GiB, no grace timer, writes succeed.

**Decisions of this amendment (code level; the foreman logs them in the notepad).**
1. Tables: add-on, after the freeze. D6f freezes `eic_v1` as written and waits for nothing. The second experiments enter `eic_v1x`, an add-on of `eic_v1`: `withheld.tsv`, `covariates.json` and `scorable.tsv` byte for byte (so an unseen lab, platform, antibody or donor has id 0, pinned interface B); `measurements.tsv`, `pairs.tsv`, `missing.tsv` and `evaluation_only.tsv` hold the `eic_v1` file as a byte prefix, followed by the second-experiment rows. Reasons: the build of the second experiments ends about 2 days after the freeze can start; every ladder comparison needs one `tables_sha256` and one `evalset_sha256`; metric 7 with two bounds and the explained fraction are reported only (rulings 31 and 32), so no settlement needs them. D6g builds `eic_v2` as written and waits for nothing. Chunk E8c builds `eic_v2x` (the same add-on on `eic_v2`) at wave 27 for the all-track layers. Add-on tables are evaluation tables only: no training job, config or average table may name `eic_v1x` or `eic_v2x` (pinned rule; OXd tests it and the constructor enforces it).
2. Marker: a fourth `in_eic` value, inside a hashed table. A second-experiment row of a training or a validation track has `in_eic = evalonly` in `measurements.tsv` (an addition to pinned interface B, valid in add-on tables only). `tables_sha256` hashes `measurements.tsv` (`dataset.py:168-169`), so the marker cannot be removed while a hash still matches. The row is also listed in `evaluation_only.tsv` with `use = "other_experiment: evaluation use only, never training"` (the testbed's convention); `check_dataset.py` requires the two to agree, and no guard reads that file. The second experiment of the blind track has `in_eic = blind` and enters `eic_v2x` only. The build list and `meta.json` carry the same value `evalonly`. Barriers, each with its line: (a) code before OXd refuses any tables that hold the value (`dataset.py:312`), so an add-on fails closed on an old kit; (b) the table builder `build` sends an experiment without a ledger role to `missing.tsv` (`build_tables.py:503-505`); (c) no role set contains the value: `ROLES` (`dataset.py:171`) and `train_roles` (`dataset.py:297`, which is `(train, val)` for the final model), so `allowed` in mode `train` (`:323-324`), `train_tracks` (`:327`) and `train_base` (`:330`) never hold such a row; (d) the per-position average sums `train_base` only and refuses any other source (`dataset.py:861-871`, `:1004-1006`); the position-average baseline, its control average and the retrieval baseline read `ds.train_base` (`score.py:667`, `:705`, `:719`; `baseline_retrieval.py:254-257`); (e) OXd adds: mode `train` is refused in the constructor on any tables that hold an `evalonly` row or whose `build_info.json` has `addon_of`; `_base_view` (`dataset.py:671-676`) never returns a second experiment for another row; (f) chunk FEa names `$T1` (its Interfaces line), and the pinned rule of decision 1 holds for every later training chunk. The pair `split` of an `other_experiment` pair is `val` when its EIC side is a training or a validation track and `blind` when it is blind.
3. Ledger exception: `accession_ledger.tsv` is not touched. `$OXL` is a separate ledger. `build_tables.py` and `check_dataset.py` accept an experiment of `$OXL` only as a pooled uniform base view that is listed in `evaluation_only.tsv`, sits in no pair with `split = train`, and sits only in `other_experiment` pairs. Everything else of such an experiment is a violation.
4. Control: a second ChIP-seq experiment is built without a control and without signal products (`control_experiment` empty, products `counts+pr1+pr2`). Reasons: metric 7 and the layers read counts only; the count path reads no control; the EIC cell's control comes from another lab or batch for at least 43 of the 89 histone candidates, and a mismatched control would add a batch artefact to the layer under measurement; the candidates' own controls would add up to 26 deep control experiments that no metric reads. OXs records each candidate's own control and its state, so a signal-space add-on stays possible. No experiment is dropped for a missing control.
5. One rung, labelled pairs. `pairs.tsv` keeps the pinned rung value `other_experiment` (the scorer's upper bound and the sibling algebra read it). `knobs_differing` holds the pair class: `experiment` (same lab, same donor), `experiment;lab`, `experiment;donor`, `experiment;lab;donor`; an unknown donor is written `donor?`. `layers.py` keeps the pooled `other_experiment` row and adds one labelled row per pair class. The experiment (lab) layer of ruling 30 is read from `experiment;lab`. The class `experiment` is a repeat-experiment layer: batch without a lab change, close to a biological replicate. It is kept and labelled, per the ruling "all 121", and it is never quoted as a lab layer. A class with a donor change is labelled `experiment+donor`. After the class, `knobs_differing` lists every technical knob that differs between the two experiments, from `run_type, read_length, platform` (for example `experiment;lab;read_length;platform`); the pair class is the part made of `experiment`, `lab`, `donor`. Stated limits, which every report chunk repeats: (i) run type, read length and platform are confounded with lab, because the base view keeps the native read length (`fastq_uniform.py:30-33`), so the lab layer holds these technical differences; (ii) `layers.py:538-542` thins every pair of an assay to the shallowest side of any pair, so a second experiment can lower the common depth, and the rows of the `eic_v1x` layer table are not comparable with the rows of the frozen `eic_v1` table of E7a; (iii) only 8 of the 20 validation tracks with a candidate have one from another lab, and ATAC-seq has none, so for 12 or more validation targets the upper bound of metric 7 is a same-lab repeat; the bound is reported per pair class and the other-lab class is the one that matches ruling 31.
6. Add-on eval set. `$EVX` holds the regions of `$EV`, the validation targets of `$EV` that have an `other_experiment` pair (at most 20), and the second experiment of each as an extra imputation target with the same inputs and its own covariates, flagged `evaluation_only: true`. `replicate_pairs` of `$EVX` holds only the `other_experiment` pair ids of those chosen targets (at most 20), because the scorer turns every listed pair of the scored split into a pair job (`score.py:1351-1362`). The layer table reads all pairs from `pairs.tsv` through a second file, `$O/eval/eic_v1x/evalset_layers.json`, which no scorer reads. Flagged targets enter only `explained_fraction_per_layer` and `per_target`. An add-on score is written to `<seed dir>/ox/scores.json`; the frozen-evalset `scores.json` is never rewritten. E7a and E7b stay as written; chunk OXy is their add-on twin.
7. Order on Nibi. The second experiments are one batch of the priority-4 batch sequence of chunk D6c, submitted at the first batch boundary after D6b (see OXa). Earlier is not safe: D6b starts only when `squeue -n onion_build,onion_stage,onion_signal` is empty, and its resubmitted rows feed the freeze.

**Compute, storage and the critical path.**
- Build. ChIP-seq and ATAC-seq: 91 experiments x 108 million reads x (0.160 to 0.177 alignment + 0.027 views) = 1,838 to 2,005 CPU-h. DNase-seq: 30 x 108 x (0.04 + 0.016 views) = 181 CPU-h. Sum 2,019 to 2,186; planned 2,190 CPU-h. Cross-check: 121 x 14.8 = 1,791 CPU-h (the foreman's figure was 121 x 15 = 1,815). The read counts of the candidates are not known yet; OXs replaces 108 with the catalog reads. Resubmissions at 5%: 110 CPU-h. Verify, tables, check and the pair smoke: about 10 CPU-h.
- Evaluation. An add-on score runs 40 targets and at most 20 pair jobs, against 45 targets and every correction pair in a frozen score. It is planned at the full 6.6 CPU-h of a frozen score, as an upper figure; OXy measures it. Add-on eval set 1 CPU-h; layers on `eic_v1x` 50 CPU-h (the estimate of E7a); 5 baselines x 6.6 = 33; at most 6 rungs x 2 arms x 3 seeds + 3 seeds of the final design = 39 runs x 6.6 = 257 CPU-h (rung 1 on the GPU slice: 6 runs x 5.3 = 32 GPU-slice-h in place of 40 CPU-h). `eic_v2x` and its layers: inside the 175 CPU-h of E8c.
- Total added: 2,190 + 110 + 10 + 1 + 50 + 33 + 257 = about 2,650 CPU-h, i.e. 9.1% of the 29,200 CPU-h of Amendment 2b.
- Storage on Nibi `/scratch`. Transient: 121 x 4.34 GB = 525 GB of staged FASTQ, one batch, under the 1,800 GB batch cap of D6c; because the batch runs alone, the worst transient of the whole build stays 1,800 GB. Kept in `work/`, measured: the read-set halves of 175 built EIC experiments (`cruxvault/results/t240/readsets.tsv`, column `bytes`, every unit and arm summed) have median 0.85 GB, 95th percentile 2.98 GB and maximum 7.65 GB per experiment. Planned at the 95th percentile: 121 x 3.0 = 363 GB. `measurements/`: at most 1.2 GB x 121 = 145 GB (the per-experiment figure with all arms, `KIT_INTEGRATION.md:132`; one base view is smaller). Sum at most 508 GB and at most 121 x 47 = 5,700 files. Worst case, if the BAM drop fails on the cluster (it left 0.2 to 0.7 GB per row on the 4 rows of `t180/SUBMITTED.md`, a small sample): 91 x 21 + 30 x 15 = 2,361 GB of BAMs and read sets. OXa therefore stops after its smoke if a smoke row still holds a BAM. `/project`: the `eic_v1x` tables (the size of `eic_v1` plus 121 rows) and about 250 log files.
- Critical path: unchanged. No chunk of the path D6b, D6f, E7a, E7b, G2, L1a to FDb depends on a chunk of this amendment. Priority 4 moves about 1 day later (staging 121 rows at 22 to 25 rows per hour = 5.5 h; 121 x 4.3 row-hours / 39 rows at once = 13 h). Priority 4 feeds D6d, D6g and E8a, which the blind pass needs about 18 days after the freeze, against 5 to 6 days of priority-4 build. The code chunks merge under the digest rule below, so a merge during the ladder changes no ladder run.
- Merge rule for OXd, OXe, OXl, OXr, OXt. Each test file pins, on the synthetic fixtures, that the output without second-experiment rows is byte-identical to the output before the edit (a digest written on the base commit). OXd also keeps `tests/test_minicandi_extra_integration.py` green (the default digests of the six rungs). The foreman does not push kit `main` between the start and the acceptance of D6f or of E7a.
- Analysis files that read `in_eic` directly. `mixed_model.py:271` keeps rows with `in_eic` in `(train, val)`, `scale_variance.py:350` keeps `in_eic == split`, and `resolution.py:252` reads rows through the same split filter, so a row with `in_eic = evalonly` never enters them. The one second experiment with `in_eic = blind` exists in `eic_v2x` only, and only `layers.py` reads `eic_v2x` (addendum to E8c below). No chunk edits these three files.
- Gate GX (the foreman, after OXc and before OXx). Inputs: `COLLECT.md` and `PAIR_SMOKE_ALL.md` of OXc. The minimum is the one of the code: `MIN_PAIRS = 5` pairs from `MIN_CELLS = 3` cells (`layers.py:77`). (1) If the class `experiment;lab` has fewer than 5 built pairs from 3 cells in every assay class, the lab layer is not estimable: the foreman stops before OXx and asks the PI. (2) A class under the minimum in one assay class is reported pooled by assay class with the flag `few pairs` (today's behavior); ATAC-seq has 0 other-lab pairs and is reported so. (3) If more than half of the built validation pairs are Undefined under the 5% rule of the smoke, the foreman calls the planner before OXy; the bound is then not quoted.
- Review minors declined. (1) "Drop the retrieval add-on score": declined, the PI wants that baseline in every table; OXe owns the `--addon` path of `baseline_retrieval.py`. (2) "The `align_views.py` edit may be unnecessary": kept as a conditional edit; OXb makes it only if the `run --row` path refuses the value.

**Decisions needed (Amendment 4; the foreman carries these lines to `## Decisions needed`).**
- (l) A track whose every candidate fails the selection checks (perturbed sample, depth under the floor, no served FASTQ). Recommended: drop the track and list it in `dropped.tsv`; the count then falls under 121.
- (m) ChIP-seq second experiments are built without a control and without signal tracks (decision 4). Recommended: accept; metric 7 and the layers read counts only.
- (n) The second experiments run before batches 2 and 3 of priority 4 and move the blind and per-library builds about 1 day. Recommended: accept; the blind pass has about 12 days of slack.

#### Track X, wave X1 (start now; all file sets disjoint)

#### OXs Select the second experiments   wave X1   task: new
Goal: Resolve the 121 candidates of the census into one reviewed list with a first choice and a fallback per track.
Owns: `tools/onion_data/other_experiments.py`, `tests/test_onion_data_other_experiments.py`, `results/<T:OXs>/` (`other_experiments.tsv`, `dropped.tsv`, `SELECTION.md`); Nibi `$OXL` (a copy of `other_experiments.tsv`).
Interfaces: `other_experiments.py select --census cruxvault/results/pi_notes_2026-11-12/other_experiment_census.tsv --catalog $O/catalog/encode --ledger $O/eic/accession_ledger.tsv --build-list $O/eic/build_list.tsv --out <tsv> --dropped <tsv>`, run on Nibi (the catalog of D1 covers the 35-assay panel; compute nodes have internet). It reads the candidates of `best_candidate` and `other_accessions`; it makes no new census (the exact-term undercount of the Log stays stated in `SELECTION.md`). Checks per candidate, each written to the column `checks`: (1) status released, and no audit of level ERROR; (2) not perturbed: no treatment, no genetic modification, and the same biosample term as the EIC experiment; (3) not an EIC ledger experiment, not an EIC control, and no library accession shared with the EIC experiment (`libraries.tsv`); (4) at least one FASTQ served: one HTTP HEAD per file, as `build_list.probe` does; (5) run type, read length, platform and biological-replicate count read from `files.tsv`; (6) depth: the catalog reads are at or above the 5th percentile of the catalog reads of the EIC training base experiments of the same assay class (computed from `files.tsv` and `build_list.tsv`; the value is printed), so no second experiment is shallower than what EIC itself holds; depth is counted in fragments here and in `log2_depth_ratio` (a paired-end read pair is one fragment; `catalog_reads` keeps the mate-counting figure for the cost); (7) own control: accession, served or not, and whether the EIC build already holds it (recorded only; decision 4). Order among the candidates that pass: other lab before same lab (ruling 33); then same donor before other donor before unknown donor (a pair that shares the donor gives the clean layer of ruling 30); then the smallest absolute log2 depth ratio to the EIC experiment; then the accession. The second in that order is the fallback. An experiment serves at most one track, as first choice or as fallback: tracks are assigned in the order validation, blind, training, then `eic_track`, and a track takes its best candidate that no earlier track holds (C02M02 and C03M02, both adrenal gland DNase-seq, share their candidates). Columns of the output: `eic_track, split, cell, assay, assay_class, eic_experiment, eic_lab, eic_donor, experiment, lab, donor, biosample, pair_class, same_biosample, run_type, read_length, platform, tech_diff, n_bio_reps, catalog_reads, fragments, log2_depth_ratio, fastq_files, fastq_gb, own_control, own_control_state, checks, fallback, fallback_pair_class, fallback_fastq_files, fallback_fastq_gb`. `pair_class` takes the four values of decision 5 (with `donor?`); `tech_diff` lists which of `run_type, read_length, platform` differ from the EIC experiment. New subcommand `other_experiments.py pair-smoke --pairs <a dir>:<b dir>[,...] --evalset <json> --out <md>`: for each pair it reads both `counts.h5` on the eval regions, depth-matches as `metrics.variance_ratio` does, and prints `sum(a)/sum(b)` and whether it is outside 1 +- 0.05 (`metrics.py:543`, `:587-588`: such a pair gives an Undefined ratio). A track with no passing candidate goes to `dropped.tsv` with the failed check of each candidate. `SELECTION.md` gives the counts per split, assay class and pair class, the depth floor per class, the summed `fastq_gb`, and the build cost recomputed from the catalog reads and the rates above.
Depends on: none
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_data_other_experiments.py -q` prints 0 failed (hand-written catalog fixtures: a perturbed candidate, a shallow one, a shared library and an EIC accession are each refused; the order rule picks the other-lab same-donor candidate; two tracks that share all candidates get two different experiments and the validation track chooses first; a paired-end candidate is compared in fragments), and `awk -F'\t' 'NR>1' cruxvault/results/<T:OXs>/other_experiments.tsv cruxvault/results/<T:OXs>/dropped.tsv | cut -f1 | sort -u | wc -l` prints 121, and `awk -F'\t' 'NR>1{print $9}' cruxvault/results/<T:OXs>/other_experiments.tsv | sort | uniq -d | wc -l` prints 0.

#### OXd Dataset guard for evaluation-only second experiments   wave X1   task: new

(2) Constructor: mode `train` raises `RoleError` when the tables hold an `evalonly` row or when `build_info.json` has the key `addon_of`, with or without `train_on_validation` and `final_model`. `build_average_h5` builds mode `train`, so it refuses add-on tables too. (3) Modes `val` and `blind`: an `evalonly` row is readable by id (`check_row`), as a target or as a pair member; it is absent from `group_tracks`, `train_tracks`, `train_base` and every input list. (4) `_base_view` (`:671-676`) leaves `evalonly` rows out of its candidates; when the target row is itself `evalonly`, its base view is the row itself. (5) `evaluation_only.tsv` is not read. (6) Tables without such rows give bit-identical batches. Pinned rule: no file under `tools/minicandi/` or `slurm/minicandi/` names `eic_v1x` or `eic_v2x`.

#### OXe Eval set add-on and add-on scoring   wave X1   task: new
Goal: Build the add-on eval set from a frozen eval set, and score a run on it without touching its frozen score.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/evalset.py`, `tools/onion_eval/score.py`, `tools/onion_eval/baseline_retrieval.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_addon.py`.
Interfaces: (1) `evalset.py build` never lists a row with `in_eic = evalonly` as a target or as an input, under every option (`--inputs-roles train,val` and `--include-blind` included), and with `--include-blind` it never lists a blind row that `evaluation_only.tsv` marks `other_experiment`; `other_experiment` pairs enter `replicate_pairs` under the usual split rule (used for the layer file only). (2) `evalset.py addon --base <frozen evalset.json> --tables <add-on tables> --out <json>`: regions, fit regions and strata of the base, verbatim; `imputation_targets` = the base targets of regime `leave_one_assay_out` that have an `other_experiment` pair in the add-on tables, plus, for each, the second experiment as a target with the same `inputs`, the same regime and split, and the optional key `"evaluation_only": true`; no correction pairs; `replicate_pairs` = only the `other_experiment` pair ids whose EIC side is one of those chosen targets (at most 20; decision 6); `scorable` of the base; new keys `addon_of` (sha256 of the base file) and `tables_sha256` of the add-on tables. `evalset.py check` accepts it. (3) `score.py --addon`: required when the eval set has `addon_of`; it accepts a validation score when the run's `tables_sha256` equals `build_info.json::addon_of_tables_sha256` of the add-on tables (the check of `score.py:505` otherwise stays); it refuses an output directory that holds a `scores.json` with another `evalset_sha256`. (4) A flagged target is predicted and enters `explained_fraction_per_layer` and `per_target` only; no headline block, no `selection` term and no `n_targets` count reads it. (5) `slurm/onion_eval/score.sh` needs no change: the add-on form is `score.sh $K $EVX <seed dir>/ox run <seed dir> --addon`. (6) `baseline_retrieval.py run --tables <add-on tables> --evalset <add-on evalset> --out <dir> --addon` passes `--addon` to the scorer; its retrieval pool stays `ds.train_base`. (7) `per_target` of metric 7 states for each Undefined ratio its reason text, and the block gains `n_undefined_upper_bound`.

#### OXt Tables add-on and the ledger exception   wave X2   task: new
Goal: Write second-experiment rows and `other_experiment` pairs into an add-on tables directory, and make the dataset check prove that none can leak into training.
Owns: `tools/onion_data/build_tables.py`, `tools/onion_data/check_dataset.py`, `tests/test_onion_data_tables_addon.py`.
Interfaces: `build_tables.py addon --base <frozen tables> --other-build-list $BX --other-experiments $OXL --measurements $M --work $W --out <add-on tables>`. It copies every file of the base byte for byte, then appends. A row is appended only when its experiment is a selected or fallback experiment of `$OXL`, its directory verifies (as `build` verifies), its `meta.json` says `in_eic = evalonly`, and it is the pooled uniform base view. Written `in_eic`: `evalonly`, or `blind` for the blind track (decision 2); `eic_track`, `cell`, `assay` of the EIC track; `control_measurement_id` empty. `evaluation_only.tsv`: the base rows plus one row per appended measurement with the `use` text of decision 2. `pairs.tsv`: the base rows plus one row per appended measurement: `a` = the EIC track's pooled base view (uniform before native, never testbed; the rule of `other_cell_type`), `b` = the second experiment, `rung = other_experiment`, `knobs_differing` = the pair class of `$OXL` followed by its `tech_diff`, `subset_pair = 0`, `never_trained = 0`, `split` = `val` or `blind` (decision 2). A second experiment whose EIC partner is absent from the base (the blind track, under `eic_v1`) goes to `missing.tsv` of the add-on with that reason. No pseudoreplicate, designed or `other_cell_type` row is written for a second experiment. `build_info.json`: `addon_of`, `addon_of_tables_sha256` (`minicandi.dataset.tables_sha256` of the base), `other_experiments_sha256`. The subcommand `build` is unchanged and still sends every experiment outside the EIC ledger to `missing.tsv`. `check_dataset.py --tables <dir> [--addon-of <base>] [--other-experiments <tsv>]` (defaults from `build_info.json`): the old lines stay and stay 0, with `$OXL` experiments allowed only under decision 3; the rule `blind rows readable in train or val mode` keeps its meaning for testbed rows and blind-role rows; new lines, each 0: `second-experiment rows outside the reviewed list`, `second-experiment rows not flagged evaluation-only`, `second-experiment rows that are not a pooled uniform base view`, `second-experiment rows in a training pair or outside the other_experiment rung`, `evaluation-only rows readable in a training mode` (mode `train` must raise `RoleError` in the constructor under the three configs; the check then builds `OnionDataset` in mode `val`, which is allowed, and counts the second-experiment ids in `train_tracks`, in `train_base`, and as the `_base_view` of any other row), `evalonly rows that disagree with evaluation_only.tsv`, `rows of the base tables changed or missing`; and the count line `second-experiment rows: N (other lab n1, same lab n2)`.
Depends on: OXd (merged), OXs (the columns of `$OXL`)

The new file pins: `build` output on the synthetic fixture is byte-identical to a digest of the base commit; an add-on holds the base byte for byte; six planted leaks each make the check exit 1 (the `evaluation_only.tsv` row removed; `in_eic` set to `train`; a pair with `split = train`; an experiment outside `$OXL`; a second experiment in a `designed` pair; a second-experiment row that is a depth arm).

#### OXa Build the second experiments (submit)   wave 5   task: new
Goal: Submit the priority-5 rows of `$BX` as one batch of the build sequence, after a 3-row smoke.
Owns: `results/<T:OXa>/` (`SUBMITTED.md`, `PAIR_SMOKE.md`).
Interfaces: start only when all hold: D6b is accepted; no row of its `RESUBMITTED.md` is queued or running; `du -sh /scratch/mforooz/onion/fastq` is under 300 GB; no batch of D6c is staging. The foreman runs this batch at the first such boundary: before batch 1 of D6c if D6c has not been submitted, else between two of its batches; the next D6c batch then waits for the same rule. Never touch a queued or running job. Before submit: `quota -s` and `diskusage_report` (stop if a grace deadline shows less than 21 days), and `scontrol show config | grep MaxArraySize` (stop if it is not above the highest row number of `$BX`). Step 1, smoke: `bash $KX/slurm/onion_data/build_row.sh submit $KX $BX 5 50 2 <one ChIP-seq row, one DNase-seq row, one ATAC-seq row>`; wait until the three rows have their measurement directories; `align_views.py verify --measurements $M --experiment <x>` passes; the build log of the ChIP-seq row shows no exit 75 and no control read set; `meta.json` says `in_eic = evalonly`; no BAM is left in the three run directories (else stop: the worst-case storage of this amendment applies and the foreman decides); `du -sh` of the three `work/` directories is recorded. Pair smoke: the smoke rows are chosen from the other-lab class where it exists and so that their EIC partners are built; `$RC KX tools/onion_data/other_experiments.py pair-smoke --pairs <EIC base dir>:<second dir>,... --evalset $EV --out ...` writes `PAIR_SMOKE.md` (the ratio of regional totals per pair, and the count outside 1 +- 0.05). If `$EV` does not exist yet, the smoke uses 64 seeded regions of its own and says so. Step 2: `bash $KX/slurm/onion_data/build_row.sh submit $KX $BX 5 50 2 <rows of batch 1>`. Health check at +20 to +30 minutes as D6c. `SUBMITTED.md` holds the job ids, the array ranges, the storage readings and the collect commands (the D6b recipe with `$KX`, `$BX` and priority 5). Rows of priority 6 are not submitted.

#### OXc Collect the second experiments   wave 6   task: new

A row that fails again, or whose error class is `md5`, `download` or `not_applicable`, is replaced by its priority-6 fallback row, submitted once: `bash $KX/slurm/onion_data/build_row.sh submit $KX $BX 6 50 2 <rows>`. A track with no built experiment after that is listed with its cause; no third experiment is tried. Never resubmit a COMPLETED, queued or running row. `COLLECT.md` gives, per pair class and split, the built count beside the selected count, the measured CPU-h per million reads per pipeline, `du -sh` of the new `work/` and `measurements/` directories and their file counts, and the pair smoke on every built validation pair (`other_experiments.py pair-smoke`, written to `PAIR_SMOKE_ALL.md`), with the count of pairs that the 5% rule makes Undefined, per pair class. `COLLECT.md` and `PAIR_SMOKE_ALL.md` are the inputs of gate GX.

One tar per 50 measurements to `/nearline/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion/eic_v1x/` under the rule of D6f. After the check passes, `$T1X` is frozen.
Depends on: OXc, gate GX, OXt (merged and pushed as kit `main`), D6f
Compute: about 3 CPU-h. Wall: 1 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh --time 02:00:00 --cpus 8 --mem 32000 main tools/onion_data/check_dataset.py --tables /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/tables/eic_v1x --addon-of /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/tables/eic_v1` exits 0 and prints the D6f lines with 0, `rows of the base tables changed or missing: 0`, `evaluation-only rows readable in a training mode: 0`, `second-experiment rows in a training pair or outside the other_experiment rung: 0`, `second-experiment rows outside the reviewed list: 0` and `second-experiment rows: N` with N above 0; and `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_data/eic_accessions.py check --ledger /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/eic/accession_ledger.tsv` still prints `mismatches: 0`.

#### OXy Add-on eval set, the experiment layer and the baselines' two bounds   wave 8   task: new
Goal: Build the add-on eval set, measure the experiment layer per pair class, and score the baselines on the add-on set.
Owns: `results/<T:OXy>/` (`OTHER_EXPERIMENT.md`, `layers_by_pair_class.tsv`, `h7_bounds_baselines.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$O/eval/eic_v1x/`.
Interfaces: (1) `$RC main tools/onion_eval/evalset.py addon --base $EV --tables $T1X --out $EVX` and `evalset.py check --evalset $EVX`. `$EVX` is frozen after this step. Layer file: `evalset.py build --tables $T1X --strata $O/eval/strata/masks.h5 --n-regions 256 --n-fit-regions 16 --seed 0 --pair-splits train,val --out $O/eval/eic_v1x/evalset_layers.json` (all pairs; no scorer reads it). (2) `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_run.sh --name layers_v1x main 8 32000 12:00:00 tools/onion_eval/layers.py run --tables $T1X --evalset $O/eval/eic_v1x/evalset_layers.json --out $O/eval/eic_v1x/layers.tsv`. (3) Add-on scores of the baselines: `sbatch --time=12:00:00 --output=$O/logs/%x_%j.out $K/slurm/onion_eval/score.sh $K $EVX $O/eval/eic_v1x/baselines/<name>/seed0 baseline <name> --addon` for `marginal_nb`, `position_average`, `depth_only_scaling`, `pseudoreplicate_floor`; and the retrieval baseline through its own tool: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_run.sh --name retr_v1x main 2 8000 12:00:00 tools/onion_eval/baseline_retrieval.py run --tables $T1X --evalset $EVX --out $O/eval/eic_v1x/baselines/retrieval/seed0 --addon`. (4) Wait with `remote_check.sh --wait <job id>`. `OTHER_EXPERIMENT.md`: the experiment layer per assay class and pair class with pair counts, cells and intervals; the statement of which class is the lab layer (decision 5); per baseline the ratio at the pseudoreplicate rung and at the `other_experiment` rung, the pass rate, the count of targets with both bounds, and the count of targets whose upper bound is Undefined with the reason (`n_undefined_upper_bound`), each split by pair class; the three limits of decision 5, in the same words; the add-on scoring cost per run from `sacct` (it replaces the planned 6.6 CPU-h). The slip carries one finding text per result.

No settlement reads these files. If `$EVX` does not exist yet, the foreman submits these scores when OXy is accepted.

The blind pass BPa is unchanged: it scores on `eic_v2`, and its upper bound of metric 7 prints `not populated`.
