#### D6u Update the dataset from a watchdog report   wave U (repeats; one run per report)   task: new
Goal: Act on one watchdog report: resubmit a failed tail row once, and build the next version when rule B.2 says so, with its notes.
Owns: `results/<T:D6u>/` (`UPDATE_<k>.md`, `CHANGES_<k>.md`, `tail_closed.tsv`, `tail_resubmitted.tsv`, `versions.tsv`); Nibi `$O/tables/eic_v1_<k>/`, `$O/eval/eic_v1_<k>/`.
Interfaces: input `$TS` and `$TD`. (1) For each tail row with a failed state that `tail_resubmitted.tsv` does not list: class the error from its build log, and resubmit once: `ONION_TIME=7-00:00:00 [ONION_MEM=192000M] bash $KF4/slurm/onion_data/build_row.sh submit $KF4 $B <priority> 50 2 <row>`; append row, job ids, cause and time to `tail_resubmitted.tsv`. A row that the file already lists and that failed again goes to `tail_closed.tsv` with its reason and is never run again (stop rule, section B.5). A queued or running row is never touched. (2) For each tail row that is done: `align_views.py verify --measurements $M --experiment <acc>`; for a validation row, check `signal.h5`, `pr1_signal.h5`, `pr2_signal.h5`, and submit its signal task if they are absent (the build kit's own signal step, row list only). (3) If rule B.2 (i), (ii) or (iii) holds: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh --time 03:00:00 --cpus 8 --mem 32000 main tools/onion_data/build_tables.py build <the arguments of D6f> --superset-of <previous version> --out /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/tables/eic_v1_<k>`, then `check_dataset.py --tables <new> --superset-of <previous>`. If the superset check refuses (a line of the previous version would change), nothing is written: the chunk reports the differing lines and the foreman calls the planner. (4) `evalset.py build` with the arguments of E7a step 1 into `$O/eval/eic_v1_<k>/evalset.json`, `evalset.py check`, and the regions assertion against `$RG`. No baseline is scored here; the foreman submits E7a's steps (3) to (5) on a version only when it becomes the ladder version or the last version. (5) `CHANGES_<k>.md` as section B.2 pins it, also copied to `$O/tables/eic_v1_<k>/CHANGES.md`; `versions.tsv` gains one line: `version, built_utc, tables_sha256, evalset_sha256, superset_of, n_train, n_val, n_missing_train, n_missing_val, scorable_assays, role (none|ladder|last)`. `UPDATE_<k>.md` says what the foreman must do next (nothing; fix the ladder version; declare the last version). On the first run the chunk also checks, with `select_rule.py check-done` on a copy of rung 1's settlements beside a run of another version, whether settlements of two versions may stand in one ladder file; if the tool refuses, the fallback is to rerun rung 1's ablation arms on the ladder version (24 runs), and the foreman logs that cost.

Compute: per update about 30 CPU-h (tables and check) + 2 (eval set). A 7-day rerun of one DNase-seq row: at most 2 CPUs x 168 h = 336 CPU-h. Wall: 3 h per run.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh --time 02:00:00 --cpus 8 --mem 32000 main tools/onion_data/check_dataset.py --tables /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/tables/eic_v1_<k> --superset-of <previous version>` exits 0 and prints `rows of the older tables changed or missing: 0`, `hpc run nibi 'python3 /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/main/tools/onion_data/build_list.py pending --build-list /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/eic/build_list.tsv --priority 2 --out /scratch/mforooz/onion/measurements --work /scratch/mforooz/onion/work --files /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/catalog/encode/files.tsv | wc -l'` prints the number of priority-2 rows that `UPDATE_<k>.md` lists as still missing, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:D6u>/CHANGES_<k>.md`.

**D. Cost, critical path and risks.**
- Compute added by the version policy: about 250 CPU-h and 19 GPU-slice-h (section B.4), plus about 32 CPU-h per update and at most 336 CPU-h per 7-day rerun.
- Critical path: D6f at the freeze time, then E7a, E7b, L1a at once. The tail no longer holds the ladder. Rungs 2 to 6 take the newest version at gate G2b.
- Risks, each with its check. A comparison across versions: the hash refusals of `score.py`, `select_rule.py` and `arm_report.py`; `versions.tsv`; `FREEZE.md` names both versions. A version is edited: every update is a new directory, and `check_dataset.py --superset-of` proves the old lines. A row finishes during the freeze build: the as-of record; the row enters the next version. Rung 1 is settled with a small scorable set: the count is logged at gate G2, and rung 1's selected arm is rerun on the ladder version. Too many versions: the build rule of B.2. The validation DNase-seq rows never arrive: the stop rule of B.5; DNase-seq is then reported as non-scorable in the last version, with the reason. A tail row is resubmitted twice: `tail_resubmitted.tsv`. A second-experiment or priority-4 job touches a tail experiment's work directory: `TAIL.md` and the left-out rows. The regions of an eval set differ from `$RG`: the assertion.
- Review (`.orchestrate/review_A6.md`). F1 (the early collect could not start in time): the early collect and the interim tables are deleted; D6b runs at a fixed time on what is done. F2 (no rule if row 56 or 57 fails the 7-day run): the stop rule of B.5. F3 (row 56 would time out inside the signal pass): done by the foreman; the signal pass is replaced and row 56 has its 7-day run. Minors applied: line cites; "at least 10, not a bound"; HAP-1's training control; rows 46 and 52; the tail experiments' `se` rows are COMPLETED; the full peak-fetch command; one cost figure. Minors that no longer apply, because their chunks are deleted: the environment variable through `remote_check.sh`, the `dataset_probe` cause, the single baseline in E7p, the signal-free `$TPRE`.

**Decisions needed (Amendment 6).** None. Decision (s) is answered by PI ruling 38.

### Amendment 7 (2026-11-13: region budget and selection)

Seventh planner call-back. Amended once in place after the cold review `.orchestrate/review_A7.md` and PI rulings 39 to 41 (2026-11-13): every blocking finding is fixed; the declined minors are listed at the end of section F. It adds the experiments that answer the PI's questions of 2026-11-13 (`cruxvault/INSTRUCTIONS/peeling_the_onion/onion region budget and selection.md`): how many training regions, how to select them under a limited budget, whether 256 evaluation regions are enough; and the gap between seen and unseen loci. It changes no finished chunk, no PI ruling, no decision D1 to D16, no Log line and no existing chunk section. Where it touches an existing chunk it says "addendum to the foreman's brief of chunk X". Every chunk below is `task: new`. Names as in Amendments 2, 5 and 6. New names: `RR=$O/runs_region` (the runs of this amendment, a sibling of `$R`); `RGN=$O/region` (region lists, strata tables); `EV1=$O/eval/eic_v1/evalset.json` (sha b5f7a4cf); `EV1K=$O/eval/eic_v1/evalset_r1024.json`; `RSET=$RR/settlements/region/settlements.tsv`. "Window": one training example of 1,024 bins (25.6 kb). "Block": one aligned unit of 16,384 bins (409,600 bp) with a mask-valid fraction of at least 0.9; there are 6,724 (`cruxvault/results/t283/REGIONS.md`). "Tile": one aligned unit of 2,048 bins (51.2 kb) with a mask-valid fraction of at least 0.9. "Universe U": the 6,452 blocks that are neither evaluation nor fit regions (6,724 - 256 - 16), and the tiles inside them. "Seed spread": D13's, max minus min over 3 seeds, the larger of the two arms. "Rung class": `no context` (rung 1) or `context` (rungs 2 to 6, measured on rung 4). "Broad marks": H3K9me3, H3K27me3 and H3K36me3. "Region experiment": any run of this amendment.

**PI rulings applied (2026-11-13; they answer the former decisions (t) to (v)).** (39) No protocol change now: training windows may fall inside the evaluation regions. One arm excludes the evaluation regions from training, and the gap between seen and unseen loci is reported; the PI rules after he sees it. (40) Leak-free strata only. cCRE and ChromHMM strata are not used now; the idea is written down for later in `onion region budget and selection.md`. (41) The evaluation-region count may go to 512 on evidence; 1,024 needs the PI.

**Inputs read, and what was verified in code.** `cruxvault/results/region_budget/REGION_RESEARCH.md`, in full. Verified: the trainer draws a uniform cell and a uniform start over all window starts, with a mask test of `min_valid_frac` (0.5), no region list and no strata (`tools/minicandi/dataset.py:626-640`, `:642-665`); training windows fall inside the evaluation regions at about the genome rate (3.4%). The resolution arms draw 512 training regions disjoint from evaluation and fit regions (`tools/onion_data/recount.py:157-178`). `budgets.json`: `param_budget` 1,000,000; 80,000 windows per arm; 12 CPUs for a CPU rung. The budget of a run is `config["windows_seen"]` and the schedule follows it; `--max-windows` only stops a run early (`tools/minicandi/train.py:17-26, 123`), so a smaller budget needs its own config. `submit_arms.sh` takes `<name>=<json>` and a runs root, calls `score.sh` with no extra argument, and has no sbatch pass-through; the unmerged branch `chunk/onion-L1a` (5dfee5f) adds `SCORE_MEM` to it (read by diff). `score.sh:50` forwards trailing arguments. `select_rule.py compare` writes to the challenger's parent directory unless `--settlements` is given. The loss has weights per target kind and per assay, none per window (`tools/minicandi/losses.py`, module docstring). Rung 1 has 7 equal-budget arms and rung 4 has 8, each beside `matched_compute`. The score interval is a bootstrap over cells, and `suffstats.npz` holds sums per target, not per region. The frozen DNA embeddings are two sets (chunk A5c): Flashzoi, whose model trained on ENCODE tracks, and Nucleotide Transformer v2, a model trained on DNA sequence alone with a masked-token objective.

**A. The questions as testable statements.** Each expectation is written here, before any run. No region experiment is an architecture arm: none is in `ladder.json`; none is read by `check-rung`, `check-done`, chunk LC or a freeze file; no ladder settlement is reopened by any result below. Their comparisons use `select_rule.py compare --settlements $RSET` for the arithmetic, and every difference is also reported with its paired region-bootstrap interval (interface J), because 2 seed spreads of 3 seeds is about 4 standard deviations of a difference and tests little alone.

| id | statement | expectation (written before the runs) | deciding metric | rule that settles it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a1 | A smaller budget of windows ranks the arms of a rung as 80,000 windows does | pass at 40,000 for both rung classes; pass at 20,000 for rung 1 only (its curve is flat by about 24,000) | the ranking test below | the smallest budget that passes is the prototype budget of that rung class |
| a2 | At 80,000 windows, training on R blocks of U is as good as training on all of U | within 1 seed spread for R = 4,096 and 1,024 (each block visited 1.2 and 4.9 times); lower by more than 2 seed spreads for R = 64 (26 Mb, 78 visits); R = 256 between them | composite; per-assay headline 1 | the knee is the smallest R within 1 seed spread of the control and whose paired region interval includes 0; reported, no default changes |
| b | At a limited budget (20,000 windows), a weighted stratified draw beats the uniform draw | a weighted peak-based arm gains 1 to 2 seed spreads on headline 1 of the narrow marks; no arm passes the full rule; the unweighted peak arm is worse than uniform on pooled PIT deviation and on the broad marks by more than 1 seed spread | composite, with two vetoes | an arm wins only with a composite gain above 2 seed spreads, no scorer stratum, region class or broad mark worse by more than 2 of its own seed spreads, and pooled PIT deviation not worse; the best winner is confirmed once at 80,000 windows; a confirmed win is a proposal to the PI, not a change |
| c | 256 evaluation regions are enough | yes: the region half-width of a paired composite difference at 256 is at most 1 seed spread, and resolved orderings hold in at least 95% of region draws; per-assay intervals are limited by the 3 validation cells, not by regions | region-bootstrap half-width; ordering stability; split-half seed spread | section D |
| d | Seeing the evaluation loci in training does not raise the score | equivalence at today's rate: `today` minus `no_eval` is inside the margin of plus or minus 1 seed spread, with a paired region interval inside the same margin; `eval_heavy` (half of the windows from the evaluation regions) gains a positive amount below 2 seed spreads | composite; headline 1 in counts | reported as a finding (ruling 39); "no detectable gap" is stated only if both the seed rule and the region interval are inside the margin; else the gap is reported with its interval |

The ranking test of a1. Arms: the equal-budget arms of the rung (7 for rung 1, 8 for rung 4; `matched_compute` is left out). Statistic: the composite, mean of 3 seeds, per arm. A pair of arms is "resolved" when their difference at 80,000 windows exceeds 2 seed spreads. A budget passes when (1) every resolved pair keeps its order at that budget, (2) the top arm is the same, or is not resolved from it at 80,000, and (3) the Spearman rank correlation of the arms' composites with the 80,000-window composites is at least 0.8. Condition (3) counts only when the 80,000-window ranking is itself stable, i.e. when the Spearman correlation of seed 0 against the mean of seeds 1 and 2 at 80,000 is at least 0.8; else it prints `not informative` and conditions (1) and (2) decide. With no resolved pair the whole test prints `not informative`. The same test is printed for each goal term and for the logged validation loss.

**B. The experiments.**

**B.1 Budget (a1).** Budgets 20,000 and 40,000 windows, each its own config, 3 seeds; the 80,000-window runs are the ladder's own. Part 1: rung 1 on `eic_v1` and `$EV1`, 7 arms x 2 budgets x 3 seeds = 42 runs, beside L1a's runs. Part 2: rung 4 on the ladder version and its evaluation set, 8 x 2 x 3 = 48 runs, beside L4a's runs. The 10,000-window budget of the first draft is cut (its expectation was fail). Not tested, and said so in the answers page: the interaction of fewer windows with less territory, and model capacity (scaling is out of scope in `## Goal`).

**B.2 Territory (a2).** Rung 4 `base`, 80,000 windows, 3 seeds, `eic_v1` and `$EV1`. Arms: `terr_r64`, `terr_r256`, `terr_r1024`, `terr_r4096`: R blocks of U, nested, drawn with the training seed (one list per seed, so that the seed spread holds the choice of blocks); control `blocks_minus_eval` (all of U). 5 arms x 3 = 15 runs.

**B.3 Unseen loci (d; ruling 39).** Rung 4 `base`, 80,000 windows, 3 seeds, `eic_v1` and `$EV1`. Arms: `today` (the ladder's sampler, unchanged; it is rung 4 `base` on `eic_v1`, which the ladder does not run on that version); `no_eval` (today's sampler with one change: a drawn window that overlaps an evaluation or fit region is rejected and drawn again; a plain exclusion, with no move to the block universe); `eval_heavy` (half of the sampling mass on the 256 evaluation regions, half on U; the upper end of the dose-response 0%, 3.4%, 50%). 3 arms x 3 = 9 runs. Every arm trains on training tracks only; no validation track is read.

**B.4 Selection at a limited budget (b; ruling 40).** Rung 4 `base`, 20,000 windows, 3 seeds, `eic_v1` and `$EV1`, tiles of U. Controls at the same budget: `blocks_minus_eval_w20k` (uniform over U) and `terr_r1024_w20k` (random territory of 419 Mb, the fair comparator of `kcenter`). Every table reads training tracks, the DNA sequence and gene annotation only. Sampling share of stratum h: q_h. Genome share: g_h = valid bins of h / valid bins of U. Loss weight of a window from h: w = g_h / q_h; the sum over strata of q_h x w_h is 1, so the expected weighted loss equals the uniform loss.
- `peakfreq_w`: 5 strata by the fraction of training tracks (all assays) with a peak bin in the tile: 0, (0, 0.05], (0.05, 0.20], (0.20, 0.50], above 0.50; from the 267 training narrowPeak files; the denominator is the training tracks present in `eic_v1` that have a peak file, stated in `STRATA.md`; q_h proportional to sqrt(g_h); weighted.
- `peakfreq_u`: the same draw with w = 1. The one unweighted control: against `peakfreq_w` it isolates the weights, against the uniform control it isolates the tilt.
- `ccre_like_w`: the leak-free answer to "stratified for cCREs": classes built by the cCRE recipe from training-track peaks only. Per tile: promoter-like (an accessibility peak and an H3K4me3 peak in at least one training cell), enhancer-like (an accessibility peak with H3K27ac or H3K4me1 and no H3K4me3), accessible only, other marked (any other training peak), none. q_h proportional to sqrt(g_h); weighted.
- `variance_w`: deciles of the variance across training cells of log1p of the tile's mean count per million reads, averaged over assays; the top 3 deciles at twice the sampling mass; weighted.
- `kcenter`: 8,192 tiles (419 Mb) chosen by greedy k-center on a per-tile vector = log1p of the training-average rate per assay (from `/scratch/mforooz/onion/aux/eic_v1_average.h5`, whose `measurement_ids` must all have `in_eic = train`) joined to the tile mean of the Nucleotide Transformer v2 embedding (the PCA file of chunk A5c), each part standardized; w = cluster size / mean cluster size. The Flashzoi embedding is not read by any table: Flashzoi trained on ENCODE tracks that may include EIC validation and blind experiments, which is the leak that ruling 40 excludes. The Nucleotide Transformer embedding is leak-free because its model saw DNA sequence only and no functional track.
5 arms + 2 controls = 7 arms x 3 = 21 runs. The first draft's `gcgene_w` is cut (one weighted non-peak arm is kept). Confirmation: only if an arm wins at 20,000 windows, the best winner is run at 80,000 windows (3 seeds) against `blocks_minus_eval` of B.2 (chunk RTc).
- cCRE and full-stack ChromHMM: not used (ruling 40). They stay reporting strata of the scorer, which no training reads.
- Per-stratum reporting, for every arm of B.2 to B.4: per-assay headline 1 with the broad marks named; PIT deviation per scorer stratum (promoters, enhancers, gene bodies, heterochromatin, mappability deciles); and the composite per region class, where the class of an evaluation region is its training peak-frequency stratum (so quiescent regions are a visible row).

**B.5 Evaluation-region count (c; ruling 41).** (1) The scorer writes per-region sufficient statistics (pinned interface J; chunk PRg, after chunk SM). (2) `region_boot.py` resamples clusters of regions with replacement (2,000 draws); adjacent regions form one cluster (about 10 adjacent pairs are expected among 256); it recomputes the composite, each goal term, each per-assay metric and paired differences with the scorer's own aggregation. The interval is conditional on the validation cells; it is printed beside the interval over cells, and no total interval is claimed. (3) Data: rung 1 `base` and `scrambled_covariates`, 3 seeds each, and the four baselines of the composite, scored on `$EV1K`, a 1,024-region superset of `$EV1`: its 256 regions, its 16 fit regions unchanged, and the next 768 blocks of the same seeded order (`order[:256] + order[272:1040]`). `$EV1K` is built with E7a's exact build line; only the regions option changes. (4) Analysis: for each size m in 64, 128, 256, 512, 1,024, draws of m regions with replacement from the 1,024 (an m-out-of-n bootstrap): half-width of the composite, of each per-assay metric and of the paired difference; the share of draws that keep each resolved ordering; the seed spread on two disjoint halves of 128 regions (equal at 128 and at 256 means a genome-wide seed effect, which more regions do not lower); a consistency check (`region_boot.py metrics` on the 256 regions of `$EV1K` reproduces the `$EV1` scores of `rung1_base` seed 0). The answer comes from rung 1 and one pair of arms; `REGION_COUNT.md` says that it is extrapolated to rungs 2 to 6.

**Pinned interface I: training regions and exclusion (chunk TR).** Two optional config keys; without them the sampler, every batch, `meta` and the loss are bit-identical to today and `plan()` draws no extra random number.
- `exclude_regions`: the path of a JSON with `regions`, `fit_regions`, `region_bins`. `_draw_locus` rejects a start whose window overlaps any listed region and draws again inside its loop of tries (the loop count rises to 64 for this key; a window that still overlaps is never returned: the draw raises).
- `train_regions`: the path of a TSV (the text `{seed}` in the path is replaced by the training seed) with header comment lines `# roles_read=train`, `# universe=<text>`, `# sha256_inputs=<...>` and columns `chrom, start_bin, n_bins, stratum, sample_weight, loss_weight`. `_draw_locus` draws a row with probability proportional to `sample_weight`, then a start uniform in `[start_bin, start_bin + n_bins - L]` (`n_bins >= L` required). On a failed mask test it draws a new start in the same row (8 tries, then the best try), so the realized share of each row equals its `sample_weight`. `plan()` also returns `loss_weight` and `stratum`; the batch carries `w_window`, float32 `[B]`; `losses` multiplies every per-bin loss of window b by `w_window[b]` in every term (the main term, the base leg, the shape term and the multiscale term) and keeps today's denominators. A file without `# roles_read=train` is refused. The two keys may not be given together.
- The run record stores the sha256 of the file read, and `log.jsonl` the window count per stratum.

**Pinned interface J: per-region output of the scorer (chunk PRg; chunk SM is asked to keep its per-region sums reachable).** `score.py --per-region` writes `<out>/per_region.npz` beside `suffstats.npz`: `regions` `[R, 2]`, the ids of targets and pairs, and, for every additive statistic that `suffstats.npz` holds per target or per pair, the same statistic with one more axis over regions, such that the sum over regions equals the `suffstats.npz` value (relative difference at most 1e-9) and `scores.json` is byte-identical with and without the option. `tools/onion_eval/region_boot.py`: `metrics --per-region <npz> [--regions <index file>]`; `interval --arm <dir> [...] --baselines <dir> --n-boot 2000 --sizes 64,128,256,512,1024`; `paired --a <dirs> --b <dirs>`; `by-class --classes <tsv: chrom, start_bin, class>`; `split-half --arm <dirs>`. A metric that is not a function of additive per-region statistics is listed in `per_region.npz::not_additive`, and the tool prints `not available per region` for it.

**Pinned interface K: `slurm/minicandi/submit_arms.sh` (chunk SAx).** Two more environment variables, on top of L1a's `SCORE_MEM`: `EXTRA_SCORE_ARGS` (appended to the `score.sh` line, after the run arguments) and `NICE` (adds `--nice=$NICE` to the training and the scoring sbatch line). Without them the `DRY_RUN=1` output is byte-identical to the script as L1a merged it.

**C. Versions, seeds, cost, queue and order.**
- Versions. One dataset version and one evaluation-set version per comparison (Amendment 6, B.4). a1 part 1, a2, b, c and d: `eic_v1` (hash a068a867) with `$EV1`, or `$EV1K` for c. a1 part 2: the ladder version and its evaluation set. A result on `eic_v1` is not rerun on a later version.
- Seeds: 0, 1, 2 everywhere.
- Unit costs. Measured in the pilot on Fir: rung 4 trains at 0.0250 s per window on 12 CPUs (80,000 windows = 6.7 CPU-h; 20,000 = 1.7); rung 1 at 0.0466 s per window on a GPU slice (80,000 windows = 1.04 GPU-slice-h). Still estimates: rung 4's speed on Nibi; the scoring cost at 256 regions (planning figures 6.6 CPU-h, or 5.3 GPU-slice-h for rung 1) and its memory, until chunk SM, chunk E7b and L1a's first scoring report measured values; scoring cost linear in the region count.
- a1 part 1: training 21 x (20 + 40) / 80 x 1.04 = 16.4 GPU-slice-h; scoring 42 runs, between 42 x 0.66 = 28 and 42 x 5.3 = 223 GPU-slice-h.
- a1 part 2: training 24 x (1.68 + 3.35) = 121 CPU-h; scoring 48 x 6.6 = 317; 438 CPU-h.
- a2: 15 x 13.3 = 200 CPU-h. d: 9 x 13.3 = 120. b: 21 x (1.7 + 6.6) = 174, plus the strata tables 55 (estimate). c: (6 runs + 4 baselines) x 4 x 6.6 = 264 at the CPU planning figure. Conditional: the confirmation of b, 3 x 13.3 = 40; a count change at gate G2b (section D), 32 GPU-slice-h + 53 CPU-h.
- Total: about 1,250 CPU-h and 45 to 240 GPU-slice-h, i.e. about 4.3% of the 29,200 CPU-h of Amendment 2b (the first draft: 1,500 and 60 to 350). With Amendments 3 and 4 the additions to that budget stand at about 19% to 24% (the review's sum).
- Scoring caps. A region scoring job is submitted only after chunk SM is merged and pushed and only if SM's measured memory is at most 16000M per 256 regions (above that the 4000 MB per core rule bills a scoring several times its CPU-h); else the foreman asks the planner. If SM is not merged within 3 days, the foreman reports that region scoring still waits. If the measured scoring cost per run is above twice its planning figure (13.2 CPU-h, or 10.6 GPU-slice-h for rung 1), the 20,000-window runs of a1 are scored for seed 0 only.
- Queue rule (it protects the ladder). The limit is 1,000 queued jobs per user, array tasks included. (1) At most 120 region jobs are queued or running at any time. (2) A region batch is submitted only if (jobs in the queue) + (the batch) + (the job count of the next ladder wave, from its chunk section) is at most 900. (3) Every region job runs with `NICE=300`: below the ladder (0), above the QC fill (500). (4) Before a ladder wave submits, the foreman holds every pending region job (`scontrol hold <ids>`), and releases them (`scontrol release <ids>`) after the wave's jobs are in the queue; the ids are in each region chunk's `SUBMITTED.md`. (5) The rung-1 GPU trainings of RB1a stay held until L1a's scoring jobs have started. (6) Region runs spend fair-share whatever their nice value; if the ladder's pending time grows and the foreman sees region jobs running, he holds the pending region jobs until the ladder wave has started.
- Order. Now, with no cluster job except kit tests: V7, TR, ERx, STt, RBk. After L1a's branch is merged: SAx. Then RB1a (held as rule 5 says) and STa. Waits for SM: PRg and every region scoring. Waits for E7b: RTa, ECa and RB1s. Waits for L1a's runs: ECa, RB1s. Waits for the ladder version and L4a: a1 part 2. Before TR is launched the foreman reads SM's diff; if SM edits `tools/minicandi/dataset.py`, TR is cut from the umbrella after SM's merge.

**D. How the results feed back.**
- Prototype budget. If a1 passes at a budget for a rung class, later screening of that class (mix-and-match proposals, evolution proxy candidates, extra ruling-14 arms before they are entered) may use that budget. Every settlement under D13, every arm of `ladder.json` and every confirmation stays at the full budget.
- Default sampler. Uniform over the genome mask, as today. It changes only by a PI ruling. A winner of b that is confirmed at 80,000 windows goes to the PI as a proposal with its vetoes and per-stratum table.
- Evaluation-region count (ruling 41). `REGION_COUNT.md` (chunk ECb) starts with one of four lines. `keep 256`: both conditions of row c hold. `raise to 512`: a condition fails at 256 and holds at 512, and the split-half test shows a local seed effect. `raise to 1024 (needs the PI)`: it holds only at 1,024; the foreman asks the PI. `keep 256, add seeds`: the split-half test shows a genome-wide seed effect; regions would not help, and the foreman calls the planner under the existing rule of gate G2b (a chunk that raises the number of seeds). A raise needs chunk SM's measured scoring cost first. A raise is applied to a version as a whole: the version's evaluation set is rebuilt through the regions-file option (the 256 regions stay a subset; the fit regions stay), its baselines are scored on it (4 x 6.6 x 2 = 53 CPU-h at 512), and every run of that version that a later chunk reads is scored again on it. For the ladder version that means the rung-1 runs that chunk LC reads (3 seeds of rung 1's selected arm: 3 x 5.3 x 2 = 32 GPU-slice-h at the planning figure), also when the ladder version is `eic_v1`; a rung-1 settlement made within the 256-region set stays valid. 512 regions raise a small rung's run cost from 13.3 to 19.9 CPU-h (+50%). Timing: ECa waits for L1a's runs, E7b, SM and PRg, so `REGION_COUNT.md` may not exist at gate G2b. Then the ladder version keeps 256 and the count is applied to the last version only (from chunk FDa on); the report says so.
- Unseen loci (ruling 39). `UNSEEN_LOCI.md` goes to the PI. The ladder keeps its protocol.
- The answers are written into `onion region budget and selection.md` by the foreman from chunk RGs's slip, and both reports get one section on them. The foreman also writes the cCRE and ChromHMM idea of ruling 40 there, under a heading "For later".

Scorings with `--per-region` into `$O/eval/eic_v1/r1024/<name>/seed<s>`: `rung1_base` and `rung1_scrambled_covariates`, seeds 0 to 2 (L1a's runs, read only), and the four baselines; time limit 4 times the measured 256-region scoring time plus 50%; memory 4 times SM's measured figure unless SM's report gives another rule; `--nice=300`; under the queue rule and the scoring caps. No output goes under `$R`, and no ladder file reads these scores.
Depends on: ERx, PRg, SM (in kit `main`), E7b (accepted), L1a (the two arms' runs finished)

**F. Risks, each with its check.** A region experiment delays the ladder: the queue rule of section C (120 jobs, the 900 test, `NICE=300`, hold and release). A TR, SAx or PRg edit changes a ladder input: the digest rules, and no kit push while a ladder chunk submits. A stratum table reads a held-out track or a model that saw one: STt's refusals and tests; `# roles_read=train` in every list; no Flashzoi, cCRE or ChromHMM input. Weights are wrong: the sum rule, the same-row redraw of interface I, and the 2% check in RTa's first run. A region result is taken as a ladder settlement: its own runs root and `$RSET`; none is in `ladder.json`. The rung-1 budget result is applied to rungs with context: two rung classes, each with its own test. The scoring cost or memory is far above the estimate: the scoring caps. The 1,024-region set changes the old regions, targets or pairs: the superset rule and ECa's assertions. A count change splits the ladder across two evaluation sets: section D rescoring. The d result is misread as a leak: the track split is unchanged, and the report says so.
- Review minors declined. (1) "Add capacity arms (`param_budget` 0.5e6 and 2e6)": declined, scaling is out of scope in `## Goal`; the answers page says so. (2) "A two-way bootstrap over cells and regions for a total interval": declined, with 3 validation cells per assay it would not be stable; both one-way intervals are printed side by side.

**Decisions needed (Amendment 7).** None. The former lines (t), (u) and (v) are answered by PI rulings 39, 40 and 41.

**Rules that hold for every chunk.**
- After wave 0, builders never edit `cruxvault/INSTRUCTIONS/peeling_the_onion/long-horizon notepad.md`. Each builder returns its notepad entries in its hand-back report and the foreman appends them.
- `results/<T:X>/` means `cruxvault/results/<task id that the task map gives chunk X>/`. Chunk V0 creates the tasks and the map.
- New code is additive: `tools/onion_kit/`, `tools/onion_data/`, `tools/onion_eval/`, `tools/minicandi/`, `slurm/onion_kit/`, `slurm/onion_data/`, `slurm/onion_eval/`, `slurm/minicandi/`, `tests/test_onion_*.py`, `tests/test_minicandi_*.py`. No chunk edits `src/candi/` or an existing test, so `tools/golden.py check` stays at 0 ULP. `tools/golden.py` takes exactly `save <path>` or `check <path>`.
- Tests import tools as `tests/test_t146.py` does (insert `tools/` in `sys.path`). The package `__init__.py` files exist from V0.
- Laptop test form (light, single file): `PT = PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest`. Verified 2026-11-10: 72 passed on 4 files, `candi` imported from this worktree. Do not run `tests/test_t146.py` on the laptop (272 s; it trains).
- Cluster form. A chunk that needs the cluster pushes its own kit with `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh <chunk id>` to `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/<chunk id>` (the foreman pushes `main` after each merge). It runs a Python check with `RC = bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh <chunk id> <python args>` and a longer job with `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_run.sh <chunk id> <cpus> <mem MB> <hh:mm:ss> <python args>` (prints the job id). Both add `--cluster fir` or `--cluster nibi` to override `.orchestrate/cluster`. Every job builds its venv in `$SLURM_TMPDIR` from `requirements-fir.txt` and the kit's `wheels/` and sources `slurm/_kit_pin.sh`, so `candi` comes from the kit. Heavy work (suites, smokes, training, scoring) runs as sbatch jobs, never on the laptop.
- Cluster traps: always set `--time` (Nibi default is 15 min); omit `--partition`; CPU jobs use `--gres=none` and 4000 MB per core; GPU jobs use only `--gres=gpu:nvidia_h100_80gb_hbm3_1g.10gb:1` with 1 CPU on Fir or 2 CPU on Nibi and 10000M; exclude Fir nodes `fc10713,fc10715`; never pass a comma-valued variable through `sbatch --export`; never chain `afterok` on a finished job; `rsync -e 'ssh -o BatchMode=yes'`; exit 69 means the tunnel is down, stop and report; quote remote globs; the Bash tool shell is zsh; cap torch-importing array concurrency at 12 on Fir when a shared venv is used.
- Each chunk that uses compute reports CPU-h and GPU-h (`sacct -j <ids> --format=JobID,Elapsed,AllocTRES%60,State`), storage (`du -sh` on its own output directory only) and the file count it added (`find <its dir> -type f | wc -l`). Before any job that writes more than 10,000 files or 100 GB, the chunk reads `diskusage_report` and stops if the write would pass 80% of a space or file quota. `/project` quotas are shared by the group.
- Datasets never come to the laptop. Only tables under 1 MB come into `cruxvault/results/<task>/`.
- Blind tracks: no chunk before gate G4 scores a model or a baseline on a blind track. Building blind measurements is allowed; the loader refuses `in_eic = blind` rows unless `allow_blind` is set.

**Pinned interface A: clusters and layout.** Quotas read 2026-11-10. Fir: `/project` 20 of 28 TiB, 3280K of 5000K files (the file count moved 157K in one day); `/scratch` 3879 GiB of 19 TiB, 811K of 1000K files; CPU fair-share 0.094. Nibi: `/project` 4181 of 4657 GiB, 307K of 500K files; `/scratch` 1826 GiB used, soft quota 1024 GiB, hard limit 20480 GiB (`quota -s`; the PI's "20 TB" is the hard limit, and the grace column was empty); CPU fair-share 0.40. Nibi already holds the Caper venv, the chip-seq and atac-seq images and references (`/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/EIC_REPRO/003_pipeline/{caper_venv,sif,refs_encode,runner}`), the 7-track counterfactual testbed and the 48 single-end blind reprocessings.
- Default choice, confirmed or overturned by chunk K0 on evidence: **Nibi is the ONION cluster** for the FASTQ processing, the new EIC dataset, training and scoring. Fir serves the pilot on the existing `CANDI_STORE/eic`, the catalogs if Nibi nodes lack internet, and overflow training. K0 writes the name to `.orchestrate/cluster` and the evidence to `.orchestrate/cluster_evidence.md`. K0 overturns the default to Fir only if a 1 GiB write test on Nibi `/scratch/mforooz` fails or `quota -s` shows an expired grace. The paths below are the same string on both clusters.
```
/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/<kit id>/          # code kit, with wheels/
/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/                       # tables, ledgers, scores, small files
  catalog/encode/{experiments,libraries,files,donors}.tsv  ladder_census.tsv  eic_census.tsv
  catalog/{epiatlas,chipatlas}/experiments.tsv          catalog/source_report.md
  eic/accession_ledger.tsv   eic/build_list.tsv   eic/truth/{qnorm,se_unnormalized}/   eic/board/
  tables/<tables name>/{measurements.tsv,pairs.tsv,withheld.tsv,covariates.json,scorable.tsv,missing.tsv}
  eval/strata/masks.h5       eval/<tables name>/{evalset.json,layers.tsv,baselines/<name>/seed0/}
  runs/<arm>/seed<k>/{run.json,ckpt.pt,scores.json,suffstats.npz,DONE}
  logs/
/scratch/mforooz/onion/measurements/<measurement_id>/{counts.h5,pr1.h5,pr2.h5,provenance.json}
/scratch/mforooz/onion/work/                            # reads, BAMs, tagAligns kept for arms
genome layer: /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/genome/{dna.h5,mask.h5,chrom_sizes.json}
  (on Fir a symlink to /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/CANDI_STORE/genome; on Nibi built by K0 with
   `python -m candi.store build-genome`, sha256 of dna.h5 equal to the Fir manifest's fasta_sha256)
```
Tables names: `pilot_eic` (a view of the existing `CANDI_STORE/eic` on Fir, no new data), `eic_v1` (frozen for the whole ladder: training and validation experiments, base, halves, per-replicate views, alignment-level arms, FASTQ-level arms), `eic_v2` (superset: per-library and per-lane views, blind measurements; used for the onion layers and the final blind pass). Measurements live on scratch; D6b writes a tar copy per tables name to Nibi `/nearline/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion/` (file limit 1025, so one tar per 50 measurements).

**Pinned interface B: measurement id and tables.**
- `measurement_id = {experiment}.{unit}.{layer}.{arm}`. `unit`: `pooled`, `br<i>` (biological replicate), `br<i>tr<j>` (one library), `br<i>tr<j>ln<k>` (one lane or run of one library). `layer`: `uniform` (in-house ENCODE-DCC pipeline run at default settings), `native` (the existing CANDI_STORE track or ENCODE's own alignments), `testbed` (the existing counterfactual products). `arm`: `base` or `<knob><value>` with knob in `depth, crop, se, nodedup, mapq, ctl, abproxy` (e.g. `depth0.50`, `crop36`, `se`, `nodedup`, `mapq0`).
- `counts.h5`: one dataset per chromosome `/chrN`, shape `(n_bins,)`, uint16 or uint32, the floor grid of the genome layer, 25 bp. Counting rule: each read adds 1 to every bin from its start bin to its end bin (`tools/t112/bin25.py::counts_from_tagalign`). Root attrs `schema=1, measurement_id, depth_reads, depth_fragments, resolution=25, counting_rule`. `pr1.h5` and `pr2.h5` are the pseudoreplicate halves (random split of the reads without replacement, seed 0, as the pipeline's `encode_task_spr.py`), same layout. Validation and blind measurements also carry `signal.h5`, `pr1_signal.h5`, `pr2_signal.h5` (the assay's own pipeline signal track, float32, for the signal-space metric).
- `measurements.tsv` columns, in order: `measurement_id, experiment, assay, assay_class, cell, eic_track, in_eic (train|val|blind), unit, bio_rep, tech_rep, library, layer, arm_knob, arm_value, parent_measurement_id, reads_subset_of (measurement_id or empty), depth_reads, depth_fragments, run_type, read_length, platform, lab, pipeline, pipeline_version, image_digest, dedup, mapq_threshold, antibody, donor, control_measurement_id, has_pseudoreps, has_signal, nsc, rsc, nrf, pbc1, pbc2, frip, frag_len, counts_path, sha256`. A missing value is the empty string. `counts_path` is `h5:<absolute path>` or `store:<corpus root>:<biosample>:<assay>` (reads an existing CANDI_STORE track through `candi.store.reader.CorpusStore`).
- `assay_class` values and members: `accessibility_dnase` (DNase-seq), `accessibility_atac` (ATAC-seq), `broad_histone` (H3F3A, H3K27me3, H3K36me3, H3K4me1, H3K79me1, H3K79me2, H3K9me1, H3K9me2, H3K9me3, H4K20me1), `narrow_histone` (every other histone mark of the panel), `control` (chipseq-control). Source: the ENCODE histone standards page; D3 verifies the broad list and reports any change. The panel has no TF ChIP-seq.
- `pairs.tsv` columns: `pair_id, a, b, rung, assay, cell, knobs_differing, subset_pair (0|1), split (train|val|blind), never_trained (0|1)`. `rung` is one of `pseudoreplicate, sequencing, technical, isogenic, anisogenic, other_experiment, other_cell_type, designed`. `subset_pair = 1` when the reads of one side are a subset of the other side's reads (pure depth thinning): for such a pair depth-only scaling is exact in expectation, so it never counts toward headline 4 or its gates. `split` is the EIC role of the pair's tracks.
- `withheld.tsv` columns: `source_knob, source_value, target_knob, target_value, assay_class`. Each row is a (source covariates, target covariates) combination that training never samples, on any track. `never_trained = 1` in `pairs.tsv` exactly for designed validation or blind pairs whose combination is in `withheld.tsv`. `OnionDataset` in training mode refuses every pair whose combination is in `withheld.tsv`, and a test proves it.
- `covariates.json`: vocabularies of the categorical covariates and the column order. Metadata columns (D6 default arm), K_META = 11: `log2_depth, run_type_pe, read_length, dedup_on, mapq_threshold, pipeline_id, pipeline_version_id, lab_id, platform_id, antibody_id, donor_id`. QC columns (D6 arm "QC on input only", appended to C only, never to C'): `nsc, rsc, nrf, pbc1, pbc2, frip, frag_len`. An unknown categorical value has id 0.
- Metadata strata (used in every imbalance and balance report): `run_type`, `read_length` bin (36 or less, 37 to 75, 76 or more), `log2_depth` tercile within assay, `lab`, `platform`.
- Track order: the 35 assays of `CANDI_STORE/eic/manifest.json::assay_vocabulary`, then `chipseq-control` at index 35. `A = 36`. Controls are kept everywhere (EIC has 53 control experiments; 73 of 89 biosamples have a control track).

**Pinned interface C: miniCANDI contract (`tools/minicandi/contract.py`).**
```
Batch (dict of torch tensors; B windows, L bins, A = 36 tracks, K covariates):
  x_counts   int32   [B, L, A]   input counts, 0 where not given
  x_avail    bool    [B, A]      track given as input (False = missing or assay-masked)
  x_bin_mask bool    [B, L, A]   True = this bin of an available track is hidden from the input
  x_dna      uint8   [B, L*25]   codes A=0 C=1 G=2 T=3 N=4
  c_in       float32 [B, A, K]   input covariates C, 0 where x_avail is False
  c_out      float32 [B, A, K_META]  output covariates C'
  y_counts   int32   [B, L, A]   target counts
  y_avail    bool    [B, A]      a target exists
  y_kind     uint8   [B, A]      0 none, 1 imputation (track not given), 2 correction (other view
                                 of the given track), 3 same-view reconstruction
  avg        float32 [B, L, A]   optional, leave-one-cell-out per-position average rate (training cells)
  meta       list[dict]          cell, chrom, start_bin, measurement ids in and out
class MiniCandi(torch.nn.Module): forward(batch) -> {"n": float32 [B,L,A] > 0, "mu": float32 [B,L,A] > 0}
nb_nll(n, mu, y, mask) -> scalar            # contract.py, the core loss
loss_mask(batch) -> bool [B, L, A]          # contract.py: kinds 1 and 2 on every bin; kind 3 only on
                                            # bins where x_bin_mask is True (never a visible bin)
build_model(config: dict) -> MiniCandi      # tools/minicandi/registry.py; config["rung"] in 1..6
OnionDataset(tables_dir, mode, config, seed)  # tools/minicandi/dataset.py; mode in train|val|blind
ckpt.pt = torch.save({"state_dict": ..., "config": <the full config dict>, "param_count": int,
                      "windows_seen": int, "tables_sha256": str, "git_sha": str})
load_run(run_dir) -> (model, config)        # registry.py; the only loader E6 and E4 use
```
Config keys (json under `tools/minicandi/configs/`): `rung, context_bins (default 1024), covariate_injection (concat|film|tokens), mask_mode (token|drop), dna (own|off|shuffled), scramble_covariates (bool), count_transform (offset|log1p|asinh|anscombe|pearson|local_ratio|quantile|raw), qc_on_input, two_step, base_depth (native|fixed), average_input, control_input, predict_control, param_budget, windows_seen, lr, batch_size, simpler_than_base (true|false|null)`. `simpler_than_base` declares the simplicity order of an ablation arm against its rung's base: `true` for `dna_off` and for an arm that removes a component, `false` for an arm that adds one, `null` when neither is clearly simpler. CLI: `python tools/minicandi/train.py --config <json> --tables <dir> --seed <k> --out <run dir> [--device cpu|cuda]`.
- Matched parameters: `param_count` within 10% of `param_budget` (default 1.0e6). Matched compute: equal training FLOPs, i.e. `windows_seen x FLOPs per window`, with FLOPs per window measured by `torch.utils.flop_counter.FlopCounterMode` on one batch (in torch 2.6, no new dependency). Chunk P2b sets the two common budgets from the pilot and logs them.

**Pinned interface D: eval (`tools/onion_eval/`).**
- `evalset.json`: `{"tables", "regions": [[chrom, start_bin]] (fixed seeded random regions of 16384 bins, mask fraction >= 0.9), "imputation_targets": [{"target": measurement_id, "inputs": [measurement_id], "regime": "leave_one_assay_out"|"accessibility_only", "split"}], "correction_pairs": [{"source", "target", "never_trained", "subset_pair", "split"}], "replicate_pairs": [pair_id], "scorable": {"imputation": [assays], "correction": [assays], "layers": {rung: [assays]}}, "strata": [names]}`. Under the EIC split, a leave-one-assay-out target is a validation track, and its inputs are the training tracks of the same cell (uniform base views). The accessibility-only regime gives only the cell's DNase-seq or ATAC-seq training track.
- Scorable set (ruling 4), computed by `evalset.py` into `scorable.tsv` (`assay, n_train, n_val, n_val_cells, has_floors, imputation_scorable, correction_scorable, reason`). Imputation: at least 3 validation tracks from at least 3 distinct cells, each with pseudoreplicate halves, and at least 5 training tracks. Correction: at least 2 validation tracks that each have at least one never-trained pair with `subset_pair = 0`. A rung of the layers is usable for an assay with at least 5 pairs from at least 3 cells; otherwise the rung is reported pooled by `assay_class` with a `few pairs` flag. From the Fir manifest (2026-11-10): 11 assays have 3 validation tracks (H3K27me3, H3K36me3, H3K4me1, H3K9me3, H3K27ac, DNase-seq, H3K4me3, H3K4me2, H3K79me2, H3K9ac, H4K20me1); H4K20me1 has only 3 training tracks, so the expected imputation-scorable set has 10 assays. H2AFZ has 2 validation tracks, 10 assays have 1, 13 have 0 (ATAC-seq has 0 validation and 3 blind tracks). Macro averages and gate thresholds use scorable assays only. Every other assay stays in training and is reported in the table `non_scorable_assays.tsv`.
- `scores.json` keys. Each metric is `{"macro": x, "per_assay": {assay: x}, "ci": [lo, hi]}` (cluster bootstrap over cells) unless noted.
  `specific_variance_recovered_above_noise` (headline 1), a dict over spaces `{"counts_log1p": {...}, "signal_arcsinh": {...}}`; `r_spec` (2), same two spaces; `count_crps_skill_vs_position_average` with `count_crps`, `count_crps_oracle_scaled`, `count_crps_scale_error` (3); `correction_gap_closed_vs_depth_only_never_trained` with `correction_crps_model`, `correction_crps_depth_only`, `correction_crps_pseudoreplicate_floor`, and `per_knob` (4; pairs with `subset_pair = 1` excluded and reported apart as `subset_pairs_gap_closed`); `depth_law_abs_log2_error_never_trained` (5); `pit_deviation_pooled`, `pit_deviation_worst_stratum` (6); `predicted_over_replicate_variance` (7); `replicate_pair_gap_closed_vs_depth_only` (`per_rung`: real replicate pairs, source replicate to target replicate, same half-against-half floor; EIC has no cross-lab pair); `copy_leak` (`hidden_input_max_abs_change, unavailable_input_max_abs_change, same_view_copy_gain, copy_leak_pass`); `error_budget` (`level, shared, specific, explained, unexplained, noise, added`); `selection` (`space, imputation_gap_closed, correction_gap_closed, uncertainty_gap_closed, composite`); `n_targets, n_pairs, regime, split, scorable`.
- Metric 1 spaces (ruling 13). `counts_log1p`: truth and prediction are log(1 + count) at the target's own depth; the prediction is the NB mean decoded with the target's own C'; the noise floor N comes from the target's pseudoreplicate halves by Spearman-Brown per Haar level, weighted by the target's variance spectrum, depth in fragments. `signal_arcsinh`: truth is asinh of the assay's pipeline signal track; the prediction is the D9b signal derived from the predicted NB (X2); N comes from `pr1_signal.h5` and `pr2_signal.h5` the same way. An external EIC entry is scored in `signal_arcsinh` from its submitted track as it is. `selection.space` is always `counts_log1p` (ruling 13); `score.py` takes no option to change it, and it writes both spaces for headline 1 and 2 in every `scores.json`. `signal_arcsinh` serves the comparison with external EIC entries only.
- Selection (D13): `composite = 0.5*imputation + 0.25*correction + 0.25*uncertainty`; `imputation` is headline 1 in `selection.space`; `correction` is headline 4; `uncertainty = 1 - pit_deviation_pooled(arm)/pit_deviation_pooled(marginal NB)`. Scores come from validation, leave-one-assay-out, scorable assays.
- `select_rule.py` subcommands. `compare --incumbent <arm dir> --challenger <arm dir> --simpler {incumbent|challenger|none}` prints one verdict and appends a row `comparison, incumbent, challenger, simpler, composite_delta, seed_spread, per_goal_deltas, per_goal_spreads, copy_leak, verdict` to `settlements.tsv`. Seed spread of a quantity is max minus min over seeds, the larger of the two arms (ruling 7). Verdicts: `CHALLENGER` when the challenger's composite is higher by more than 2x the seed spread, no goal drops by more than 2x that goal's own spread, and it passes the copy-leak gate; `INCUMBENT` when the mirror condition holds for the incumbent, or when the difference is within the bars and `--simpler incumbent`; `CHALLENGER_AS_SIMPLER` when the difference is within the bars and `--simpler challenger` (D13: otherwise the simpler arm stays); `TIE_ASK_PI` only when the difference is within the bars and `--simpler none`; `RULE_NOT_APPLICABLE_ASK_PI` when a lead metric is undefined for an arm; an arm that fails the copy-leak gate is out. `--simpler` is read from the arm config's `simpler_than_base`. `table --runs <dir> [--prefix p]` prints one row per (arm, seed): `arm, seed, state (queued|running|done|failed), composite, copy_leak_pass`. `check-rung --runs <dir> --rung <r> --ladder <json>` prints `rung <r>: all comparisons settled or escalated` or the missing ones. `check-done --runs <dir> --ladder <json>` prints the two D14 lines.
- Metric functions in `metrics.py` (numpy; arrays already in the chosen space): `specific_variance_recovered(E_sp, V_sp, N)`, `shared_specific(truth[T,M], pred[T,M], groups[T], noise[T]|None)`, `r_spec(y, p, a)`, `crps_split(n, mu, y) -> {crps, oracle_scaled, scale_error}` (wraps `candi.bench.distributional.oracle_scale`), `gap_closed(model, baseline, floor)`, `depth_law_error(pred_total, source_total, log2_depth_ratio)`, `pit_deviation(n, mu, y, strata) -> {pooled, worst_stratum, per_stratum}` (non-randomized PIT, 21-point grid, as `candi.metrics.calibration_pit_curve`), `variance_ratio(n, mu, rep_a, rep_b, strata)`, `to_space(x, space, depth)`.
- Correction floor (ruling 6): for a pair (source X, target Y) all three predictors predict Y's half `pr2`. The model reads X with C' set to the covariates of the half (depth halved). Depth-only scales X by the depth ratio and fits one NB size per pair (`tools/t146/depth_baseline.py::fit_size`). The floor predicts `pr2` from `pr1` with one fitted NB size.
- Copy-leak guardrail, two parts. Part 1 (a hard wiring check): changing the true counts under `x_bin_mask`, or the counts of a track with `x_avail` False, must not change any output (`max abs change` at most 1e-6 relative). Part 2 (scoring rule): same-view reconstruction is scored and trained only on hidden bins, and denoising is scored only against an independent draw (the other pseudoreplicate half, or the complement of a thinned input). J-invariance check on available tracks: on same-view batches, `same_view_copy_gain` = CRPS on hidden bins minus CRPS on visible bins of the same tracks; the gate fails when the gain exceeds the threshold that E4 calibrates on a planted identity-copy model and a planted honest model in its tests (E4 hands the threshold back as a notepad entry).

**Early start, re-examined under the EIC scope.**
- What exists. Fir: `CANDI_STORE/eic` (89 biosamples, 436 tracks, 35 assays plus controls, the EIC roles in the `T_`/`V_`/`B_` prefixes; native processing). Nibi: the counterfactual testbed (131 products of 7 EIC tracks C07M20, C07M29, C19M16, C19M22, C40M17, C40M18, C12M02, with pseudoreplicate halves; `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t112_cf/{products,pseudoreps,tagaligns}`), and 48 single-end reprocessed blind tracks (the 3 ATAC tracks were left paired-end) under `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/EIC_REPRO/003_pipeline/results/`, 41 of them with filtered BAMs.
- An early start on `CANDI_STORE/eic` now uses the right split and the right tracks, and it is valid for the uncertainty goal (headline 6). It is still not a valid D13 scorer, for three reasons. (1) Headline 1 needs a pseudoreplicate noise floor for each validation track, and the store holds no reads. (2) Headline 4 needs never-trained, non-subset covariate pairs on validation tracks; the store has none, and the testbed covers 7 tracks whose EIC roles the ledger (D3) must still confirm. (3) The store's tracks went through mixed native processing, which is the measurement-axis imbalance that the new dataset removes; training on native tracks and scoring on uniform validation tracks would repeat the EIC defect.
- So the pilot (`pilot_eic`, chunks P1, P2a, P2b) proves the whole path and gives two of the three goal terms, labelled `pilot, not a D13 settlement`. It settles nothing.
- The shortest path to a valid scorer is the FASTQ build in priority order: (1) the 45 validation experiments and their controls: base, halves, per-replicate views, alignment-level arms, FASTQ-level arms, signal tracks; (2) the 267 training experiments and their controls: base, halves, per-replicate views, alignment-level arms; (3) FASTQ-level arms on a stratified subset of training experiments, so that the training split holds every knob. Priorities 1 to 3 freeze as `eic_v1` (chunks D4, D5, D7, D7d, D6a, D6b). Priority 4 (per-library and per-lane views, blind experiments) follows as `eic_v2` and never changes the ladder's data. Rung 1 starts on `eic_v1` at gate G2. The testbed joins `eic_v1` as extra views of its 7 tracks (chunk DT).
- Rungs that EIC data cannot populate: `other_experiment` has 0 pairs by construction (each EIC cell and assay has exactly one experiment), so EIC has no cross-lab pair; `anisogenic` and `sequencing` depend on what the experiments hold and are expected to be sparse (the census in D1 gives the counts; `flowcell_details` is often empty). The layers report prints these rungs with their pair count, and the post-ladder gate G5 names the ENCODE experiments that would fill them.

### GT Globus transfer of the testbed scratch tree, Nibi to Fir   wave 1
Goal: Copy Nibi `/scratch/mforooz/t112_cf/` to Fir through Globus over an already-authenticated route and verify it, so the foreman can delete the scratch copy before the purge noted for 2026-11-20 (ruling 15).
Owns: `tools/onion_kit/globus_transfer.sh`, `results/<T:GT>/` (`TRANSFER.md`: route found, task id, file count and bytes on both sides, checksum result; or `NO_ROUTE.md`).
Interfaces: source `alliancecan#nibi:/scratch/mforooz/t112_cf/`; destination `alliancecan#fir-globus:/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION_import/t112_cf/` (1.6 TB; Fir `/project` has about 8 TiB free; read `diskusage_report` on Fir first and check the file count of the source with `find ... | wc -l` against the free file quota, tarring per subdirectory on Nibi scratch first if the tree would add more than 300K files). Route check, in order: `globus whoami` on a Nibi login node and on a Fir login node (`module load globus-cli` if present); an existing token under `~/.globus*`. Never start a login flow and never authenticate for the PI: with no authenticated route, write `NO_ROUTE.md` with the exact commands tried and their output, and stop; the foreman then asks the PI to run the same transfer in the browser. With a route: `globus transfer --sync-level checksum --verify-checksum --recursive`, record the task id, and end the chunk once the task is accepted and its first status poll shows files moving. Verification (by the foreman, or a re-run of the script with `verify`): `globus task show <id>` is SUCCEEDED with 0 faults, and file count and total bytes match on both sides. Only then does the foreman delete Nibi `/scratch/mforooz/t112_cf`. No chunk of this plan reads the Fir copy while Nibi is the ONION cluster; the testbed is read from Nibi `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t112_cf/`. If K0 chose Fir, the same script also transfers `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t112_cf/{products,pseudoreps,tagaligns}`.

### E1 Headline metric library   wave 1
Goal: Implement the 7 headline metrics and the gap-closed helpers as pure numpy functions, with the metric space as a parameter and with known-answer tests.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/metrics.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_metrics.py`.
Interfaces: the function signatures of pinned interface D. `to_space(x, "counts_log1p"|"signal_arcsinh", depth)` is the only place a space is applied; no metric hard-wires a space. Reuse `candi.metrics.nb_crps`, `candi.metrics.calibration_pit_curve`, `candi.bench.distributional.oracle_scale` and `marginal_nb`, and `tools/eic_decomp/errdecomp.py` (`haar`, `gram_by_scale`, `shared_specific`, `calibrate`). Port `r_spec` from Max's `metrics_lib.py::rspec` (`gh api repos/mlibbrecht/01_2026-10-19_research/contents/projects/03_2026-10-20_epi_imputation/sprints/03_2026-10-24/scripts/metrics/metrics_lib.py`): about 400 quantile knots, the 0.2% guard, and the test that any linear rescale of the average scores exactly 0. Every CRPS returns its `oracle_scaled` and `scale_error` split. Tests hold a planted-error check per metric (the metric must move in the stated direction), a tie case, and a case with only 3 tracks per assay.

### E2 Onion layers along the replicate ladder   wave 1
Goal: Implement the primary onion decomposition of D12: nested variance differences between consecutive replicate rungs, in counts, per assay, per region stratum and per Haar band, with a pair count and an interval per rung, and with empty rungs reported as empty.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/layers.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_layers.py`.
Interfaces: `pair_variance(a, b, depth_a, depth_b, strata, bands) -> dict` (variance of the depth-matched difference in log1p counts; the deeper track is thinned to the shallower depth) and CLI `layers.py run --tables <dir> --evalset <json> --out <layers.tsv>`. `layers.tsv` columns: `assay_or_class, pooled_by_class (0|1), stratum, band (fine|peak|domain|broad|all), rung, n_pairs, n_cells, variance, layer_increment, ci_low, ci_high, ci_width, flag (ok|few pairs|not populated by EIC)`. Rung order: `pseudoreplicate, sequencing, technical, isogenic, anisogenic, other_experiment, other_cell_type`. A layer increment is taken against the nearest populated lower rung, and the row names that rung. Interval: cluster bootstrap over cells. Bands: Haar levels 1-3, 4-7, 8-11, 12-14 plus the remainder.
Depends on: V0
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_eval_layers.py -q` (a synthetic ladder with planted layer variances and one empty rung; the code recovers the planted values within the interval and flags the empty rung).

The builder verifies each URL by a web lookup before coding. `load_strata(path, names) -> dict[str, dict[chrom, np.ndarray]]`. `foreground_mask(avg, frac)` takes the top `frac` of bins by the leave-one-cell-out average rate; the test covers the old trap (a threshold of 0 selecting every position).

### E4 Training-free baselines and copy-leak guardrail   wave 1
Goal: Implement the three training-free baselines of D14 as predictors with the model's output format, and the two-part copy-leak guardrail as a hard gate.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/baselines.py`, `tools/onion_eval/guardrail.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_baselines.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_guardrail.py`.
Interfaces: coded against pinned interface C only; it does not import `tools/minicandi` (tests build batches by hand). `baselines.py`: `MarginalNB`, `PositionAverage`, `DepthOnlyScaling`, each with `fit(training batches)` and `predict(batch) -> {"n", "mu"}`. `MarginalNB` fits one NB per assay on training tracks. `PositionAverage` is the leave-one-cell-out per-position mean rate over training cells that measured the assay, scaled to the target depth from C', one NB size per assay fitted on training data. `DepthOnlyScaling` scales the source by the depth ratio with one NB size per pair; it exists for correction and replicate pairs only. `guardrail.py`: `copy_leak(model, batches) -> dict` with the keys of pinned interface D, and CLI `--run <dir>` (loads through `registry.load_run`, imported lazily). The tests hold a planted identity-copy model that must fail and a planted honest model that must pass; the calibrated `same_view_copy_gain` threshold is returned as a notepad entry. No blending of a model with any baseline anywhere (D10).

### M1 miniCANDI contract, dataset and shared components   wave 1
Goal: Implement the batch contract, the NB head and loss, the table-driven dataset that honors `withheld.tsv`, the covariate encoder, and the building blocks that all six rungs and all per-rung ablations share.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/contract.py`, `tools/minicandi/dataset.py`, `tools/minicandi/covariates.py`, `tools/minicandi/components.py`, `tools/minicandi/synth.py`, `tests/test_minicandi_contract.py`, `tests/test_minicandi_dataset.py`.
Interfaces: pinned interfaces B and C. `components.py`: `CovariateInjector(mode)` (concat, FiLM, tokens), `DnaTower(mode)` (own, off, shuffled), `MaskHandler(mode)` (token, drop), `CountTransform(name)` (each with `forward` and the depth add-back), `NBHead`. `dataset.py`, training mode: per window it draws one cell; the candidate inputs are that cell's training tracks; for each assay it draws one input measurement and one target measurement among the views of that track (same view = kind 3 with a random `x_bin_mask`; another view = kind 2), masks a random set of assays (kind 1; whole-assay masking hides that assay's C), never samples a pair whose combination is in `withheld.tsv`, never reads a row with `in_eic` of val or blind, and builds the leave-one-cell-out average on request. Validation mode is deterministic from `evalset.json`. An imputed target is decoded with the target's own C'. `mode = blind` raises unless `config["allow_blind"]` is true. `store:` paths support read-free binomial depth thinning for the pilot only. `synth.py` makes a small synthetic tables directory (with `withheld.tsv`) with planted cross-assay, context and covariate effects.
Depends on: V0

### D4 EIC build list and withheld design   wave 2
Goal: Write the build list of the new EIC dataset in priority order, and the table of never-trained covariate combinations (ruling 8).
Owns: `tools/onion_data/build_list.py`, `tests/test_onion_data_build_list.py`, `results/<T:D4>/` (`build_plan.md`, `withheld_design.md`).
Interfaces: writes `eic/build_list.tsv` with columns `row, priority (1|2|3|4), experiment, eic_track, role, assay, assay_class, unit, arm, fastq_files, fastq_gb, control_experiment, pipeline, keep_bam (0|1), expected_measurements` and `tables/eic_v1/withheld.tsv`. Priorities as in "Early start": 1 = validation experiments and their controls, every applicable arm, signal tracks; 2 = training experiments and their controls, base and alignment-level arms; 3 = FASTQ-level arms on a stratified subset of training experiments (stratified by assay class and metadata strata; at least 3 training tracks per scorable assay and per FASTQ-level knob where the reads allow); 4 = per-library and per-lane units, and the blind experiments (base, halves, signal tracks, arms). Only experiments with `match = 1` in the ledger enter (D8). Withheld design: a set of (source knob and value, target knob and value) combinations per assay class, chosen so that every knob appears in training on both sides and every validation track has at least 2 never-trained pairs with `subset_pair = 0`; combinations where source and target differ only in depth by thinning are marked `subset_pair` and never counted. The design is an agent decision; return it as a notepad entry. `build_plan.md` gives the row count per priority, the estimated CPU-h from the full-pipeline figure as an upper bound, and the transient storage per 50 concurrent rows.
Depends on: D1, D3

Determinism as AGENTS.md invariant 10. Rung 1: a permutation-invariant set encoder over the observed tracks at one bin (base `mask_mode = drop`; covariates as `film`; `tokens` means extra set members), a short DNA vector, an MLP, the NB head. `slurm/minicandi/train.sh <kit> <config> <tables> <seed> <out> <cpu|gpu>` then runs `score.sh`; the GPU form uses only the fixed slice spec.

Then arrays of `slurm/onion_data/fastq_uniform.sh` (and `fastq_dnase.sh`), one task per build-list row, priority 1 first; concurrency set so that transient storage stays under 4 TiB and added files under 150,000 on `/scratch` (compute both from `smoke_cost.tsv`; with the Nibi soft quota already exceeded, read `quota -s` and stop if a grace deadline shows less than 21 days). Never resubmit a task whose `sacct` state is COMPLETED. 30-minute check: at least 5 tasks reached the alignment step; fewer than 5% of started tasks failed; no failure on md5, quota or import; `/scratch` files grew by fewer than 2,000 per running task.

### D6f Build, check and freeze the eic_v1 tables   wave 5

Interfaces: start when no row of `RESUBMITTED.md` is queued or running. (1) `$RC main tools/onion_data/build_tables.py build --build-list $B --priorities 1,2,3 --measurements $M --work $W --testbed-rows $O/eic/testbed_rows.tsv --withheld $T1/withheld.tsv --ledger $O/eic/accession_ledger.tsv --out $T1` (chunk D6t). The 78 testbed rows enter with `in_eic = blind`. (2) `check_dataset.py` (Test below). (3) `fastq_cost.tsv` from every `$W/<experiment>/<arm>/ALIGN_DONE` and from `sacct`: per experiment `cpu_hours, wall_hours, max_rss_gb, n_reads, assay_class`, and one summary line with the median CPU-h per experiment, the CPU-h per million reads per pipeline, the total CPU-h of priorities 1 to 3 beside the projection (1,565 + 4,282 + 1,484 = 7,331), and the wall time of the build at the reached concurrency. (4) `qc_coverage.tsv`: the share of training and validation measurements with each QC column filled (input to chunk QCa). (5) Storage: `du -sh $M $W /scratch/mforooz/onion/fastq`, file counts, `diskusage_report`. (6) One tar per 50 measurements to `/nearline/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion/eic_v1/` if the directory can be created; else state that the only copy is on `/scratch`, with the purge rule that the Nibi documentation states.
- Stop rule: `eic_v1` needs every one of the 45 validation tracks with base, halves, signal and at least 2 never-trained non-subset pairs, and at least 97% of the 267 training tracks with base and halves. Below that, the chunk writes no tables, reports the missing rows with their error class, and the foreman calls the planner. After the tables pass they are frozen: no chunk edits a file in `$T1` again.

### D6c EIC build, priority 4 (submit, in three batches)   wave 5
Goal: Submit the priority-4 rows of the build list: per-library and per-lane units, and the blind experiments with their arms and signal tracks.
Owns: `results/<T:D6c>/SUBMITTED.md`, `results/<T:D6c>/batches.tsv`.
Interfaces: rows 496 to 1217 (722 rows, 178 experiments, 51 signal rows). The staging of priority 4 alone is 5,329 GB (`cruxvault/results/t180/projected_cost.tsv`, `new_or_row_fastq_gb`); staged reads are deleted only when their row has built. If every row were staged before any was built, the transient peak would be 5,329 GB on top of the 2,304 GiB that `/scratch` already held on 2026-11-11, which is over the plan's 4 TiB transient limit. So the rows go in three batches of at most 1,800 GB of staged reads each (5,329 / 3 = 1,776): the chunk sums `fastq_gb` per row from the build list, writes `batches.tsv` (`batch, rows, fastq_gb`), and submits batch 1 only: `KF=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/KF; bash $KF/slurm/onion_data/build_row.sh submit $KF /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/eic/build_list.tsv 4 50 2 <rows of batch 1>`. The worst transient is then 1,800 GB. `SUBMITTED.md` gives the literal command for batches 2 and 3; the foreman runs each when `build_list.py pending --priority 4 --rows <previous batch>` lists only failed rows and `du -sh /scratch/mforooz/onion/fastq` is under 300 GB. Before each batch: `quota -s` and `diskusage_report` (stop if a grace deadline shows less than 21 days), and `squeue` must hold no `onion_*` job of the batch's rows. The kit stays KF, so priorities 1 to 4 share one recipe. Health check as D6a at +20 to +30 minutes: at least 5 build tasks at the alignment step or later (or the stage queue is the stated cause); fewer than 5% of started tasks failed; no md5, quota or import error. The 48 older single-end blind reprocessings are not imported. No blind measurement is scored.
Depends on: D6b
Compute: 722 rows; projected 14,920 CPU-h and 76.3 h of wall at 50 rows at once (118,985 million reads x the measured CPU-h per million reads). Three batches and the queue: expect 5 to 6 days.
Test: `hpc run nibi 'squeue -u mforooz -n onion_build,onion_stage -h | wc -l'` prints a number above 0, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:D6c>/SUBMITTED.md -a -s cruxvault/results/<T:D6c>/batches.tsv`.

(4) `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_run.sh --name layers_v1 E7 8 32000 12:00:00 tools/onion_eval/layers.py run --tables $T1 --evalset $EV --out $O/eval/eic_v1/layers.tsv`. (5) `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_run.sh --name avg_v1 E7 8 32000 1-00:00:00 -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'tools'); from minicandi.dataset import build_average_h5; build_average_h5('$T1', '/scratch/mforooz/onion/aux/eic_v1_average.h5')"` (the average-as-input arm reads it through `config["average_h5"]`; it is outside the tables directory, so `tables_sha256` does not move). Goal: Train rung 1's base arm and each per-rung ablation arm with 3 seeds on `eic_v1` (Nibi) and score each run. Rung 1 leads the ladder so that its seed spread is known before the other rungs spend compute.
Owns: `results/<T:L1>/SUBMITTED.md`; Nibi `$R/rung1_<arm>/`, `$R/probe/`. It owns no config file (chunk LK wrote `tools/minicandi/configs/arms/rung1/*.json`) and creates no crux task or hypothesis (V1 and the foreman did).
Interfaces: arms: `base`, `dna_off`, `scrambled_covariates`, `scramble_all` (agent extra; rung 1 only), `inject_concat`, `inject_tokens`, `mask_token`, `matched_compute`. Submit with literal paths (a variable inside a single-quoted `hpc run` string arrives empty): `hpc run nibi 'bash /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/main/slurm/minicandi/submit_arms.sh /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/main /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/tables/eic_v1 /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/eval/eic_v1/evalset.json /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs 1'` (all arms of the rung, seeds 0, 1, 2; a training job on the GPU slice with 2 CPUs, then a dependent scoring job on the GPU slice with `--time=08:00:00`; `matched_compute` trains 24,565 windows). Nibi CPU timing probe (no CPU rung has been timed on Nibi): the same script with `EXTRA_TRAIN_ARGS="--max-windows 4000"`, `SEEDS=0`, an empty eval-set argument, the runs root `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs/probe` and rungs 2 and 5 with arm `base`; `SUBMITTED.md` records seconds per window from each `log.jsonl` beside the Fir values (0.0244 and 0.119), and the foreman scales the Budget by the ratio before wave 10. The chunk ends when `$R/SUBMITTED.tsv` lists 24 runs and `rung1_base` seed 0 passed the 30-minute check: loss lower than at step 0 by at least 5%, no NaN, parameter count within 10% of the budget. It also records the first scoring job's Elapsed (the measured scoring cost of rung 1 on `eic_v1`).

CPU beside the GPU: 2 CPUs per job on Nibi, so at most 2 x 150 = 300 CPU-h. Probe: 2 runs, about 3 CPU-h. Wall: under 1 day.

### L1b Rung 1: collect and settle   wave 9
Goal: Verify every rung 1 run, measure the seed spread, apply D13 to each per-rung comparison, and hand back the settlements.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder/rung1.json` (rung 1's comparisons; chunk LC merges the six rung files into `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json`), `results/<T:L1>/` (`SETTLEMENTS.md`, `scores.tsv`, `dev_evals.tsv`, `seed_spread.md`, `compute.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$R/settlements/rung1/settlements.tsv`.
Interfaces: required artefacts: `$R/rung1_<arm>/seed{0,1,2}/{DONE,SCORED,scores.json}` for the 8 arms; check: `select_rule.py table` shows 24 rows `done`; a failed run is resubmitted once through `submit_arms.sh` (it skips finished runs), then reported. Wiring check first: an arm whose `copy_leak` part 1 fails on any seed is out (verdict `INCUMBENT`); if `rung1_base` fails it, the chunk stops, settles nothing, and the foreman opens a fix chunk on the rung module and reruns the rung. For each arm except `base` and `matched_compute`: `select_rule.py compare --incumbent $R/rung1_base --challenger $R/rung1_<arm> --name rung1_<arm> --settlements $R/settlements/rung1/settlements.tsv` (the file name must be `settlements.tsv`: `select_rule` finds it by that name under the runs root; one file per rung, so the five parallel settle chunks never write one file). `--simpler` is read from the arm config; the two controls carry `simpler_than_base = false`, so a result within the bars is `INCUMBENT`, never a tie for the PI. Then `select_rule.py ladder-add --ladder tools/minicandi/configs/ladder/rung1.json --name rung1_<arm> --incumbent rung1_base --challenger rung1_<arm> --simpler <s> --d14 <true|false>` (`false` for `scramble_all`). `matched_compute` gets no verdict in the rung: it is an input of chunk LC. The settled configuration is `base` plus every arm with verdict `CHALLENGER` or `CHALLENGER_AS_SIMPLER`; with two or more such arms the chunk only names them (chunk LCa writes and runs the combined configuration). The controls never join the settled configuration; `scrambled_covariates` must be worse than `base` on the correction goal, and if it is not, the slip says so. A `TIE_ASK_PI` or `RULE_NOT_APPLICABLE_ASK_PI` verdict goes to the foreman as a PI question and blocks no other comparison; until the PI answers, every later chunk treats the incumbent of that comparison as the winner and the foreman logs it. When the PI answers, the foreman runs `ladder-add` for that comparison with `--verdict PI_CHALLENGER|PI_INCUMBENT|PI_CHALLENGER_AS_SIMPLER --notepad-ref "<entry title>"`; an answer for the challenger that arrives before the design freeze reopens the chunks that used the incumbent, and after the freeze it is reported only. `scores.tsv` and `dev_evals.tsv` come from `tools/onion_eval/arm_report.py` (chunk RP). The slip carries one notepad entry and one finding text per settlement, with its evidence path; the foreman records them (builders never run `crux finding`). Every CRPS is quoted with `oracle_scaled` and `scale_error`, and every number with its seed spread and its noise floor.
