If 2x the composite seed spread of rung 1 exceeds 0.05, the foreman calls the planner, who adds a chunk that raises the number of scored regions or seeds, and rung 1 is scored again before any other rung starts. Otherwise rungs 2 to 6 start (wave 10) with no planner call. GOOD NEWS line: **rung 1 of miniCANDI is settled on the new EIC data**, with the composite, the three goal terms and the seed spread.

**Names used in the amendment 2 sections.** `K=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/onion_kit/main` (the umbrella kit; the foreman pushes it after each merge). `O=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION`, `M=/scratch/mforooz/onion/measurements`, `W=/scratch/mforooz/onion/work`, `T1=$O/tables/eic_v1`, `T2=$O/tables/eic_v2`, `EV=$O/eval/eic_v1/evalset.json`, `R=$O/runs`. `PT` and `RC` are the laptop and cluster test forms of "Rules". The laptop form was run on 2026-11-11 on six existing test files: 299 passed, with `candi` imported from this worktree. A chunk that owns code pushes its own kit (`bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh <chunk id>`) before a cluster test; a chunk that owns no code uses the kit `main`.

**Pinned interface E (amendment 2): arm keys, wrappers, losses, arm names.** A config without a new key must train bit-identically to today (each chunk tests this on the synthetic tables with a fixed seed). The code as merged is the source of truth for everything this interface does not name.
- Dataset facts that already hold (`tools/minicandi/dataset.py`): `two_step = true` adds `c_base [B,A,K_META]` and `y_base [B,L,A]`; `base_depth = fixed` thins the base to 2^23 reads; `qc_on_input` appends the 7 QC columns to C only; `average_input` with `average_h5` adds `avg`; `control_input` and `predict_control` drop the control from the inputs or the targets.
- Wrappers (chunk A1 owns `registry.py`). `build_model(config)` builds the rung with `two_step` and `control_head` removed from the config, then applies `minicandi.control_head.wrap(model, config)` when `config["control_head"] != "channel"`, then `minicandi.two_step.wrap(model, config)` when `config["two_step"]` is true (lazy imports, this order). `wrap(inner: MiniCandi, config: dict) -> MiniCandi`. A wrapper's `param_count()` counts the inner model and the wrapper. `ckpt.pt` stores the full config, so `load_run` rebuilds the wrapper. A rung's own `Model(config)` still raises `NotApplicable` for `two_step`. `config["model_file"]` (a path relative to the repository root; default absent) makes `build_model` load `class Model` from that file with `importlib` in place of the rung module named by `config["rung"]`; the mix-and-match hybrids and the evolved candidates use it, and `load_run` follows it.
- Two-step correction (D15; chunk A1, `tools/minicandi/two_step.py`). Leg 1: the inner model with `c_out := c_base` predicts the base view of every target track, `(n_base, mu_base)`; a track with no base view (`c_base[..., 0] == 0`) uses its own `c_out` in leg 1. Leg 2: the inner model reads `x_counts := mu_base` (float, not detached), `x_avail :=` the tracks with a target, `x_bin_mask := False`, `c_in := c_base` and the batch's `c_out`. The output is leg 2's `{"n", "mu"}` plus `{"n_base", "mu_base"}`. `forward` never reads `y_base` or `y_counts`. `two_step` with `qc_on_input` raises `NotApplicable` (the base view has no QC vector). Arms: `two_step_native` (`base_depth = native`), `two_step_fixed` (`base_depth = fixed`). Direct correction is the incumbent.
- Losses (chunk A1, `tools/minicandi/losses.py`). `total_loss(out, batch, config) -> (loss, parts)`; `train.step_loss` calls it and `log.jsonl` gains `parts`. `loss`: `nb_nll` (default, `contract.nb_nll` on `contract.loss_mask`), `poisson_mean_detached_size` (Poisson likelihood for the mean; the NB size is fitted by the NB likelihood with the mean detached; the PPML form of the testbed), `nb_crps` (CRPS of the NB, differentiable). `loss_weights`: `{"impute": 1, "correct": 1, "same": 1, "base": 1}`, a weight per `y_kind` and for the base leg of two-step (NB likelihood of `y_base` under `(n_base, mu_base)`, on tracks with a base view).
- Control arms (chunk A4, `tools/minicandi/control_head.py`). `control_head`: `channel` (default: track 35 is an input channel that can be masked, and an output like any track) or `lambda_smooth` (output only: needs `control_input = false`, else `ValueError`; the control track's output mean is the inner model's control mean averaged in log space over `lambda_window_bins`, default 40 bins = 1 kb, then upsampled linearly; its NB size is one learned scalar). Arms: `control_output_only` (`control_input = false`), `control_none` (`control_input = false`, `predict_control = false`; never the EIC model, ruling 14), `control_lambda_head`.
- Dataset arms (chunk A2). `input_depth`: `native` (default) or `fixed` (an input deeper than `FIXED_BASE_LOG2_DEPTH` of its class is thinned to it by a binomial draw seeded by measurement, chromosome and start bin; `c_in` states the thinned depth). `mask_scheme`: `uniform` (default), `dirichlet` (the share of visible tracks per window from a symmetric Dirichlet draw), `panel` (the masked set copies the missing-assay pattern of another training cell). `bin_mask_shape`: `random` (default) or `span`. `train_on_validation`: default false; true lets `mode = train` read `in_eic = val` rows too, and is refused unless `config["final_model"]` is true (ruling 21). `dna_embedding_h5`: path; adds `x_dna_emb float32 [B, L, d_emb]`. `dna_shift_bp`: int, default 0; reads the DNA of the same chromosome shifted by this many bases (the wrong-locus control). `qc_table`: path of a TSV `measurement_id` + the 7 QC columns that overrides the QC columns of `measurements.tsv` (chunk QCb writes it; the frozen tables do not change).
- Component arms (chunk A3). `covariate_injection` gains `adaln_zero`. `covariate_dropout`: float, default 0 (training only: a track's C and C' embeddings are replaced by a learned null vector with this probability; the depth offset of the count transform is kept). `covariate_embedding`: `full` (default) or `numeric_only` (the categorical ids are zeroed). `dna` gains `precomputed` (`DnaTower(mode="precomputed", d_emb=...)` maps a float `[B, L, d_emb]` input to `[B, L, d_out]`). `offset_eps`: float, default 1.0 (the pseudocount of the offset transform). `count_transform` gains `soft_clip` (input only, the Borzoi form). `head`: `nb` (default) or `nb_shared_size` (one learned NB size per track).
- Rung support (chunk RX). Every rung reads each key above or raises `NotApplicable` with the reason. Every rung builds `average_input` (rung 5 does not today) and reads `batch["x_dna_emb"]` when `dna = precomputed`.
- Arm names and run directories. A ladder arm is `rung<k>_<arm>` and runs in `$R/rung<k>_<arm>/seed<s>/`. The settled configuration of rung k is `rung<k>_base` or `rung<k>_combined`. A mix-and-match combination is `mix<n>`, a confirmed evolved candidate is `evo<j>`, the design that stage 2 runs on is `design`, a stage-2 arm is `s2_<arm>`, each in `$R/<name>/seed<s>/`. The final design is `final` (training tracks only), the EIC model is `final_tv` (training plus validation tracks), the D10 test model is `final_tv_avg`. Settlement files are `$R/settlements/<group>/settlements.tsv` (the file name is fixed: `select_rule` finds settlements by the name `settlements.tsv` under the runs root).

Per-rung arms (D14 condition 1 unless marked extra; chunk LK writes one json per arm under `tools/minicandi/configs/arms/rung<r>/`, each one change from that directory's `base.json`):

| rung | base | arms at 80,000 windows | matched compute | runs (3 seeds) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | film, drop | `dna_off`, `scrambled_covariates`, `scramble_all` (extra, rung 1 only), `inject_concat`, `inject_tokens`, `mask_token` | 24,565 windows | (1 + 6) x 3 + 3 = 24 |
| 2 | film, token | `dna_off`, `scrambled_covariates`, `inject_concat`, `mask_drop`; `inject_tokens` is `NOT_APPLICABLE_BY_CONSTRUCTION` | 1,137,441 | (1 + 4) x 3 + 3 = 18 |
| 3 | film, token | `dna_off`, `scrambled_covariates`, `inject_concat`, `inject_tokens`, `mask_drop` | 944,882 | (1 + 5) x 3 + 3 = 21 |
| 4 | film, token, skips from both | as rung 3, plus `skip_dna`, `skip_counts` | 1,445,783 | (1 + 7) x 3 + 3 = 27 |
| 5 | tokens, drop | `dna_off`, `scrambled_covariates`, `inject_concat`, `inject_film`, `mask_token`, and the extras `axis_position_only`, `axis_track_only`, `axis_tied` | 106,667 | (1 + 8) x 3 + 3 = 30 |
| 6 | tokens, drop | `dna_off`, `scrambled_covariates`, `inject_concat`, `inject_film`, `mask_token`, and the extras `latents_small` (32), `latents_large` (512) | 872,727 | (1 + 7) x 3 + 3 = 27 |

Total: 24 + 18 + 21 + 27 + 30 + 27 = 147 runs (129 at 80,000 windows, 18 at matched compute). "Extra" arms are agent extras (ruling 14): `d14_condition_1 = false`. `scramble_all` runs on rung 1 only (foreman call, Log 2026-11-11). The controls (`scrambled_covariates`, `scramble_all`) carry `simpler_than_base = false`, so a control within the bars settles as `INCUMBENT`.

Stage-2 arms (they run once, on the design that the ladder, the mix-and-match step and the evolution step give, each against it with the other forks fixed; 3 seeds; chunk S2K writes the configs):

| group | arms | D14 condition 1 | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| covariate vector (D6) | `qc_on_input` | yes | D6 |
| correction route (D15) | `two_step_native` (against direct), `two_step_fixed` (against `two_step_native`) | yes | D15 |
| ChIP control | `control_output_only`, `control_none`, `control_lambda_head` | no | constitution; ruling 14 |
| per-position average (D10) | `average_input` (leave-one-cell-out input; never an output blend) | no | D10 |
| DNA source | `dna_flashzoi`, `dna_gnlm` (frozen, precomputed), and the leakage controls `dna_shuffled`, `dna_shift5mb` | no | constitution |
| covariate embedding and injection | `inject_adaln_zero`, `cov_dropout`, `cov_numeric_only` | no | constitution |
| depth-controlled input | `input_depth_fixed` | no | constitution |
| count transform | `tf_log1p`, `tf_asinh`, `tf_anscombe`, `tf_pearson`, `tf_local_ratio`, `tf_quantile`, `tf_raw`, `tf_soft_clip` (input only), `offset_eps0.1` | no | ablations document |
| loss and head | `loss_poisson_mean`, `loss_nb_crps`, `loss_impute_x4`, `head_shared_size` | no | constitution |
| masking | `mask_dirichlet`, `mask_panel`, `bin_mask_span` | no | constitution |

Total: 1 + 2 + 3 + 1 + 4 + 3 + 1 + 9 + 4 + 3 = 31 arms, 93 runs. Key groups for the final assembly are the rows of this table; the leakage controls `dna_shuffled` and `dna_shift5mb` pass `--simpler incumbent` and never join a design; `control_none` never joins a design (ruling 14); `qc_on_input` and a `two_step` arm are exclusive. Per-rung against stage 2: the ablations document lists five ablations "at every rung" (DNA off, scrambled covariates, covariate injection, matched parameters then matched compute, mask tokens or drop) and the rung 4 skip source; those run on every rung. Every other ablation of the constitution and of D6, D10 and D15 is a stage-2 ablation.

Goal: Write the tool and the job script that turn a frozen sequence model into a per-bin embedding file on the 25 bp grid, for Flashzoi and for one genomic language model.

The models are fixed by PI ruling 22: Flashzoi (`borzoi-pytorch`) and Nucleotide Transformer v2 500M multi-species (`transformers`); `requirements-dnaemb.txt` pins exact versions. The laptop tests use a stub model and a small synthetic genome: no download, no new package on the laptop.

### SC Scorer and eval-set additions   wave B1
Goal: Add the scorer and eval-set options that the stage-2 arms, the final EIC model and the external entries need.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/score.py`, `tools/onion_eval/evalset.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_score_arms.py`, `tests/test_onion_eval_evalset_arms.py`.
Interfaces: (1) `score.py --lambda-source auto|observed_control|predicted_control` (default `auto`, today's behavior). `predicted_control` scores the signal space of every ChIP target with the model's predicted control, also where an observed control exists, under keys with the suffix `_predicted_control`: this is the test of the "predicted control" signal route. (2) `evalset.py build --inputs-roles train|train,val` (default `train`): with `train,val`, a blind target's inputs also hold the cell's validation tracks (ruling 21); allowed only with `--include-blind`. A blind eval set may be named `evalset_blind.json` or `evalset_blind_<tag>.json`; `score.py` accepts both. (2b) `evalset.py build --pair-splits train,val`: only correction and replicate pairs whose `split` is in the list are written (default: today's behavior); chunk E8a uses it so that no blind pair is listed. (3) `evalset.py build --regions-bed <bed> [--regions-exclude]`: regions are drawn only inside (or only outside) the BED intervals; used for the leakage square. (4) A hook for chunk XE: `score.score(..., signal_override=<callable(target row, regions) -> float32 signal per region>)` replaces the model's derived signal in the `signal_arcsinh` path; with it, every count-space metric is written as undefined with the reason `external entry: signal only`. No existing key of `evalset.json` or `scores.json` changes, and an eval set built with default options is byte-identical to one built before the change (test).

Goal: Write the tool that builds a tables directory from verified measurement directories, and the check that guards it, and prove both on synthetic directories and on the rows that the EIC build has already finished.
Owns: `tools/onion_data/build_tables.py`, `tools/onion_data/check_dataset.py`, `tests/test_onion_data_build_tables.py`, `tests/test_onion_data_check_dataset.py`.
Interfaces: `build_tables.py build --build-list <tsv> --priorities <list> --measurements <dir> --work <dir> --testbed-rows <tsv> --withheld <tsv> --ledger <tsv> --out <tables dir> [--superset-of <tables dir>] [--dry-run]`. Rows: each measurement directory's `meta.json` (the `measurements.tsv` row that `align_views.py` and `import_testbed.py` write; read their writers first), kept only when `align_views`-style verification of that directory passes and its experiment has `match = 1` in the ledger; the testbed rows come through `import_testbed.rows_for_tables(path, allow_blind=True)` and keep `in_eic = blind`. A build-list row with no verified directory goes to `missing.tsv` (never fabricated). QC columns are filled from the run directory where it holds them (`$W/<experiment>/<arm>/qc.json`, `rep<i>.xcor.qc`); `frip` stays empty (the build calls no peaks). `pairs.tsv` (pinned interface B; the row conventions are the ones that `evalset.py`, `layers.py`, `score.py` and `pilot_tables.py` read and write, so read them first): `pseudoreplicate` (the `pr1` and `pr2` halves of one measurement), `isogenic` and `technical` (`br<i>` units and libraries of one experiment), `other_cell_type`, and `designed` pairs between views of one track, with `subset_pair`, `split` and `never_trained` (exactly the designed validation or blind pairs whose combination is in `withheld.tsv`). A designed pair is written only when both sides' halves derive from one read-level split of the parent (provenance `split`); pairs between two `layer = testbed` arms go to `pairs_shared_reads.tsv` (same columns, read by no scorer), because the testbed split each arm's halves independently (Log, DT). `covariates.json` as `covariates.load_covariates_json` reads it. With `--superset-of`, every row of the older tables must be present and byte-identical, else exit 1. `--out` must not exist or must be empty apart from `withheld.tsv` (frozen tables are never overwritten). `check_dataset.py --tables <dir>` prints the lines of the D6b Test and one line per replicate rung and assay with a pair count and an interval width (`0 pairs, not populated by EIC` for an empty rung), and exits nonzero on any violation; `--superset-of <dir>` adds `rows of the older tables changed or missing: 0`. Tests build a small tree with `synth.py`-style directories, plant each violation, and require the matching line to be nonzero.

Interfaces: the six `tools/minicandi/configs/rung<r>/base.json` files are not edited (an existing test pins their placeholder `windows_seen = 200000`); `configs/arms/rung<r>/base.json` is that file with `windows_seen = budgets.json["windows_seen_matched_params"]` (80000), and every other arm is one change from it. `ladder_arms.py` holds the table as data (`ARMS[rung] = {arm: {key: value}}`; `simpler_than_base` per arm: `true` for `dna_off`, `inject_concat`, `skip_dna`, `skip_counts`, `axis_position_only`, `axis_track_only`, `latents_small`; `false` for `inject_tokens` on rungs 1, 3 and 4, for `latents_large`, and for the controls `scrambled_covariates` and `scramble_all`, so that a control within the bars settles as `INCUMBENT`; `null` otherwise) and `ladder_arms.py write` regenerates the json files; the test requires the files on disk to equal the generated ones. `matched_compute.json` is the base with `windows_seen = budgets.json["windows_seen_at_matched_compute"][rung]`. `budgets.json` gains `"cuda_cpus": {"fir": 1, "nibi": 2}` (the Nibi rule is 2 CPUs per GPU-slice job; the file gave 1) and `"score_time": {"1": "08:00:00", "2": "08:00:00", "3": "08:00:00", "4": "08:00:00", "5": "1-00:00:00", "6": "08:00:00"}` (the `score.sh` default of 3 h is too short: with the planning factor, rung 1 needs about 5.3 h and rung 5 about 16 h on 2 CPUs).
- `submit_arms.sh <kit dir> <tables dir> <evalset.json> <runs root> <rung> [arm ...]`, run on a login node. An arm argument is a name (config `<kit dir>/tools/minicandi/configs/arms/rung<rung>/<arm>.json`, run directory `<runs root>/rung<rung>_<arm>/seed<seed>`) or `<name>=<json>` (that config; run directory `<runs root>/<name>/seed<seed>`, with no rung prefix, so `final_candidate=<json>` runs in `<runs root>/final_candidate/` and `rung3_s2_qc_on_input=<json>` in `<runs root>/rung3_s2_qc_on_input/`). Env: `SEEDS` (default `0 1 2`), `DRY_RUN=1` (print the sbatch lines), `EXTRA_TRAIN_ARGS` (passed to `train.sh`), `SCORE_TIME` (overrides `score_time`), `ONION_EXCLUDE=<node list>` (adds `--exclude` to every sbatch line; needed on Fir with the nodes of `.orchestrate/exclude_fir`), `DEVICE=cpu|cuda` (overrides the rung's device). For each (arm, seed) it skips a run whose `<run dir>/SCORED` exists or that has a queued or running job named `mc_<run dir name>_s<seed>`; else it submits a training job (`slurm/minicandi/train.sh` with no eval set; device, memory and workers from `budgets.json`; CPUs for a GPU job from `cuda_cpus` of the cluster it runs on; time = 3x the projected wall and at least 1 h) and a scoring job with `--dependency=afterok` and `--time` from `score_time` (`slurm/onion_eval/score.sh ... run <run dir>`; the GPU form when the device is `cuda`, else the 2-CPU form, so all seeds of a rung are scored on one device type), and appends `arm, seed, train job, score job, time` to `<runs root>/SUBMITTED.tsv`. An empty eval-set argument (`""`) submits training only.
- Tests: every arm config builds through `registry.build_model`, its parameter count is within 10% of the budget, and it differs from its base in exactly the declared keys; `inject_tokens` on rung 2 raises `NotApplicable`; the dry run prints 2 sbatch lines per run, with the pinned GPU slice spec and the cluster's CPU count for rung 1, `--gres=none` with 12 CPUs for rungs 2 to 6, the pinned scoring `--time`, the `--exclude` list when `ONION_EXCLUDE` is set, and the run directory `<runs root>/<name>/seed0` for a `<name>=<json>` argument.

### PG Genome-wide prediction and EIC export   wave B1
Goal: Write the job that predicts whole chromosomes for a list of targets and writes count predictions and signal tracks in the challenge's format.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/predict.py`, `slurm/minicandi/predict.sh`, `tests/test_minicandi_predict.py`.
Interfaces: `predict.py --run <run dir> --tables <dir> --evalset <json> --split val|blind [--allow-blind] --out <dir> [--chroms chr21] [--stride-bins 512] [--device auto]`. For each target of the eval set's imputation items of that split (leave-one-assay-out inputs), it tiles the chromosome with windows of `context_bins` at the given stride, keeps the centre `stride` bins of each window, decodes with the target's own C', and writes `<out>/counts/<eic_track>.h5` (`mu`, `n`, float16 per chromosome), `<out>/signal/<eic_track>.npy` and `<out>/signal_plug_in/<eic_track>.npy` (the challenge format that `eic_score.load_prediction` reads: a pickled dict `{chrom: float32 on the raw 25 bp grid}`, chr1 to chr22 and chrX), `<out>/lambda_source.tsv`, and `DONE`. Signal comes from `signal_from_counts.signal_from_nb` (expectation, and plug-in beside it; DNase-seq by the MACS2 route without control, rulings 19 and 20); a ChIP target with no observed control uses the model's predicted control and is labelled `predicted_control`. `--split blind` needs `--allow-blind` and writes to `BLIND_PASSES.tsv` beside the eval set, with the scorer's own columns and writer (`score.LEDGER_COLUMNS`, `score.ledger_append`): the `run` column holds the key `predict:<resolved run dir>` (never the bare run directory, because `score.py` refuses a blind score of a run whose key already has a row for that eval set), status `started` before the first target and `predicted` after the last. A second prediction of the same key and eval set is refused unless `--repeat-blind-reason "<text>"` is given; a resumed job that finds its own `started` row and unfinished targets continues without a new row. Skip-if-exists per target. Count files are 51 tracks x 123.5 M bins x 2 arrays x 2 bytes = about 25 GB per model, beside 50 GB of signal. Tests on `synth.py` tables: a score row and a predict row of one run do not block each other; the centre crop of overlapping windows equals a single full-window prediction where both exist; the `.npy` loads through `eic_score.load_prediction`; a blind split without the flag raises.

### EVt Proxy scorer for the evolution step   wave B1
Goal: Write the frozen scorer that a `crux auto` run calls on one candidate recipe.
Owns: `tools/onion_eval/auto_proxy.py`, `slurm/onion_eval/auto_proxy.sh`, `tests/test_onion_eval_auto_proxy.py`.
Interfaces: `python3 tools/onion_eval/auto_proxy.py --recipe <dir with config.json and model.py> [--windows 20000] [--seeds 0,1] [--tables <eic_v1>] [--evalset <proxy evalset>] [--cluster nibi] [--local]`. It prints exactly one JSON object: `{"passed": true|false, "reason": str, "composite": {"value": x}, "imputation": {"value": x}, "correction": {"value": x}, "uncertainty": {"value": x}, "seed_values": [...], "param_count": n, "flops_per_window": f, "cpu_hours": c}` and exits non-zero when `passed` is false (the `crux-autopilot` scorer contract). Steps: static checks on the candidate (the config holds none of `allow_blind`, `train_on_validation`, `final_model`; `model_file` points at the recipe's own `model.py`; the module imports only `torch`, the standard library and `minicandi.{contract,components,covariates}`, checked on the syntax tree; the parameter count is within 10% of 1.0e6); `contract.wiring_check` with a perturbed head (the hard gate); then, per seed, one job through `hpc run <cluster>` that trains `--windows` windows and scores `mode = val` on the proxy eval set (it waits for the job and reads `scores.json`); the composite is `select_rule`'s clipped composite, averaged over the seeds. It appends the attempt's CPU-h to `<runs root>/evolve/TALLY.tsv` and refuses (`passed` false, reason `budget exhausted`) when the tally passes `ONION_EVOLVE_CAP_CPUH` (default 3000). `--local` trains and scores in-process on given tables (the laptop test on `synth.py` tables, 40 windows). Exit 69 from `hpc` is reported as `passed` false with the reason `tunnel down`, so the driver discards the attempt and no score is invented.
Depends on: none (it calls `registry.build_model` with `model_file`, chunk A1, same wave; the laptop test uses a rung config without `model_file` and one stub with it marked to run after A1 merges)
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_eval_auto_proxy.py -q` prints 0 failed.

### FZ Which EIC experiments are among the Borzoi and Flashzoi training targets   wave B1
Goal: List, read-only and from the published target table, which EIC training, validation and blind experiments were training targets of Borzoi (whose targets Flashzoi shares), so that the PI can rule on evidence whether a Flashzoi-based design may be the EIC-comparison entry.
Owns: `tools/onion_data/borzoi_targets.py`, `tests/test_onion_data_borzoi_targets.py`, `results/<T:FZ>/` (`FLASHZOI_LEAKAGE.md`, `borzoi_eic_overlap.tsv`, `source.md`).
Interfaces: find the published human target table of Borzoi by a read-only web lookup (the `targets_human.txt` of the Borzoi repository or of the paper's data release; record URL, date and md5 in `source.md`; confirm from the Flashzoi paper or model card that Flashzoi was trained on the same targets). `borzoi_targets.py overlap --targets <file> --ledger <accession_ledger.tsv> --files <catalog files.tsv> --out <tsv>` matches target identifiers (ENCODE file or experiment accessions) to the EIC experiments of the ledger, through the catalog's file-to-experiment map; the two small inputs are read on Nibi through `remote_check.sh`, and only the output table comes to the laptop. `borzoi_eic_overlap.tsv`: `eic_track, role, assay, cell, experiment, in_borzoi_targets (0|1), matched_by, target_identifier`. `FLASHZOI_LEAKAGE.md`: counts per role (train, val, blind), the list of blind tracks that are targets, and the same assay and cell matches where the experiment differs (a weaker form). No model is run and no blind count is read.
Depends on: none
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_onion_data_borzoi_targets.py -q` prints 0 failed, and `awk -F'\t' 'NR>1 && $2=="blind"' cruxvault/results/<T:FZ>/borzoi_eic_overlap.tsv | wc -l` prints 51.

### S2K Stage-2 arm configs and the all-arms integration test   wave B3
Goal: Write the generator of the 31 stage-2 arm configs for any rung or hybrid, and prove that every arm trains, passes the wiring check and scores on the synthetic tables, on every rung where it applies.

An arm that raises `NotApplicable` on a rung is written to `arms.tsv` with that reason and no json. The integration test, on `synth.py` tables with small widths and a 64-bin context: for rungs 2 and 5, every applicable arm is built through the registry and gets 3 training steps, a finite loss and `contract.wiring_check`; for rungs 1, 3, 4 and 6 the same for three arms only (`two_step_native`, `average_input`, `control_lambda_head`); `load_run` and one `score.score` call run once, on rung 2 with `two_step_native`. The builder measures the test time and keeps it under 3 minutes by cutting steps, never arms of rungs 2 and 5 (chunk AS covers every arm on real data).

### AS Arm smoke on the pilot tables (Fir)   wave B4
Goal: Run every per-rung arm and every stage-2 arm that needs no new input file for 2,000 windows on real data (`pilot_eic`, Fir), so that a broken arm is found before `eic_v1` exists.
Owns: `results/<T:AS>/` (`ARM_SMOKE.md`, `arm_smoke.tsv`); Fir `$R/smoke/` and `/scratch/mforooz/onion/aux/pilot_average.h5`.
Interfaces: the foreman pushes the kit `main` to Fir first. Every job gets the exclude list: `ONION_EXCLUDE="$(sed 's/#.*//' .orchestrate/exclude_fir | tr -s ', \n' ',')"` is passed to `submit_arms.sh`; a setup failure is retried once on another node. Runs: the 43 per-rung arm configs that are not a base (through `submit_arms.sh` with `EXTRA_TRAIN_ARGS="--max-windows 2000"`, `SEEDS=0`, an empty eval-set argument and the runs root `$R/smoke`), and the stage-2 arms on rung 2 and on rung 5 except `dna_flashzoi`, `dna_gnlm` (the embeddings are on Nibi) and `qc_on_input` (the pilot rows have no QC): 28 arms x 2 rungs, as `smoke_r<k>_<arm>=<json>` arguments. `average_input` first builds `pilot_average.h5` with `build_average_h5` and reports its cost (the estimate for `eic_v1`). `two_step` uses the native base view of the pilot. Per run, `arm_smoke.tsv` holds: loss at step 0 and at the end, NaN count, parameter count, FLOPs per window, seconds per window, peak RSS, and the part-1 result of `guardrail.py --run <dir> --tables <pilot tables> --split train`. A run passes when the loss fell by at least 5%, no NaN appeared and part 1 passed. Every number carries the label `pilot, not a D13 settlement`. A failing arm is reported with its traceback; the foreman opens a fix chunk on the owning file.

Rung 1 (7 runs): GPU slice, about 0.1 GPU-h each. Total about 120 CPU-h and 1 GPU-h, plus the average table (not measured; estimate 20 CPU-h). Wall: 3 h.

The 30-minute check is on `rung2_base` seed 0. ### LCa Combined configurations within a rung, and second scorings for one device type (submit)   wave 12
Goal: For each rung where two or more arms won, write the combined configuration and run it with 3 seeds; and score again, on one device type, every arm of a cross-rung comparison whose two rungs were scored on different device types.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/arms/rung<r>/combined.json` for the rungs that need one, `results/<T:LC>/SUBMITTED.md`; Nibi `$R/rung<r>_combined/`, `$R/rung<r>_<arm>_gpu/`.
Interfaces: winners are read from the six `ladder/rung<r>.json` files and the latest rows of `$R/settlements/rung<r>/settlements.tsv` (verdict `CHALLENGER` or `CHALLENGER_AS_SIMPLER`, or a `PI_` verdict; never a control or `matched_compute`; a comparison whose tie is still with the PI counts as won by the incumbent, and `SUBMITTED.md` names it). `combined.json` is the base with every winner's change and `simpler_than_base = null`. Submit through `submit_arms.sh ... <r> combined`. A rung with 0 or 1 winner needs no run. Device rule (logged foreman rule: all runs of one comparison are scored on one device type): the cross-rung comparisons are 1-2, 2-3, 3-4, 3-5, 5-6, each for the settled configurations and for the `matched_compute` arms. Rung 1 is scored on the GPU; rungs 2 to 6 on CPU, unless L5a moved rung 5 to the GPU. For each comparison whose two sides differ in device type, the CPU-scored side (its settled configuration and its `matched_compute` arm, 3 seeds each) is scored a second time on the GPU slice: `score.sh <kit> <evalset> $R/<arm>_gpu/seed<s> run $R/<arm>/seed<s>` in the GPU form with `--time=08:00:00`. With rung 5 on CPU that is rung 2 only (6 scorings); with rung 5 on the GPU it is rungs 2, 3 and 6 (18 scorings). Chunk LC uses the `_gpu` directories for those comparisons only.
Depends on: L1b, L2b, L3b, L4b, L5b, L6b
Compute: at most 6 rungs x 3 seeds = 18 combined runs: 3 x 1.04 GPU-h and 3 x (6.5 + 6.5 + 6.7 + 31.7 + 6.5) = 173.7 CPU-h of training; scoring with the planning factor 3 x 5.3 = 16 GPU-h and 3 x (4 x 6.6 + 32) = 175 CPU-h. Second scorings on the GPU: not measured for rungs 2 to 6 on a GPU; bound by rung 1's GPU scoring cost (rung 1's forward pass is the costliest), 6 x 5.3 = 32 GPU-h with the planning factor (6 x 0.66 = 4 at the pilot cost), or 18 x 5.3 = 95 GPU-h if rung 5 is on the GPU. At most 350 CPU-h and 51 GPU-h (114 GPU-h in the rung-5-on-GPU case). Wall: under 1 day.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix rung` lists a `rung<r>_combined` row with 3 seeds for each rung named in `SUBMITTED.md` and the `_gpu` rows it names, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:LC>/SUBMITTED.md`.

### LC Cross-rung comparison and the selected rung   wave 13
Goal: Settle the combined configurations, compare the rungs at matched parameters and at matched compute, answer the five ladder questions, and name the selected rung.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json` (created here: the six rung files merged, plus the combined and cross-rung comparisons), `tools/minicandi/configs/selected.json` (a copy of the selected rung's settled configuration), `results/<T:LC>/` (`LADDER.md`, `shared_specific_by_rung.tsv`, `cross_rung.tsv`, `what_worked.tsv`, `compute.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$R/settlements/ladder/settlements.tsv`.
Interfaces: every `compare` here passes `--settlements $R/settlements/ladder/settlements.tsv`. (1) Each `rung<r>_combined` against the best single winner of its rung (`--simpler incumbent`): the combined configuration is the rung's settled configuration only with verdict `CHALLENGER`; else the best single winner is (the highest composite). (2) The five listed comparisons between settled rung configurations at matched parameters (1.0e6 parameters, 80,000 windows): 1 to 2 (local context), 2 to 3 (global attention), 3 to 4 (skips), 3 to 5 (attention across tracks), 5 to 6 (latent bottleneck): `select_rule.py compare --incumbent $R/<lower rung> --challenger $R/<higher rung> --simpler incumbent --name ladder_<a>_to_<b>`, `--d14 true`. Where LCa wrote `_gpu` score directories for a comparison, both sides of that comparison are read from GPU-scored directories. All five are always run and recorded: they are the five answers. (3) The same five at matched compute on `rung<r>_matched_compute` (2.4e15 training FLOPs), `--name ladder_<a>_to_<b>_matched_compute`, `--d14 true`; this is the "matched compute" ablation of every rung. (4) The selected rung, by a walk over the matched-parameter results. Start with rung 1 as current. Step 1-2: rung 2 becomes current only with verdict `CHALLENGER`. Step 2-3: the same. Steps to rungs 4 and 5: each is compared with the current rung. If the current rung is rung 3, those are the listed comparisons. If rung 3 lost to rung 2 (or rung 2 to rung 1), the chunk adds the direct comparisons of rungs 4 and 5 with the current rung (`ladder_<current>_to_4`, `ladder_<current>_to_5`, `--d14 false`), with the `_gpu` rule of LCa applied by one more scoring where the device types differ. Rung 6 is compared with rung 5 (listed) and, if rung 5 did not become a candidate, also with the current rung (`--d14 false`). The candidates are the rungs that beat the current rung with `CHALLENGER`. With no candidate the current rung is selected; with one, it is; with two or more, the chunk compares them pairwise (`--simpler none`), and the one that beats the others is selected; a tie goes to the PI, and until the answer the lowest-numbered tied rung is used and the foreman logs it. Where the matched-parameter and matched-compute verdicts of one step disagree, the chunk reports both; the walk uses matched parameters, and the foreman takes the disagreement to the PI (D1, case 2). (5) `shared_specific_by_rung.tsv`: each rung's gain in shared and in specific error against the rung below and against the position average. (6) `what_worked.tsv`: for every per-rung arm and every rung step, the composite delta, the three goal deltas and the seed spread (the input of chunk MXp). (7) `ladder.json` through `select_rule.py ladder-add` for every comparison. The slip carries one notepad entry and one finding text per comparison.
Depends on: LCa (runs finished, or none needed), RP
Compute: a few second scorings for added direct comparisons: at most 12 x 5.3 = 64 GPU-h in the worst case, usually 0; about 5 CPU-h. Wall: 4 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh LC && bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh LC tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py check-done --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --ladder tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json` prints `D14 condition 1: PASS` (the D6 and D15 comparisons are added later by S2b), and `test -s tools/minicandi/configs/selected.json -a -s cruxvault/results/<T:LC>/what_worked.tsv`.

**Gate G3 (after LC).** GOOD NEWS line: **the six-rung ladder is settled**, with the selected rung and the five answers. The foreman checks `LADDER.md`, runs `compute_tally.py milestone --name G3`, and starts the mix-and-match step (ruling 23) with no planner call. The planner is called only if a cross-rung step went to the PI and the answer changes the selected rung.

#### Mix and match, then evolution (PI ruling 23)

### MXp Mix-and-match combinations: the proposal   wave 14
Goal: Choose a few combinations of winning ideas from different rungs, with the reason for each from the ladder evidence.
Owns: `results/<T:MX>/MIXMATCH.md`, `results/<T:MX>/combinations.tsv`.
Interfaces: inputs: `what_worked.tsv`, `shared_specific_by_rung.tsv`, the six `SETTLEMENTS.md`, the copy-gain diagnostics. Output: at most 6 combinations. `combinations.tsv` columns: `name (mix1..mix6), base_rung, ideas, kind (config|hybrid), files, simpler_than_selected (true|false), expectation, reason`. A `config` combination is the selected configuration, or another rung's settled configuration, with config keys that won on other rungs (for example an injection mode, a mask mode, a skip source). A `hybrid` combination joins components of two rungs in a new module (for example count skips of rung 4 on the axial trunk of rung 5, or the track-axis attention of rung 5 on the bottleneck of rung 3); its `files` are `tools/minicandi/rungs/mix<n>.py`, `tools/minicandi/configs/mix/mix<n>.json`, `tests/test_minicandi_mix<n>.py`, and its row holds a module specification that one builder can code in one sitting (data flow with shapes, which existing rung code it reuses, the parameter budget 1.0e6). Selection rules: only ideas with a positive composite delta above their seed spread on some rung, or a clear gain on one goal with no goal drop; at most 3 hybrids; no control arm; nothing that reads data a rung does not read today. Each combination states the result that would count against it. The chunk runs no job. The foreman reads `MIXMATCH.md`, may cut combinations, and logs the set in the notepad.
Depends on: gate G3
Compute: 0. Wall: 3 h.
Test: `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:MX>/MIXMATCH.md && awk -F'\t' 'NR>1' cruxvault/results/<T:MX>/combinations.tsv | wc -l` prints a number from 1 to 6.

### MXh Mix-and-match: build the combinations (one builder per hybrid, in parallel)   wave 15
Goal: Write the config of every combination, and the module of every hybrid.
Owns: per hybrid n (its own builder): `tools/minicandi/rungs/mix<n>.py`, `tools/minicandi/configs/mix/mix<n>.json`, `tests/test_minicandi_mix<n>.py`. One more builder owns the configs of all `config` combinations: `tools/minicandi/configs/mix/mix<n>.json` for those n, and `tests/test_minicandi_mix_configs.py`. The file sets are disjoint by n.
Interfaces: a hybrid module follows the rung contract (`class Model(MiniCandi)`, `forward(batch) -> {"n", "mu"}`, built from the shared components, never calling `validate_batch`), is loaded through `config["model_file"] = "tools/minicandi/rungs/mix<n>.py"` (chunk A1), and its config holds `rung: "mix<n>"`, `windows_seen = 80000` and the other keys of the selected configuration. Tests on `synth.py` tables: the parameter count is within 10% of 1.0e6; `contract.wiring_check` passes with a perturbed head; the loss falls over 30 steps; `load_run` round-trips. A combination that cannot be built as specified is returned as `blocked` with the reason; the others proceed.
Depends on: MXp, A1
Compute: 0 (laptop). Wall: 3 h per builder.
Test: `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_minicandi_mix<n>.py -q` (hybrid builders) or `... tests/test_minicandi_mix_configs.py -q` (config builder) prints 0 failed.

### MXa Mix-and-match: train and score (submit)   wave 16
Goal: Train each combination with 3 seeds at the ladder budget and score it on validation.
Owns: `results/<T:MX>/SUBMITTED.md`; Nibi `$R/mix<n>/`.
Interfaces: `submit_arms.sh <kit> <tables eic_v1> <evalset> <runs root> <k> mix<n>=tools/minicandi/configs/mix/mix<n>.json ...` with literal paths, `DEVICE` set to the device type of the selected configuration's scores (so every comparison of MXb is on one device type; a hybrid on the axial trunk may need `SCORE_TIME=1-00:00:00`). 80,000 windows, seeds 0, 1, 2. Health check as L1a on each combination's seed 0, plus the measured FLOPs per window beside the selected configuration's (reported; the comparison is at equal windows and equal parameters).
Depends on: MXh
Compute: at most 6 combinations x 3 seeds = 18 runs. On a small trunk: 18 x (6.5 + 6.6) = 236 CPU-h. On an axial trunk: 18 x (31.7 + 32) = 1,147 CPU-h. Wall: 1 day.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix mix` prints 3 rows per combination of `combinations.tsv` that was built.

### MXb Mix-and-match: settle and name the winning recipes   wave 17
Goal: Apply D13 to each combination and name the 1 or 2 recipes that go to the evolution step.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder/mix.json`, `tools/minicandi/configs/recipes/recipe1.json` (and `recipe2.json`), `results/<T:MX>/` (`MIXMATCH_RESULTS.md`, `scores.tsv`, `dev_evals.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$R/settlements/mix/settlements.tsv`.
Interfaces: `select_rule.py compare --incumbent $R/<selected configuration> --challenger $R/mix<n> --simpler <incumbent, or challenger where combinations.tsv says the combination is simpler> --name mix<n> --settlements $R/settlements/mix/settlements.tsv`; `ladder-add --d14 false` into `ladder/mix.json`. A combination that fails the wiring check is out. Recipe 1 is the best design so far: the combination with verdict `CHALLENGER` and the highest composite (two such combinations are compared with each other, `--simpler none`), or the selected configuration when no combination wins. Recipe 2 is the runner-up when it is a different trunk and its composite is within 2x the seed spread of recipe 1's; else there is one recipe. A tie that goes to the PI: the incumbent is used until the answer, and the foreman logs it. The slip carries one notepad entry and one finding text per combination.
Depends on: MXa, RP
Compute: about 2 CPU-h. Wall: 3 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh MX && bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh MX tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix mix` shows every combination `done`, and `test -s tools/minicandi/configs/recipes/recipe1.json -a -s cruxvault/results/<T:MX>/MIXMATCH_RESULTS.md`.

### EVa Evolution of the winning recipes: set up and start the autopilot runs   wave 18
Goal: Set up one `crux auto` search per winning recipe with a cheap proxy objective on validation, check it, and start it (the PI authorized starting these runs, ruling 23).
Owns: `tools/minicandi/evolve/recipe<i>/config.json` and `tools/minicandi/evolve/recipe<i>/model.py` (the editable copy of the recipe's config and of its rung or hybrid module), `cruxvault/auto/<qid>_recipe<i>/plan.md`, `results/<T:EV>/` (`EVOLVE_SETUP.md`, `proxy_noise.tsv`); Nibi `$O/eval/eic_v1/proxy/evalset.json`, `$R/evolve/`.
Interfaces: load the `crux-autopilot` skill (`~/.claude/skills/crux-autopilot/SKILL.md`) for the plan format; each run is started by a dispatched subagent that follows that skill, except its last step: here the run is started. Plan file, frontmatter then guidance:
```
---
anchor: <qid of the question "which miniCANDI design maximizes the D13 composite", opened by the foreman>
repo: ..
base: <the umbrella commit that holds recipe<i>>
editable: tools/minicandi/evolve/recipe<i>/config.json, tools/minicandi/evolve/recipe<i>/model.py
scorer: python3 tools/onion_eval/auto_proxy.py --recipe tools/minicandi/evolve/recipe<i>
objective: composite max <the recipe's own proxy composite, measured by the check>
mode: climb
budget_hours: 48
attempt_minutes: 180
worker: opus high
min_gain: <2 x the proxy noise that EVa measured>
---
<guidance: the PI's words of ruling 23, quoted; then, marked as harness facts: what the scorer measures, its time, the frozen files, and the lessons of what_worked.tsv>
```
The guidance text is the PI's ruling quoted as it stands in the Log; the chunk adds only harness facts on their own marked lines. Frozen by construction (not in `editable`; a change outside it discards the attempt): `tools/onion_eval/score.py`, `tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py`, `tools/onion_eval/auto_proxy.py`, `tools/onion_eval/guardrail.py`, the dataset, the contract, the eval set. Proxy budget (chunk EVt's scorer): 20,000 training windows, 2 seeds, scored on the proxy eval set (`evalset.py build --tables $T1 --n-regions 32 --n-fit-regions 16 --seed 1 --out $O/eval/eic_v1/proxy/evalset.json`: validation targets only, regions drawn with another seed than the selection eval set), composite = the mean over the 2 seeds of the D13 composite that `select_rule` computes from the clipped terms. The scorer refuses (exit non-zero, `"passed": false`) a candidate whose wiring check fails, whose parameter count is outside 10% of 1.0e6, whose config holds `allow_blind`, `train_on_validation`, `final_model` or a tables path other than `eic_v1`, or whose module opens a file itself. Blind and testbed rows are unreachable: the scorer runs `mode = val`, and the dataset refuses `in_eic = blind` rows. Cost per attempt on a small trunk: 2 x (20,000 x 0.0244 s x 12 CPUs = 1.6 CPU-h + about 0.8 CPU-h of scoring) = about 5 CPU-h; on an axial trunk 2 x (7.9 + 4) = about 24 CPU-h. Caps: 48 h of wall per recipe (`budget_hours`), and 3,000 CPU-h over all recipes (1,500 each with two recipes): the scorer keeps a tally in `$R/evolve/TALLY.tsv` and refuses new attempts beyond the cap. Steps: (1) measure the proxy noise: the recipe scored 3 times with different seed pairs (`proxy_noise.tsv`); `min_gain` = 2x the max-minus-min. (2) `crux auto check cruxvault/auto/<qid>_recipe<i>/plan.md`; fix what it reports. (3) A dispatched subagent runs `crux auto run cruxvault/auto/<qid>_recipe<i>/plan.md` (one per recipe; in the background). The chunk ends when the first attempt of each run has a score in `crux auto status <qid> --json`.
Depends on: MXb, EVt, A1
Compute: noise measurement 2 recipes x 3 x 5 = 30 CPU-h (small trunk); the runs: at most 3,000 CPU-h. Wall: 4 h to start; the runs up to 48 h.
Test: `(cd cruxvault && /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python ~/.claude/skills/crux/scaffold/crux.py auto check auto/<qid>_recipe1/plan.md --static)` exits 0, and `(cd cruxvault && /Users/mforooz/miniforge3/envs/candii/bin/python ~/.claude/skills/crux/scaffold/crux.py auto status <qid> --json)` shows `baseline`, `noise` and at least one attempt.

### EVb Evolution: collect the runs and submit the confirmations   wave 19
Goal: Read the finished autopilot runs, pick the candidates that the proxy prefers, and train each at the full budget with 3 seeds.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/evolve/evo<j>.json` and `tools/minicandi/rungs/evo<j>.py` (copies of the picked attempts' config and module), `results/<T:EV>/` (`EVOLVE_RUNS.md`, `attempts.tsv`, `SUBMITTED.md`); Nibi `$R/evo<j>/`.
Interfaces: start when `crux auto status <qid> --json` shows `live` false for each run (budget, cap or stop). `attempts.tsv`: every attempt with its parent, proxy score, status and the diff size. Candidates: per recipe, the best kept attempt, and at most 2 more kept attempts that beat the recipe by more than `min_gain` and differ from the best in kind (not a descendant of it); at most 3 per recipe, 6 in all. With no attempt above `min_gain`, there is no candidate, and the recipe stands. Each candidate is copied out of its attempt commit into the two owned files (config with `model_file = tools/minicandi/rungs/evo<j>.py`, `windows_seen = 80000`), checked on the laptop (`contract.wiring_check` on `synth.py` tables, parameter count), and submitted with `submit_arms.sh ... evo<j>=<json>`, 3 seeds, on the recipe's scoring device type. The proxy never settles anything: only the confirmation under D13 can change the design.
Depends on: EVa (runs finished)
Compute: at most 6 candidates x 3 seeds = 18 runs: 236 CPU-h on a small trunk, 1,147 on an axial trunk. Wall: 1 day.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix evo` prints 3 rows per candidate named in `SUBMITTED.md` (or `EVOLVE_RUNS.md` states that no attempt beat the recipe by more than `min_gain`), and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:EV>/attempts.tsv`.

### EVc Evolution: confirm under D13 and name the design   wave 20
Goal: Apply D13 to each confirmed candidate at the full budget and name the design that stage 2 runs on.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/design.json`, `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder/evolve.json`, `results/<T:EV>/` (`EVOLVE_RESULTS.md`, `scores.tsv`, `proxy_against_full.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$R/settlements/evolve/settlements.tsv`, `$R/design` (symbolic links `seed<s>` to the chosen run directories).
Interfaces: `select_rule.py compare --incumbent $R/<recipe 1's run directory> --challenger $R/evo<j> --simpler incumbent --name evo<j> --settlements $R/settlements/evolve/settlements.tsv` (the evolved candidate is never the simpler side); candidates of recipe 2 are compared with recipe 2 and then, if they win, with the best of recipe 1's side. A candidate that fails the wiring check is out (the hard gate). The design is the winner with the highest composite among `CHALLENGER` verdicts, else recipe 1. `proxy_against_full.tsv` puts each candidate's proxy gain beside its full-budget gain (the report says how well the proxy predicted). `ladder-add --d14 false` into `ladder/evolve.json`. A tie for the PI: recipe 1 is used until the answer. The slip carries notepad entries and finding texts.
Depends on: EVb, RP
Compute: about 2 CPU-h. Wall: 3 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh EV && bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh EV tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix design` prints 3 rows `done`, and `test -s tools/minicandi/configs/design.json`.

**Gate G3b (after EVc).** GOOD NEWS line: **the miniCANDI design is chosen from the ladder, the mix-and-match combinations and the evolution step**, with its composite, the three goal terms and the seed spread. The foreman runs `compute_tally.py milestone --name G3b` and starts stage 2.

### S2a Stage 2 on the design (submit)   wave 21
Goal: Train and score the 31 stage-2 arms on the design (`design.json`), 3 seeds each.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/stage2/` (`<arm>.json` for each applicable arm, `arms.tsv`), `results/<T:S2>/SUBMITTED.md`; Nibi `$R/s2_<arm>/`, `$O/eval/eic_v1/borzoi_train/`, `$O/eval/eic_v1/borzoi_test/`.
Interfaces: `stage2_arms.py write --incumbent tools/minicandi/configs/design.json --out tools/minicandi/configs/stage2`. Inputs by arm: `average_input` reads `/scratch/mforooz/onion/aux/eic_v1_average.h5` (E7a); `dna_flashzoi` and `dna_gnlm` read `/scratch/mforooz/onion/dna_emb/<source>` (A5c); `qc_on_input` reads `$O/tables/eic_v1_qc.tsv` (QCb). An arm whose input is missing is not submitted and is listed with the reason. Submit with literal paths: `submit_arms.sh <kit> <tables> <evalset> <runs root> <k> s2_<arm>=tools/minicandi/configs/stage2/<arm>.json ...` (run directory `$R/s2_<arm>/seed<s>`; `DEVICE` = the device type of the design's scores). All arms train 80,000 windows (equal windows; `two_step` has about twice the FLOPs per window, which the settle chunk states). Leakage square for the two pretrained DNA arms: `evalset.py build ... --regions-bed <Borzoi training loci> --out $O/eval/eic_v1/borzoi_train/evalset.json` and the same with the test loci into `borzoi_test/`, then extra scoring jobs of the design, `dna_flashzoi` and `dna_gnlm` (3 seeds each) on both, into `<run dir>/leak_train/` and `<run dir>/leak_test/`. It ends at submission plus the 30-minute check on the first run of each group.
Depends on: gate G3b, S2K, QCb, A5c, E7b, AS
Compute: 31 arms x 3 seeds = 93 runs, cost by the design's trunk. Small trunk: training 93 x 6.5 = 604.5 CPU-h, plus 6 two-step runs x 6.5 more = 39; scoring 93 x 6.6 = 614 (planning; 93 x 0.83 = 77 at the pilot cost); leakage square 18 x 6.6 = 119; total about 1,380 CPU-h. Axial trunk: 93 x 31.7 + 6 x 31.7 = 3,138; scoring 93 x 32 = 2,976; leakage 18 x 32 = 576; about 6,700 CPU-h. Rung 1 trunk: (93 + 6) x 1.04 = 103 GPU-h of training and 111 x 5.3 = 588 GPU-h of scoring at the planning factor (111 x 0.66 = 73 at the pilot cost). Wall: 1 to 2 days.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix s2_` lists 3 seeds for every arm of `tools/minicandi/configs/stage2/arms.tsv` that has a json, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:S2>/SUBMITTED.md`.

### S2b Stage 2: collect and settle   wave 22
Goal: Apply D13 to each stage-2 comparison, hand back the settlements, and report the stage-2 tables.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder/stage2.json`, `results/<T:S2>/` (`STAGE2.md`, `scores.tsv`, `dev_evals.tsv`, `accessibility_only.tsv`, `leakage_square.tsv`, `predicted_control_route.tsv`, `winners.tsv`, `compute.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$R/settlements/stage2/settlements.tsv`.
Interfaces: `select_rule.py compare --incumbent $R/design --challenger $R/s2_<arm> --simpler <from arms.tsv> --name s2_<arm> --settlements $R/settlements/stage2/settlements.tsv` for every arm; `two_step_fixed` against `$R/s2_two_step_native`. The leakage controls `dna_shuffled` and `dna_shift5mb` pass `--simpler incumbent` (a control within the bars is `INCUMBENT`, never a tie for the PI). An arm that fails the wiring check is out. `ladder-add` into `ladder/stage2.json` with `--d14 true` for `qc_on_input`, `two_step_native`, `two_step_fixed` (D6 and D15 add comparisons to the ladder; `ladder-add` with `--d14 true` needs `rung<k>_<arm>` names, so these three comparisons are recorded with the incumbent name `rung<k>_design` and the challenger name `rung<k>_s2_<arm>`, where k is the trunk's rung, through symbolic links of those names to the run directories) and `--d14 false` for the rest. `winners.tsv`: the arms with verdict `CHALLENGER`, `CHALLENGER_AS_SIMPLER` or a `PI_` verdict for the challenger, with their key group (covariate vector, correction route, control, average, DNA source, covariate embedding and injection, input depth, count transform, loss and head, masking), excluding the two leakage controls. A comparison whose tie is with the PI counts for the incumbent until the answer (logged). A pretrained DNA arm counts as a winner only if its gain at the Borzoi test loci is at least half its gain at the training loci (`leakage_square.tsv`; else its gain is reported as possible leakage; agent call, logged). `accessibility_only.tsv`: the D11b second table for the design and the three baselines on validation. `predicted_control_route.tsv`: the design scored once more with `--lambda-source predicted_control` into `<run dir>/lambda_pred/`, against the observed-control signal on the same targets. D10: the `average_input` verdict is handed back; its EIC-board test is in XCb. The slip carries one notepad entry and one finding text per comparison.
Depends on: S2a, RP
Compute: 3 extra scoring runs for the predicted-control route: 3 x 6.6 = 20 CPU-h (small trunk). Wall: 4 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh S2 && bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh S2 tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix s2_` shows every submitted arm `done`, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:S2>/winners.tsv -a -s tools/minicandi/configs/ladder/stage2.json`.

### FDa Final design: the design plus the stage-2 winners (submit)   wave 23
Goal: Write the final-design candidate and train it with 3 seeds on the training tracks.
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/final_candidate.json`, `results/<T:FD>/SUBMITTED.md`, `results/<T:FD>/assembly.md`; Nibi `$R/final_candidate/`.
Interfaces: assembly rule (PI ruling 23 and the foreman's conflict rule). From `winners.tsv`: the best winner per key group (the highest composite delta in its group); exclusive pairs are resolved before combining: `qc_on_input` with a `two_step` arm (keep the one with the higher composite delta), and `control_none` never enters a design (ruling 14: the EIC model keeps a control output). Flashzoi rule (foreman call, open for the PI): a design that contains `dna_flashzoi` cannot be the EIC-comparison entry until the PI rules on `results/<T:FZ>/FLASHZOI_LEAKAGE.md`; without that ruling, `dna_flashzoi` is left out of the candidate, its stage-2 verdict stays in the report as an ablation, and `assembly.md` says so. With no winner there is no run: the final design is the design. With one winner the candidate is that arm's config and its existing runs are the candidate's runs (symbolic links `$R/final_candidate/seed<s>`). With two or more, `final_candidate.json` = `design.json` plus each kept winner's change, trained with 3 seeds: `submit_arms.sh ... <k> final_candidate=tools/minicandi/configs/final_candidate.json`. `predict_control` stays true in every case. A winner whose comparison is still a tie with the PI is not in the candidate.
Depends on: S2b
Compute: at most 3 runs: 3 x (6.5 + 6.6) = 39 CPU-h on a small trunk; 3 x (31.7 + 32) = 191 on an axial trunk; 3 x (1.04 + 5.3) = 19 GPU-h on rung 1. Wall: half a day.
Test: `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:FD>/assembly.md`, and `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py table --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --prefix final` prints 3 rows for `final_candidate` (or `assembly.md` states that the design has no stage-2 winner).

### FDb Final design: settle, freeze, and the done rule on validation   wave 24
Goal: Settle the candidate, freeze the final design in writing before any blind track is read, and apply D14 on validation. A validation failure does not stop the blind pass (PI ruling 24).
Owns: `tools/minicandi/configs/final.json`, `tools/minicandi/configs/final_tv.json`, `tools/minicandi/configs/final_tv_avg.json`, `tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json` (the mix, evolve, stage-2 and final comparisons merged in), `results/<T:FD>/` (`FINAL_DESIGN.md`, `FREEZE.md`, `done_rule_validation.txt`, `baselines_dir.txt`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$R/settlements/final/settlements.tsv`, `$R/final` (symbolic links `seed<s>`).
Interfaces: (1) `final_candidate` against `design` (`--simpler incumbent`, `--settlements $R/settlements/final/settlements.tsv`): the candidate is `final` only with verdict `CHALLENGER`; else the best single stage-2 winner is, if it had its own `CHALLENGER` verdict; else the design is. `$R/final/seed{0,1,2}` link to the chosen runs; `final.json` is their config. (2) One device type: `baselines_dir.txt` holds the baselines directory scored on the same device type as `final` (`$O/eval/eic_v1/baselines`, or `$O/eval/eic_v1/baselines_gpu` after the chunk scored the four baselines again on the GPU slice when `final` was scored on the GPU). (3) `select_rule.py check-done --runs $R --ladder tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json --final $R/final --baselines $(cat baselines_dir.txt)` prints `D14 condition 1` and the validation state of condition 2 with the three per-goal margins against the strongest training-free baseline (raw terms; a margin must exceed 2x the final design's seed spread of that goal). What each state means here: `PASS` or `FAIL` on validation: recorded in `done_rule_validation.txt`, and the chunk goes on in both cases (ruling 24: the blind tracks are still scored once). `ESCALATE` (a lead metric is undefined or a denominator is under its tolerance): the foreman takes the named term to the PI, and the freeze waits for the answer. `REFUSED` (scores not comparable, or a zero seed spread): a defect; the chunk names the mismatched hash or device, the foreman opens a fix chunk that scores the affected runs again on one eval set and device, and no score file is edited. `MISSING`: the named scoring job is resubmitted once. `D14 condition 1: FAIL`: the chunk lists the unsettled comparisons; each needs its settlement or the PI's verdict entered with `ladder-add --verdict PI_... --notepad-ref ...` before the freeze. (4) `FREEZE.md` is always written (pass or fail): the final config's sha256, the git sha, the budgets, the validation result, and the complete list of runs and jobs that may read blind data, each exactly once: `final` seeds 0 to 2 (training tracks only; the D14 blind pass and the training-only score of ruling 21), `final_tv` seeds 0 to 2 (`final.json` with `train_on_validation = true` and `final_model = true`), `final_tv_avg` seeds 0 to 2 (the same plus `average_input`, for the D10 expectation; left out if the final design already has the average input), the four baselines, the 25 published entries, and the data-only analyses of E8c. The board entry of each model is seed 0, declared here before any blind read, with the 3-seed spread beside it and no average of seeds (PI ruling 25). No design, budget or code change is allowed after `FREEZE.md` is committed.
Depends on: FDa
Compute: at most 4 second scorings of baselines on the GPU (4 x 5.3 = 21 GPU-h, only if `final` is GPU-scored); about 2 CPU-h. Wall: 3 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/push_kit.sh FD && bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh FD tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py check-done --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --ladder tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json --final /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs/final --baselines "$(cat cruxvault/results/<T:FD>/baselines_dir.txt)"` prints `D14 condition 1: PASS` and a `D14 condition 2:` line that is `PENDING` (validation passed, blind missing) or `FAIL` (validation failed; the blind pass still runs); and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:FD>/FREEZE.md`.

**Gate G4 (after FDb).** With a committed `FREEZE.md` (after a validation pass or a validation failure, ruling 24), `eic_v2` checked (D6g) and both blind eval sets built (E8a), the foreman starts FEa. GOOD NEWS line: **the final miniCANDI design is frozen**, with the validation result of the done rule and the three margins.

### FEa The EIC model: train on training plus validation tracks (submit)   wave 25
Goal: Train the frozen final design on the training and validation tracks (ruling 21), and the average-as-input variant for the D10 test.
Owns: `results/<T:FE>/SUBMITTED.md`; Nibi `$R/final_tv/`, `$R/final_tv_avg/`.
Interfaces: `submit_arms.sh <kit> <tables eic_v1> "" <runs root> <k> final_tv=tools/minicandi/configs/final_tv.json final_tv_avg=tools/minicandi/configs/final_tv_avg.json` with literal paths and an empty eval-set argument (a model that trained on the validation tracks has no validation score). Run directories `$R/final_tv/seed<s>` and `$R/final_tv_avg/seed<s>`. Same windows, learning rate and seeds as `final`. After training, the wiring check on each run: `guardrail.py --run <dir> --tables $T1 --split train`. A run that fails the wiring check gets no blind pass; if seed 0 of `final_tv` fails it, the chunk stops and the foreman calls the planner (the design is frozen, so no fix is allowed without a ruling). The training-only model is `final` itself. Health check as L1a on seed 0.
Depends on: gate G4, A2
Compute: 6 runs x 6.5 = 39 CPU-h on a small trunk (190 on an axial trunk; 6.2 GPU-h on rung 1). Wall: half a day.
Test: `hpc run nibi 'ls /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs/final_tv/seed0 /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs/final_tv/seed1 /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs/final_tv/seed2 | grep -c DONE'` prints 3, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:FE>/SUBMITTED.md`.

### BPa The single blind pass (submit)   wave 26
Goal: Submit, once, every job that reads a blind track: the onion scores on the blind eval sets, the genome-wide predictions of the 51 blind tracks, the challenge scoring, and the signal-space scoring of the published entries. It runs after a validation pass and after a validation failure alike (ruling 24).
Owns: `results/<T:BP>/` (`SUBMITTED.md`, `BLIND_LEDGER.md`); Nibi `<run dir>/blind/`, `$O/eval/eic_v2/baselines/`, `/scratch/mforooz/onion/pred/`, `$O/eic/board/`.
Interfaces: the list is `FREEZE.md`; a job that is not on it is not submitted. Before submitting: `evalset.py check --allow-blind` on both blind eval sets, each listing all 51 blind tracks (or the tracks that `DATASET_EIC_V2.md` reports missing). (1) Onion blind scores, each with `--time` from `score_time` and on the device type of `final`'s validation scores: `score.sh <kit> $O/eval/eic_v2/evalset_blind.json $R/final/seed<s>/blind run $R/final/seed<s> --split blind --allow-blind` for seeds 0 to 2; `score.sh <kit> $O/eval/eic_v2/evalset_blind_tv.json $R/final_tv/seed<s>/blind run $R/final_tv/seed<s> --split blind --allow-blind`, and the same eval set for `final_tv_avg`; the four baselines on `evalset_blind.json` into `$O/eval/eic_v2/baselines/<name>/seed0/blind` (`baselines_gpu` when `final` is GPU-scored). `score.py` appends a `started` row and then a `scored` row per pass to `$O/eval/eic_v2/BLIND_PASSES.tsv` and refuses a second pass of the same (run, eval set). (2) Genome-wide prediction, seed 0 of `final` (with `evalset_blind.json`), `final_tv` and `final_tv_avg` (with `evalset_blind_tv.json`): `predict.sh` arrays over the blind cells, `--split blind --allow-blind`, out `/scratch/mforooz/onion/pred/<arm>/seed0`; first one chr21 task per model to measure the cost, then the rest. `predict.py` writes its own ledger rows under the key `predict:<run dir>` (chunk PG), so they never collide with a score row. (3) Challenge scoring, chained after (2): `eic_score.py score --pred /scratch/mforooz/onion/pred/<arm>/seed0/signal --truth $O/eic/truth/qnorm --entry miniCANDI_<arm> --out $O/eic/board/scores_<arm>.tsv` (the headline, D9), the same for `signal_plug_in` against `truth/qnorm` (ruling 20), and for `signal` against `truth/se_unnormalized` (secondary; the plug-in signal is not scored against the secondary truth: foreman call). (4) `external_signal.sh` for the 25 published entries on `evalset_blind.json`. `BLIND_LEDGER.md` lists every submitted job with its run, eval set or truth, and job id, and the statement that the list equals `FREEZE.md`. A blind job that crashes leaves a `started` row with no `scored` row, and `score.py` then refuses the retry: only the foreman reruns it, with `--repeat-blind-reason "<job id and the error text>"`, after checking that the crashed job wrote no `scores.json`, and logs it in the notepad and in `BLIND_LEDGER.md`. The chunk ends at submission plus a health check on the first job of each kind.
Depends on: FEa (runs finished), FDb, D6g, E8a, PG, XE
Compute: onion blind scores 13 x 6.6 = 86 CPU-h (planning). Prediction: not measured; estimate 300 CPU-h per model, 900 for three (the chr21 task replaces the estimate). Challenge scoring: the board reproduction's bound of 2,240 CPU-h for 1,274 tracks gives 1.76 CPU-h per track; 51 tracks x 3 models x 3 scorings (expectation and plug-in against the corrected truth, expectation against the secondary truth) = 459 track scorings = 808 CPU-h at most. Published entries in signal space: estimate 50 CPU-h. Total at most about 1,850 CPU-h. Storage on `/scratch` per model: signal 51 tracks x 123.5 M bins x 4 bytes x 2 modes = 50 GB, plus count files 51 x 123.5 M x 2 arrays x 2 bytes = 25 GB; 75 GB per model, 225 GB for three. Wall: 3 h to submit, jobs 1 day.
Test: `hpc run nibi 'grep -c started /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/eval/eic_v2/BLIND_PASSES.tsv'` prints a number above 0, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:BP>/BLIND_LEDGER.md`.

### BPb The done rule on the blind tracks   wave 27
Goal: Collect the blind onion scores, apply D14 condition 2 on the blind tracks, and state whether miniCANDI is done.
Owns: `results/<T:BP>/` (`DONE_RULE.md`, `done_rule.txt`, `blind_scores_final.tsv`, `blind_scores_final_tv.tsv`, `blind_accessibility_only.tsv`, `blind_by_covariate_distance.tsv`, `ledger_check.txt`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`).
Interfaces: required artefacts: `$R/final/seed{0,1,2}/blind/SCORED` and the four blind baselines. `select_rule.py check-done --runs $R --ladder tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json --final $R/final --baselines <baselines_dir.txt> --blind-baselines <the blind baselines directory of the same device type>` prints both D14 lines and, since chunk SR, the validation state and the blind state apart with their margins, also when validation failed (ruling 24). Condition 2 is `PASS` only when both splits pass; nothing is rerun to change it. A blind state of `ESCALATE`, `REFUSED` or `MISSING` is handled as in FDb, and a missing blind score is rerun only by the foreman under the crash rule of BPa. Ledger check (`ledger_check.txt`): for each (run, eval set) of `FREEZE.md`, `BLIND_PASSES.tsv` holds exactly one `started` row and one `scored` row, a `predict:<run dir>` pair for each predicted model, any repeat carries its reason, and no run outside the list appears. Two tables, one eval set each (`arm_report.py` refuses a mix of eval-set hashes): `blind_scores_final.tsv` = `final` and the baselines on `evalset_blind.json`; `blind_scores_final_tv.tsv` = `final_tv` and `final_tv_avg` on `evalset_blind_tv.json`; `DONE_RULE.md` puts the training-only score beside the training-plus-validation score (ruling 21) with the note that their inputs differ. `blind_accessibility_only.tsv`: the D11b second table on the blind tracks (`arm_report.py accessibility` on the blind scores of `final` and of the baselines). `blind_by_covariate_distance.tsv` splits the blind results by how far each blind track's depth, run type and read length lie from the training range of its assay, read from `$T2/measurements.tsv`. GOOD NEWS line: **miniCANDI meets the done rule**, or the report that it does not, with the validation and blind margins. The slip carries the notepad entry and the finding text.
Depends on: BPa (jobs finished), SR
Compute: about 2 CPU-h. Wall: 3 h.
Test: `bash tools/onion_kit/remote_check.sh main tools/onion_eval/select_rule.py check-done --runs /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs --ladder tools/minicandi/configs/ladder.json --final /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/runs/final --baselines "$(cat cruxvault/results/<T:FD>/baselines_dir.txt)"` prints `D14 condition 1: PASS`, a `D14 condition 2:` line that is `PASS` or `FAIL`, and a `blind:` line with a state and three margins; and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:BP>/DONE_RULE.md -a -s cruxvault/results/<T:BP>/ledger_check.txt`.

### XCb EIC board and the comparison with external entries in signal space   wave 27
Goal: Put miniCANDI beside the published EIC entries, with the challenge's own measures and with headline metric 1 in signal space, and test the PI's D10 expectation.
Owns: `results/<T:XC>/` (`EIC_COMPARISON.md`, `final_board.tsv`, `signal_space_entries.tsv`, `d10_expectation.md`, `lambda_sources.tsv`, `runall.sh`, `MANIFEST.tsv`); Nibi `$O/eic/board/final_board.tsv`.
Interfaces: required artefacts: `$O/eic/board/scores_<arm>.tsv` for the three models (expectation and plug-in against the corrected truth; expectation against the secondary truth); the 25 `external_signal` score directories. (1) `eic_score.py board --scores $O/eic/board/scores_final_tv.tsv ... --out $O/eic/board/final_board.tsv`: each miniCANDI row alone in the published field, against the corrected truth (the headline, D9), seed 0 as declared in `FREEZE.md` (ruling 25), with its lambda source per track (`lambda_sources.tsv`: `observed_control`, `predicted_control` for Caco-2 and SJSA1, `local_background` for ATAC-seq and DNase-seq); the un-normalized single-end truth beside it (secondary, expectation signal only); the plug-in signal beside the expectation signal on the corrected truth (ruling 20); `final` (training only) beside `final_tv`. (2) `external_signal.py table`: headline metric 1 and `r_spec` in `signal_arcsinh` for the 25 entries and for miniCANDI. (3) `d10_expectation.md`: the average-as-input model's board rank and score against the old blended CANDI row (read the row from the published-field file, never from a remembered number), with the statement that no miniCANDI output was blended with any predictor (D10). No external method is retrained (D16). Every number carries its seed spread where 3 seeds exist. The slip carries the notepad entry and the finding texts.
Depends on: BPa (jobs finished)
Compute: about 5 CPU-h. Wall: 4 h.
Test: `hpc run nibi 'head -3 /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/ONION/eic/board/final_board.tsv'` shows a miniCANDI row with a lambda-source column, and `test -s cruxvault/results/<T:XC>/EIC_COMPARISON.md -a -s cruxvault/results/<T:XC>/d10_expectation.md`.

**Risks, each with the cheap check that detects it.**
- Nibi `/scratch` is over its soft quota (1826 of 1024 GiB; hard limit 20480 GiB), so a grace clock may run and then block writes. Check: `quota -s` in K0, D6a and D6c; chunk GT's transfer and the foreman's deletion of `/scratch/mforooz/t112_cf` (1.6 TB) bring usage under the soft quota.
- Shared `/project` quotas move without us (Fir files rose 157K in a day; Nibi `/project` has 476 GiB and 193K files free). Check: `diskusage_report` before every write over 10,000 files or 100 GB; bulk data stays on `/scratch` and `/nearline`.
- Compute nodes have no internet. Check: the K0 probe; downloads then run on the node K0 named.
- The DNase pipeline setup fails or takes long (no image or reference bundle exists on either cluster). Check: D7d's smoke; the histone build proceeds without it; DNase is 37 of 312 non-blind experiments and one scorable assay.
- The alignment-only cost is far above or below the estimate. Check: `smoke_cost.tsv` in D6a before the bulk submission.
- Validation holds 3 tracks per scorable assay, so intervals and seed spread may be as large as the differences between arms. Check: `scorer_validity.md` (interval widths) and rung 1's `seed_spread.md` at G2b, before the other rungs run.
- The correction term is degenerate on depth-subset pairs. Check: `subset_pair` exclusion in the scorer; `scorer_validity.md` reports gap closed per knob; G2 needs a non-depth knob.
- Training samples a withheld combination or a validation track. Check: the dataset test (10,000 draws) and `check_dataset.py`.
- The blind covariates lie outside the training range (the D3 ledger check, Log 2026-11-11: blind tracks are 100% paired-end against 9.7% in training, 51 of 51 on one platform, 48 of 51 from one lab). Check: `eic_imbalance.tsv` in D3; designed arms can only shorten and thin, so the end chunks report blind results by covariate distance.
- A copy shortcut inflates denoising. Check: the two-part guardrail on every run and the planted-copy test.
- A metric instrument is broken. Check: known-answer and planted-error tests in E1, E2, E5, E6; the baseline order check in E7b; depth-only must score a gap closed of exactly 0 and the floor exactly 1.
- GPU fair-share runs out on one cluster. Check: the CPU against GPU-slice probe in P2a; `budgets.json` records the device.
- A kit runs another checkout's `candi`. Check: `slurm/_kit_pin.sh` in every job; the K0 test prints the import path.
- The EIC truth or scorer is not the exact one. Check: `eic_score.py verify-published` before any EIC number is quoted.
- An accession does not match the published list. Check: `eic_accessions.py check`; unresolved rows go to the PI before D6a submits them.
- Max's group works on the same questions (sprint 08 covariate correction, an `s09` harness on Fir, reference recipe R2). Check: the foreman re-reads `notes/project_state.md` in `mlibbrecht/01_2026-10-19_research` at each gate. The `notes_mirror` path in `CLAUDE.md` does not exist on Fir.
- A long job is resubmitted by mistake. Check: every submit chunk writes `SUBMITTED.md` with job ids; every job is skip-if-exists.

**Budget, amendment 2b (projections from the measured pilot and smoke costs; each chunk reports measured values).** All `eic_v1` and `eic_v2` work is on Nibi. Costs measured on Fir (pilot) are used for Nibi; chunk L1a times rungs 2 and 5 on Nibi CPUs, and the foreman scales this table by the ratio.

| 2 | 80,000 x 0.0244 s x 12 = 6.5 CPU-h | 92.5 CPU-h | 0.83 CPU-h (measured) | 6.6 CPU-h = 3.3 h on 2 CPUs | 8 h |
